# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here. Format: [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Versioning: [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/), tags `v0.x` until the first audited release.

## [Unreleased]

### Removed
- **Specializations: the "Advanced: set a raw row" card is removed** (fixes #1133; operator decision; the page no longer asks for an XP and a level to write as typed for one specialization, so an exact level cannot be set and XP cannot be lowered that way any more; not checked in the game). The card, its Set button and its question ("Set this raw row?") are gone, and so is its line in the Specializations help. Selecting a specialization and adding XP, Grant LV 100, Reset XP, the keystone buttons and the Advanced: specialization numbers card (drawn only when a number is missing or not the game's own) are unchanged. The save write route behind the card, `POST /api/player/specs`, stays in the program for now; nothing on the page calls it.
- **Landsraad Command Center: the Choose shown key is removed; All in the Filter menu's House list does the same choice** (refs #1116; display only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 320, 375 and 1280 px with the synthetic answers, not in the game). The key in the filter row (and its label with the number of shown Houses) is gone. Filter, Houses, All ticks every House that is shown and, when every shown House is ticked, clears the choice, exactly as before. The count line still says "All 25 shown" when no filter narrows the board. Clear in the action bar now hands the focus to the Filter key (it handed it to Choose shown).
- **Specializations: the Classic list is retired; the Command Center view is the only view** (refs #1105; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). The "View: Classic list | Command Center" switch, the Classic table (its Add XP, Grant max and Reset buttons, its totals and its keystone column) and the view saved in the browser (`spec.view`) are gone, and the Command Center view is the default. Every request the page sends is byte-identical: no Go handler, route or save field changed, and the recorded-request golden file `lcc-spec-requests.golden.json` is unchanged and played on the one view. The two Advanced cards that only the Classic list had (specialization numbers, with Assign and Set by hand; set a raw row) stay on the page under the view, with the same buttons and handlers. If the Command Center scripts do not load, the tab says so and asks for a reload. Display and navigation only otherwise.
- **Landsraad: the Classic list is retired; the Command Center board (Grid and List) is the only view** (refs #1028; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). The "View: Classic list | Command Center" switch, the Classic list's rows, filters, glance cards, bar and the view saved in the browser (`lsb.view`) are gone, and the board is the default. Every request the board sends is byte-identical and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged (no Go handler and no save field changed). The Classic list's quick Fill and Complete (or Reopen) buttons are now on every row of the board's List layout, the same controls as the House Intelligence panel's; Expand all and Collapse all have nothing to open on the board. If the Command Center scripts do not load, the tab says so and asks for a reload.

### Added
- **Move the Reserve into two named storage boxes: `tabr-tau move-reserve --boxes "Name A,Name B" [--dry-run]`, and a button on Player > Bases** (refs #1191; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; checked on copies of a real save, not in the game). The Reserve can only be filled by the game, so after a recycle it is a one-way inbox. The move puts every stack of your character's Reserve into the two Developer storage containers you named in the game, whole and unchanged (only the inventory and the slot of each row change), into box 1 until it is full, then box 2. Each box is found by the name you gave it; it must be the only box with that name, in a base of yours, not in the Deep Desert and not in a base being recycled, or the whole move is refused and says what it found. The room (slots and volume, with what is already in the boxes) is checked before anything changes; one slot or one unit of volume short is a refusal that changes nothing and says needed against free. One edit, read back inside itself, backed up first, fingerprint-guarded, queued while the game runs and dropped if the game changed what it read. An empty Reserve writes nothing. The command refuses while the game runs, takes a verified backup copy and reads the written file back. Walk-through in `docs/recycle-a-base.md`.
- **Notices: the module versions in the notices are checked against go.mod, every embedded data file must be named in the notices, and the stale wording is fixed** (refs #621, audit findings D11-2 to D11-5; docs, comments and tests only: no page, request, save field or code of the editor changed; nothing checked in the game). The notice test now matches each module's heading with the version go.mod requires, so a dependency bump without its notice fails CI; the headings of go-humanize (v1.1.0), go-strftime (v1.1.0), x/sys (v0.49.0), libc (v1.80.1) and sqlite (v1.62.0) are refreshed (the licence texts were compared with the module cache and are unchanged). A new test reads every `//go:embed` line of the tree and fails for a JSON, SQL or text file that the notices do not name; `internal/layoutroll/worldlayouts-line.txt` and `internal/ops/decree-names.json` are now pinned, and the layout line has its notice. The remaining hits of the role word in a workflow step name, a tool header and the continuation prompt say "owner" or "a person", and the font file of the design module is described as being in the tree.
- **Player > Bases: Recycle base can make a Solido of the base first** (refs #1187; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; checked on copies of a real save, not in the game). A box on the Bases card, off by default. Ticked, the same edit first writes the game's own Solido of the base (a blueprint row, its pieces, placeables and pentashields) into a blank Solido device of the character's, or into a new device in the backpack (in the loadout only when the backpack is full) when there is none, and then recycles the base as before; a filled Solido is never touched. The conversion was measured on a pair of saves from the game and is checked row by row against it; the game's numbering of the rows is not reproducible and is not copied. See `docs/recycle-a-base.md`.
- **Player > Bases: Recycle base deletes a base of yours and returns everything to your character's Reserve** (refs #1176, phase 1; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; checked on a copy of a real save, not in the game). A button on every standing base, next to the others, asks first (how many pieces and placeables are deleted, how many item stacks move, the refund item by item, holograms, Developer storage containers and water, that everything goes to the Reserve and that only restoring a backup of the save undoes it) and then writes one edit, after the usual backup, or queued while the game runs. Deleted: the base's totem, placeables and building actors with their entities and inventories, every building piece, the land claim, the fuel times, the permission records, the base's map marker and the respawn point at the base; no base backup is written. Moved: every item stored in the base (chests, the totem's slots, windtrap filters, generator fuel, refinery and fabricator slots) goes to the Reserve unchanged. Added: the refund, 100% of what each piece and placeable costs to build by the game's own building tables (a new embedded table of ids and whole numbers, `internal/catalog/buildcosts.json`, 752 piece types and 527 placeable types of game build 26592269), as new stacks; a base in the Deep Desert gives back half the table cost, because a piece costs half there. Fixed rules: a Developer storage container gives 10 Iron Ingot, a hologram gives nothing, a door counts once, water in cisterns is dropped, vehicles are never touched. Refused, with nothing changed: a base that is not your own, a base already picked up or not in the normal state, a running crafting queue, a base a character is waiting to respawn at, a base that a row of another table points at (named in the message), and a piece or placeable type the cost table does not know (named in the message, never treated as free). The write needs the fingerprint of the question and fails whole when what it reads back differs from the plan. The game's own recycle of a base backup returned about half of the cost in the one measured case (Stone 125 and Salvaged Metal 37 of a table cost of 242 and 75); returning everything is the operator's decision. Choosing storage boxes as the destination is a later phase. See `docs/recycle-a-base.md`.
- **`scripts\layout-roll-task.ps1` takes `-SavePath`: the weekly task names the save it checks** (refs #1171; the script and its help only, nothing in the program or the save changes; nothing is registered unless you run the script yourself). With more than one save on the computer the weekly roll refused every week because it could not tell which save to check, until the task action was edited by hand. `-SavePath` (the `game.db` file or its folder, which must exist; a relative path is made a full one) is written into the task action as `--save "<path>"` before the word `layout-roll`, and `-WhatIf` shows the whole line. Without it the action is unchanged. The help page for the layout roll says so.
- **`tabr-tau layout-roll` takes your vehicles out of the Deep Desert first: a Scout or a Sandbike goes into the game's own vehicle backup, every other vehicle is moved in front of the character** (refs #1174; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). After the verified checkpoint and before the roll, each vehicle you own that stands in the Deep Desert is stored or moved in ONE edit of the save that is read back inside the edit (any difference undoes the whole edit) and again from the written file. A Scout or a Sandbike gets the game's own two rows (the vehicle's state 2 and one `backup_vehicles` row, as seen in a real before and after save; the backup has one place for each character, so a second one, or one whose customization is not recorded, is moved instead and the line says why). Every other type is moved to the character's map the way Bring vehicle does it, with the same placement code: in front of the character's saved position and facing, ground vehicles 10 m ahead, aircraft 30 m ahead and 7 m up, several in a line at least 12 m apart (ground vehicles first, aircraft after them), clear of base pieces, claims and other vehicles; cargo stays with the vehicle. A run with no clear spot for a vehicle, a character in the Deep Desert or a vehicle whose map is not recorded refuses and changes nothing; so does a run that will refuse for a base. `--dry-run` lists each vehicle, what would be done and where, in words and coordinates. The guard no longer counts a stored vehicle as being in the Deep Desert (it is not in the world). The line placement now reaches as far out as the line is long (it stopped at 80 m before). The help page says to log out facing open ground with room in front (rock and terrain are not in the save) and gives every command in a form that can be pasted from any folder.
- **Player > Bases: Back up and remove takes a base of yours out of the world the way the game's own base backup does** (refs #1127, bases only; vehicles are not part of it and nothing is deleted; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; checked on copies of two real saves, not yet by placing the backup again in the game). A button on every standing base asks first (the base's name, how many pieces and placeables leave the world, that it becomes one base backup to place again with the Base Reconstruction Tool, that the claim and the base marker go, and that stored items stay where they are) and then writes one edit, after the usual backup, or queued while the game runs: the totem and the placeables that stand on the base go to state 5 in place; the building actor is made again under a new number with the pieces moved to it; one base backup and its links are added; the totem's permission rows and the base's marker are deleted. Only your own standing bases are accepted (a member base, another player's base, a base nobody owns and a base the game already picked up are refused). Items and inventories are never touched: in the game a chest of four stacks kept its stacks when the game picked the base up. Applied to copies of two real saves from before the game's own pick-up, the result matches the save the game wrote table by table. See `docs/back-up-and-remove-a-base.md`.
- **`tabr-tau layout-roll` now checks the Deep Desert in your save before it rolls: a verified checkpoint, a refusal while a base, the character or a vehicle of yours is there, and a base file of each such base** (refs #1167; the run only READS the save apart from one verified copy of it in the save's own backup folder; deleting the bases is NOT built; not checked in the game, tested on made-up saves). With the game closed, and after the weekly rule, the run (1) takes a checkpoint of the whole save (`Save.BackupCheckpoint`, the same verified copy as Back up now, tagged `-layoutroll`; the pruning of automatic backups never counts or removes it), (2) opens the save read-only and refuses the roll, changing nothing and writing the reason in `layout-roll.json` (`lastCheck`, `lastRun`) and on screen, when any of these is true: a standing base you own is in the Deep Desert, the character (controller, body or player state) is in the Deep Desert, or a vehicle you own, placed or stored, is in the Deep Desert; bases and vehicles of other owners, picked-up bases and things on other maps never count, and a character or vehicle whose map is not recorded counts as in the Deep Desert, (3) when bases stand there, exports each as a console base backup file into a dated folder `layout-roll-bases-<time>` beside `layout-roll.json`, reads each back with the editor's own reader and records the folder and file names; if an export fails the run stops and says so. `--set` and `--force` do not skip the check. `--dry-run` shows what each step would find and writes nothing (no checkpoint, no export, no state file). The save is the one named with `--save` (before `layout-roll`) or the only one found; with none or several the run refuses. New in Go: `Ops.DeepDesertHolds()` (read only). The Help page, the README and `scripts/layout-roll-task.ps1` help say so.
- **`tabr-tau layout-roll`: change the single-player Deep Desert layout at random, or to a chosen one, once a week if you like** (refs #1141; a write to the game's own `Game.ini`, NOT to the save, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game by this program: the setting itself was changed by hand in the game on 2026-10-27, the command is tested on temporary files). It changes the number in the `m_WorldLayoutsPerWorld` line (or adds the line), keeps every other line as it is, and the game must be restarted. A random roll picks 0 to 11 and never the layout that is set now; `--set N` names one (0 to 11); `--dry-run` writes nothing; a roll is skipped when one already happened since the last Tuesday 05:00 unless `--force`. Guards: refuses while a Dune process runs or the process list cannot be read; a timestamped backup beside `Game.ini` before every change (newest 10 kept); atomic replace and read-back; refuses a link, a folder, UTF-16 text, a file over 1 MiB, or a line it cannot edit exactly (the key twice, an ini operator, no number). The last roll is recorded in `%APPDATA%\tabr-tau\layout-roll.json`. `scripts/layout-roll-task.ps1` registers (or, with `-Remove`, removes) a weekly Tuesday 05:00 scheduled task for the current user; nothing is registered until you run it. New Help page: Changing the Deep Desert layout, with the warning that bases stand on different terrain on each layout. No window and no button: the command line is the way.
- **Database > Save backups: a Back up now button takes a backup of the save by hand** (fixes #1168; READ ONLY on the save: it reads the database file and writes only into the backup folder; not checked in the game). The button asks first (the question says the copy is what the game last wrote, so it works while the game is running, and that nothing in the save is changed), then copies the save file the way every automatic backup is copied: into the same `tabr-tau-backups` folder, with the time in the name and `-manual` before the extension, and checks the copy by its size and SHA-256 against the bytes that were read, and reads the save file once more after the copy; a copy of a file that changed under it is removed and refused. The result line names the backup, its size and the start of its checksum, and the list tags it **manual backup**. Restore treats it like any other backup. The automatic pruning only ever looks at the `-auto` ones, so a manual backup is never removed and never counted among the newest 60. New route `POST /api/save/backup-now` and `Save.BackupNow()` in Go, which the weekly run (#1167) can call as its checkpoint step (that caller is not built here). The whole-folder copy (autosave ring, SOLO, ClientPersistence) is not built; the backup is the database file, like the automatic ones.
- **Landsraad: the board shows your level in each House's specialization at once, without opening the Specializations tab first** (refs #1108, #646; READ ONLY: the Landsraad read gains a `specialization_levels` field, one whole number per line your save holds a track row for, from the same XP table as the Specializations tab; no request body, route or save write changed; nothing checked in the game). A save with no track rows still shows "Level —". After a raise that gives specialization XP the old level is dropped and the re-read answer brings the new one.
- **Landsraad Command Center: a summary line for the term, your level in each House's specialization, and a list whose Place and Next tier cells fit** (refs #440, #646, #1103; display only: no request, save field, count or write changed and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark, with the synthetic answers, not in the game). One line under the term counts the Houses at top tier, in progress and not started from the same House state the Show tabs filter by, and each count is a key that sets that Show tab; it is not drawn for a character in no faction and it wraps at 390 px without running over. The House panel's specialization line ends with "Level N" and the hover text of a card and of its emblem carries it; the levels are the ones the Specializations tab already read (no new request), so until that tab has been opened, and again after a write that gave specialization XP, the level is a dash with the reason in the hover, never a made-up number. In the list at 1280 px the Place cell reads "Deep Desert · B8" (the word cell stays in its label and hover) and the Next tier cell drops "(1,200 more)" from view (still in the hover and read out), so neither wraps to three lines and the Not collected column stays whole; no column was given a minimum width. The Help page of the board says the same.
- **The Saved map has the pictures of Arrakeen and Harko Village** (refs #758; display only: nothing is written, no request or save field changed; checked in a headless browser, not in the game). The two hubs, which the entry below added as plain outlines, now have a picture each (`arrakeen.png` 1920 x 1097, 1,996,045 bytes, and `harko-village.png` 2048 x 2048, 1,396,204 bytes, in `internal/web/static/maps/`), and the five Houses that stand there (Mutelli, Varota, Wallach in Arrakeen, Alexin and Spinette in Harko Village) are pinned on them. **Where the pictures come from:** they are the map tiles of the gaming.tools companion app, not Funcom's own render and not from the Dune Docker console like the two older map pictures; they are shipped on the project owner's decision, are unofficial and not affiliated with gaming.tools or Funcom, are not covered by the MIT licence or licensed by this project, and are removed on request. `docs/art-notice.md`, `internal/notices/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` and the art manifest (`mapArt`: source, size, hash of each) say so; `withoutPicture` is empty now. The frames are unchanged from the entry below (they are the verified ones of the pictures). New check: every map picture has the shape of its map space.
- **Landsraad: Unfill chosen and Complete and fill chosen** (fixes #932; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). Two new buttons next to Fill chosen, on the board and in the Classic list. **Unfill chosen** sets your own contribution to 0 on each chosen House (tiers back to 0 of 5) and never changes whether a House is taken; it takes back the waiting reward rows of the removed tiers by the one existing rule (only rows the editor's own record vouches for, never a collected reward, never a row in a gap), keeps the Solari, House Scrip, XP and faction progress the missions paid, always asks first and names what it takes back and what stays. **Complete and fill chosen** completes the chosen Houses for the chosen faction and fills your own contribution to the top tier in one write with one backup and one result line, through the same grant as a raise (stale-ground checks `was_paid`, `was_paid_rows`, `was_elsewhere`, the confirm step, the question for ten missions or more). Both are off, with the reason shown, while a faction other than your own is chosen. No new route: the page sends the existing apply request. New in the contract: the read of every House gains `unfill` (what removing every tier would do to the waiting rewards, from the same rule as the write) and the request gains the optional `was_taken` (the number the question named; a different number now is a conflict and nothing is changed). The request golden of the board gains six scenarios and nothing else in it changes. The action bar has five buttons; the phone bar has two rows of buttons and its scroll padding grew from 110 to 170 px.
- **The Saved map opens Arrakeen and Harko Village, and the Landsraad link of a House at a hub lands on its hub** (refs #758; display only: nothing is written, no request or save field changed; nothing checked in the game). The map buttons of the Saved map are Hagga Basin, The Deep Desert, Arrakeen and Harko Village. A hub has a frame (the world rectangle its map covers, the same formula as the other maps), the markers of the save on that map, the character when it stands there, and the House representatives the editor's table of fixed places puts there (Mutelli, Varota and Wallach in Arrakeen, Alexin and Spinette in Harko Village) as faded markers unless the save holds a marker for the House on that map. On the Landsraad board those five Houses now have a pin, a distance when the character is in the same hub, and the "Open in the Saved map" link, which opens the hub centred on the House. **No picture of either hub is included**: the only pictures known to fit the frames are a third party's map tiles, which are not cleared for this repository, so the Saved map draws the hub's frame as a plain outline and says so on the page. The pictures are not game artwork this project may ship until a cleared copy exists; `docs/art-notice.md` and the art manifest (`mapArt`) say so, and the tests keep the manifest, the embedded pictures, the notice and the map list in step. Travel (the Map box) still offers the two maps only: a hub is a map to look at, not a place the character is moved to. The Harko Village frame was fitted by eye, and the hub positions come from the same reference map as the frames, so no position on a hub is checked against a marker of a real save yet.
- **A Help tab: the explanations that used to stand on the Command Center are now pages you open when you want them** (#980; page and build only: no save write, no request body and no route that writes changed, and nothing is fetched from the network). The pages are Markdown files in `docs/help/`, one per feature (the Landsraad board, Fill, Complete and tiers, Term and Decrees, Specializations) plus a table of what is checked in the game; they are embedded in the program (`go:embed`, answered by `GET /api/help`), so they work offline and always match the running build, and GitHub shows the same files. The tab draws them as text only (a small Markdown reader with no raw HTML and no new dependency; a link to another Help page opens it inside the editor, any other link is only its words) and carries an optional "Also on GitHub" link. The design note is `docs/design/2026-11-18-help-tab.md`, with every removed text and where its content went.
- **Command Center: the Term and Decrees section moves up under the plaque, the Decree preview strip is gone, the Decree pool and Earlier terms are closed behind a Show control, and the term's start and end read in days, hours and minutes** (#923, #922, #930; display and placement only: every control of the section sends the same request, asks the same question and says the same words as before, and the request goldens are unchanged; the one Go change is a read-only field; requested by the operator on 2026-11-18, to be tested; not checked in the game). The section is drawn once, directly under the plaque on the Command Center board, where the strip stood; the strip (the sample decree box, its link and its "sample only" note) and the plaque's sample mode and demo badge are removed, so the plaque always shows the term's own active decree. The Decree pool and the Earlier terms stay in the section, closed until Show is pressed. The save holds the term's start and end as play-clock seconds only, and no calendar date can be worked out for them (the play clock stops while the game is closed and the save does not say when), so the section shows how far each is from the point where the save was written, in days, hours and minutes of play ("5 d 20 h 13 min of play after the save"), the length of the term, and the real time of that save in your local time; the read gained one field, `clock` (the play clock at the save and the save's real time). The line "Your character is in <faction>." is removed from the board header (#930); the classic list is unchanged. See `docs/design/landsraad-command-center/DECISIONS.md`, G.
- **Command Center final verification, milestone M7** (refs #768; documents and tests only: no page, route, request body or Go code changed; nothing checked in the game, on a real save or on a phone): the acceptance note `docs/design/landsraad-command-center/10-m7-final-verification.md` (what was verified and how, what was not, open issues by priority, the checks left for the operator, rollout and rollback), the requirements matrix with a status and evidence for every row, a test that fixes the list of routes that write and ties each to an equivalence test, a seeded independent reading of the twelve lines against the evaluator and against the cards on the page, a text-floor check over all four Command Center style files, checks of line ends and public text, and a headless browser sweep (`scripts/lcc-m7-sweep.mjs`) of the board, Strategy, Term and Decrees and Specializations pages at 320 to 1600 px, dark and Dayside, 200 and 400 percent zoom, forced colours and reduced motion. Two defects were found and filed (#906, #907).
- **Command Center Term and Decrees and Sysselraad Strategy, milestone M6** (refs #768; the page changes and no save write logic does: every term control sends the request, says the words and returns the focus that the classic panel does, proved by a recorded-request test that plays fourteen scenarios in both views; no route, request body or Go code changed; not checked in the game): in the Command Center board view, a Term and Decrees section replaces the classic term panel and earlier terms (the classic list keeps them): the term, its start and end in play-clock seconds, the reigning faction, the active and elected decree with their pictures, Apply, one row per decree with Make active and Set flag (the term states and the save's pool flag shown apart), the hidden plain SpecialVendorActive row behind a warning, and the earlier terms read-only. Sysselraad Strategy is a switch on the board: the twelve lines with a plain sentence each (a line that is not held is a preview, never a result), a chosen line marked on the board, a jump to the House it misses, and an order switch. The House Inspector opens the House's path in Specializations. What the save does not hold (active missions, top contributors) says so; the sample decree picker is labelled and marks the plaque DEMO. The difference matrix is in `docs/design/landsraad-command-center/09-m6-decree-term-integration.md`.
- **Command Center: the twelve lines and the winning treatment, milestone M4** (refs #768; display and evaluation only: nothing is written, no request, field or Go code changed; not checked in the game). A pure function now reads the 25 Houses in board order and evaluates the five rows, five columns and two diagonals: which lines are held (five Houses of one faction), blocked, one House away or still open, how many lines are still possible for each faction, the House counts and whether a faction holds 13 or more. An unreadable House is never counted as open, so the line and the count are shown as incomplete and no number is made up. The POTENTIAL LINES tile shows the real count. The five Houses of a held line get the winning outline at full glow (the 0.5 dial still applies to the rest of the page) and the words LINE OF FIVE, with a moving border only under Motion Full and no reduced-motion request; calm, off, reduced motion and forced colours show a still outline, and forced colours use a border and an outline that do not depend on colour. A faction with 13 or more Houses gets a second outline marked MAJORITY HOUSE, COUNT NOT VERIFIED. The Imperial Victory Analysis panel says "five in a line" or "13 Houses held" and never says a faction wins: the save holds no record of the start of the Voting Session, so a House-count lead is shown only as a comparison marked not verified. Both factions holding a line is shown as a conflict, not a result. A folded list gives the twelve lines with one sentence each. Evaluator tests cover all twelve lines, mixed and open boards, the 12 and 13 boundary, unreadable Houses and the 40 board cases of the fixture set; board, wording and style tests cover the tile, the outlines, the panel states and the reduced-motion and forced-colours rules.
- **Command Center Specializations view, milestone M5** (refs #768; the page changes and no save write logic does: every button sends the request, asks the question and says the words that the classic list does, proved by a recorded-request test that plays sixteen scenarios in both views; one added handler line checks the typed XP amount before calling the existing one; not checked in the game): a second view of the Specialization tab, chosen with a switch above it and remembered, with the classic list still the default. It draws a hero with three totals, five path lanes with their scroll icons, a progression dossier for the chosen path (level from the game's XP table, XP bar, keystone bar, a level scale, three fact tiles), the editor's own operations beside it (add or remove XP, Grant LV 100, Reset Track XP, and the three all-track operations in a labelled group), the Houses of the path (choosing one opens it on the Landsraad board; nothing is written) and the retained advanced controls, which open by themselves when a track cannot be tied to a path. Only values of the save are shown: a track whose number is unknown shows no XP or level and its XP controls are off with the reason, a path with no keystone count says "Not in the save", a stored level that disagrees with its XP is reported and left alone, and a Landsraad that cannot be read leaves the Houses "Not in the save". Dark and Dayside, forced colors, reduced motion, no text under 11 px, reflow from 1600 to 320 px. The difference matrix is in `docs/design/landsraad-command-center/08-m5-specializations.md`.
- **Command Center board for the Landsraad tab, milestone M3** (refs #768; a SAVE WRITE PATH because its buttons send the existing Landsraad write, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game): a second view, chosen with a switch above the tab and remembered, with a fixed five-by-five board of the 25 Houses numbered 01 to 25, a list layout (sort, group by region, Saved map link), a House Inspector (two progress scales, the reward ledger, the grant before the click) and the same actions, bulk actions, stale guard, write queue and result line as the classic list, which stays the default. No new route, field or query. A single click on the board that would replace another faction's win asks first. House numbers 01 to 25 also show on the rows of the classic list. The board shows a count, never a won vote; contributors and missions say "Not in the save"; a board that fails its checks is shown read-only with the reasons. New style tokens `--lcc-glow-win` and `--lcc-outline-win` and an optional animated border (Motion Full only) for the Houses of a faction that holds more than half of the Houses; the five-in-a-line lines come with M4. Fidelity pass against the approved design: each card now has the specialization badge, five numbered reward boxes (collected, waiting, no delivery record, not reached) with the N/5 REACHED line, the approved fonts and look; above the board are a read-only decree preview ("Sample decree only, not written to a save") and the term row; beside it the Imperial Victory Analysis; five small badge pictures were added to the embedded art (game artwork, listed in the manifest). The matrix of every element is in docs/design/landsraad-command-center/06-m3-fidelity-matrix.md. No write behaviour changed.
- **A version stamp, the version in the header and an About panel** (#806, slice 1 of the release and update note; page and build only: no save write, no change to what any request writes, and no network code, so nothing leaves the machine). The editor now knows which build it is. `scripts/buildinfo.ps1` works the version out of git and prints the linker flags that stamp it into the program: the version is the nearest `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` tag (with `+N` after it for N commits since the tag, and `.dirty` for a tree with local changes; `0.0.0+<build>` while no tag exists), the build number is the count of commits that lead to the built commit, the commit is its first 7 characters and the date is the commit's own date, so a rebuild of one commit says the same. A plain `go build` is not stamped and reads `dev`. `tabr-tau --version` prints the one line (`tabr-tau v0.14.2 (build 317, a1b2c3d)`); the base files the editor writes now name the real version instead of `dev` for a stamped build. The header has a version button after the title (`v0.14.2`, `v0.14.2+5` for a development build, `dev` for an unstamped one) that opens an **About Tabr Tau** panel with the version, build, commit, date, channel and platform, and, when a `notes.md` sits in the editor's own folder, its text as plain text (read only, up to 64 KiB, control characters dropped). The page reads everything from a new read-only `GET /api/about`, which takes no input but the fixed `notes=1`. Tests: the stamp's shape and every value that is not a version thrown away, the notes file (missing, a folder, hostile text, over the limit), the route (stamped values, notes only from the editor's folder, no session refused, no write method), the script on made-up repositories (no tag, on a tag, commits after it, dirty, untracked, a pre-release tag, another module's tag ignored), the real program built with the script's flags and run with `--version` (so a renamed variable cannot stamp nothing without a failing test), and the page in a browser engine (label, panel, notes as inert text, focus, Escape).
- **Docs: a design note for build numbers, releases and an in-app update check** (docs only, no behaviour change; #806). It settles the version and build counter, the release layout, signing, the release workflow, the swap of the running exe and the build slices, and records that the update check will be the one exception to "no outbound connection" and which places change with it. Nothing is built yet.
- **Reference pictures for the Landsraad tab in `docs/art/landsraad/`** (docs only; nothing in the editor loads them): the 25 house symbols at 128 px, the five mission lane icons, the five specialization banner icons and 15 decree pictures, with a README that lists every file. Game artwork, covered by `docs/art-notice.md`.
- **Command Center visual foundation, milestone M2** (no editing wired; nothing in the editor loads it yet): scoped styles and small parts in `internal/web/static/lcc/` (the voting plaque with its 25 seats and the gold 13th seat, the specialization lane card, the House card, and the primitives they share), optimized local copies of the House, lane, path banner and decree pictures with a fixed-list lookup (no file name is ever built from a name; the Mikkarol / Mikarrol spelling is an explicit alias; the plain special-vendor decree gets a neutral picture), a manifest with hashes (`docs/design/landsraad-command-center/art-manifest.json`), and a component gallery under `docs/design/landsraad-command-center/gallery/` that is labelled fixture data, is not embedded or routed, and reads no save. Dark and Dayside, the three looks, the three motion settings, forced colours and a narrow layout are covered by tests and by headless captures (`scripts/lcc-gallery-shots.mjs`). The Landsraad and Specialization tabs are unchanged. The pictures are Funcom game art outside the repository licence (`docs/art-notice.md`).
- **Command Center plaque: the real Atreides and Harkonnen crests replace the drawn placeholder marks** (display and art only; nothing in the editor loads the Command Center yet): the white crest on the two header shields, the two cloth standards and a claimed 13th seat, and the round medallion on every claimed seat, as in the approved plaque. They are a separate set from the House and path pictures, found through the same kind of fixed-list lookup that always answers (a missing picture leaves the words and the drawn seat mark standing), listed in the art manifest, and covered by the art notice as Funcom game art outside the repository licence (`docs/art-notice.md`, `docs/art/landsraad/factions/`).
- **Command Center plaque: the two contributor cloths are laid out as in the approved plaque** (display only; nothing in the editor loads the Command Center yet): a larger crest, a one-line heading, a centred numbered list of up to five names (a long name is cut with an ellipsis on the wide plaque and wraps on the narrow one, never as markup), the foot of the cloth clear of the House nameplates with a gold edge in the narrow layout, equal narrow columns, an outlined box in forced colours, and an empty state that stays the honest "Not in the save".
- **Command Center board: a text floor, larger tier boxes, a wider page and a House Intelligence panel that stays in view** (display remediation A, stacked on the Command Center board; display only, no save write and nothing a click sends changed; not checked in the game). The audit of the board at nine widths found text down to 6 px, tier boxes too small to hit, a page held at 1300 px and a blank band beside a tall House Intelligence panel. Text is now 11 px or more everywhere on the board (names and key labels 12 px or more) and the cards grow, or the board reflows, instead of shrinking the text. The tier boxes are 24 x 24 px with a 26 x 36 px hit area at 900 px and below and on touch (the design's 18 x 20 px stay in a wide panel); the board keeps five columns down to a panel of 696 px, then three, then two at 480 px and below. The 1300 px page cap is lifted while the board view is on (1800 px), the House Intelligence panel is sticky beside the board from 1080 px of the tab (it clears the page header and the game and launch notices, scrolls inside itself and does not pass the scroll on), and below that the victory analysis is a full-width band with the House Intelligence panel full width under it (in two columns from 860 px of the tab), so no empty space is left beside a short panel. The narrow plaque is compact (about 310 px tall at 768 px, banners beside the track from 600 px); the cards of a row line up and "NO TIERS IN THE SAVE" is styled like the other owner lines; nothing is cut at 320 px. Container queries and the layout that needs `:has()` sit inside `@supports`, and the light-theme borders of the tier boxes pass 3:1. Tests: the floor, the tier boxes, the guards, the layout and the contrast are pinned in `web-tests/lcc-css.test.mjs`. The fidelity matrix lists what changed row by row, with the audit findings N1 to N15 and what is left for a later step (moving the bulk bar's markup, keeping the scroll position across a redraw, moving the Actions block).
- **Command Center board: the bulk bar in reading order, the panel keeps its place, Actions first, notes folded, a bar for phones** (display remediation B, stacked on remediation A; presentation and focus only: no save write, and nothing a click sends or when changed; not checked in the game). The bulk bar now sits in the board grid right after the board panel, so Tab goes from the last card to the bar and then to the House Intelligence panel, the order in which they are seen, and the layout no longer relies on `display: contents`. A redraw (a filter, a card, a layout, a faction, a write) keeps the scroll position of the panel for the same House and of the page, and the focused control stays in view. The Actions block is the first block under the House header; the question and the result of a click made in the panel appear right under it (a bulk question and result stay in the bulk bar). The long notes (the Fill caption, the legend, the "Important" note, the backup and refusal notes) are folded into disclosures. On a phone (coarse pointer or 640 px and narrower) a bar pinned to the bottom shows "N chosen" with Fill chosen, Complete chosen and Reopen chosen, which press the bulk bar's own buttons, a "Back to board" link returns to the House's card, and the cards are compact (page 11 to 14 percent shorter at 390 and 320 px), all with the text floor kept (names 12 px or more, other text 11 px or more). Tests: `web-tests/lcc-board-requests.test.mjs` plays every writing control (card and panel tier boxes, Fill, Fill faction goal, Complete, Reopen, Apply tier, the bulk buttons from the bulk bar and from the phone bar) and compares the request, the question and the result line with those recorded before this change; the layout, order, disclosure and phone rules are pinned in `web-tests/lcc-css.test.mjs` and `web-tests/lcc-board-layout.test.mjs`. This change needs the review of the write path together with the board it is stacked on.

### Changed
- **The base file import holds a piece's health to the most its type has in the game, and checks volumes and durability records more strictly** (refs #613, audit G-009, G-021, G-045; SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). A building piece or placeable whose health is above the highest figure seen for its type in a real save with a quarter added (7,000 x 1.25 for a foundation, 8,750 for a type with no figure of its own, 25,000 for a placeable with none) is refused with the row and the limit; the ceiling was 1,000,000 for every type. An inventory's volume limit or an item's volume that is negative or above 1,000,000, a durability given as text, and stats that mention a durability but are not a record, or a durability record that is not the game's pair of a list and a record of fields, are refused too (the check used to pass such stats unread). A durability key given twice (in the stats or in the record) or spelled in another case is refused as well: a file that says one thing first and another last would be read one way by this check and another way by the database. The limits of the import and of the repair functions now live in one small package, `internal/policy`, so the file check and Repair buildings, Repair base and the automatic repair keep to the same caps. Not changed: a slot past an inventory's stated slot count is still allowed (the game's own saves hold some), and quality, shelter, sand buildup and positions have no range check, as no real figure limits them.
- **Create a vehicle, Bring vehicle and Duplicate vehicle look in front of you first, and say so** (refs #480; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). The first spot was already 10 m ahead for a ground vehicle and 30 m ahead and 7 m up for an aircraft (#603, #541), but when something was in the way the search tried the whole ring round the character, behind included, before going farther ahead, so a vehicle could land 10 to 18 m behind a character who faced open ground (seen 2026-11-23: facing east, three vehicles to the west and south-west). Now, outdoors with a facing that can be read, the half in front (a quarter turn to either side of the facing) is searched to 30 m past the first spot before anything behind is tried; the clear-spot rules, the rule in a base, the refusal when no spot is clear and the height (the character's feet plus 150; 7 m up for an aircraft) are as before. The preview, the result and the toasts say "10 m in front of you (east)" ("behind you" when the spot is behind, the compass point alone to a side; in a base "outside your base, ..." as before), and the preview and the result line add the map and the coordinates ("Map and place: HaggaBasin, x 1100, y 200, z 450."; the answer's `place.coords`). A new internal helper, `placeLine`, puts several vehicles down in one go in a line along the facing, 12 m apart (more when two footprints are wider), each keeping clear of the ones before it; Create, Bring and Duplicate put down one vehicle at a time and do not call it yet. Terrain and rocks are not in a save: face open ground before you quit to the menu. Tests: all four facings for ground and flying classes, the line of three, a blocked front searched in front before behind, only the half behind left open, the words and coordinates, no facing, no clear spot. The existing tests changed only in the words ("10 m in front of you (south)" for "10 m south of you").
- **Landsraad: the Actions panel of the House inspector is tidied, the count line reads N / 25 shown, the action bar N / 25 chosen, and the term panel's result is a toast** (refs #1118, #1119, #1120; display and wording only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed and the recorded-request golden files are byte-identical; not checked in the editor). **Actions panel:** a two-column grid of equal-width keys with every row ending at the panel's right edge; the Faction box over the whole width; Fill 5 tiers and Fill faction goal side by side, with Fill 5 tiers the one accent key; Complete House next to Reopen, with Reopen the quieter one (it keeps its question; the red edge is replaced by the quiet look); Set tier and Apply tier a little below. **Counts:** the count line is always "N / 25 shown" ("25 / 25 shown" when no filter narrows the board; the word All is gone from it and from its sentence for a screen reader), and the action bar says "N / 25 chosen". **Term panel:** what Apply, Make active and Set flag did is a toast like the board's other results (a refusal is an error toast that stays until it is dismissed); the green line under the drop-downs that never went away is gone. The "Not applied yet" draft hint under the Active decree box is not a result and stays. A result of the Landsraad page is no longer drawn with the green check and edge of the module (the Atreides hue): its check and edge are the gold of the page.
- **Landsraad and Specialization: plain wording for the leftovers, one name for each thing** (refs #1094, #1095, #1099, #1100, #1101; wording only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark, not in the game). A House with no reward tiers says "NO TIERS" (card), a dash (Inspector) and "This House has no reward tiers." instead of "in the save", with the reason in the hover; the Classic distance "From you: not known" keeps its reason in the hover; the Specialization note says the Houses "are not shown". The contributor hover on the banners no longer talks about rows and what is worked out. The unused FROM THE SAVE and WORKED OUT HERE labels are removed from `lcc.js`. The Classic list says "earned" where the Command Center does (the tier words "Earned, collected" and "Earned, no delivery recorded", "top tier earned", the legend and the tile counts), "Place" for where a House is (the peek and "Place unknown" in both views), and the House hover card no longer says "experimental". The Classic "N of N houses shown" count is read out to a screen reader but no longer stands on the page, and the Term tile that repeated the heading "Term N" is gone. The Classic Advanced card is "Advanced: specialization numbers". Not changed here, because the recorded-request golden files hold the text: the result lines "(tiers N to M reached)", "Type the track number first." and "... is track number N".
- **Add XP shows its refusal above the box, keeps Ctrl+minus, and the refusal is a live region** (fixes #1039, items 1 to 3; the Command Center Specialization page only; no validation rule, request, save field or write changed: XP is still a whole number from 1 up, the refusal text is the same, and the request of Add XP is the same as before; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark, and in jsdom, not in the game). The refusal ("Type a whole number of XP from 1 to 44,182. ...") sat under the box, where the tip that opens while the box has focus covered it, so a typed 0 or a pasted -5 showed no reason until Escape. It is now drawn between the label and the box, never under the tip. The box swallowed every minus key, also Ctrl+minus and Cmd+minus (the browser zoom out); those keys pass now and the plain minus key is still refused. The refusal was hidden and unhidden (a live region that is unhidden is often not announced): it is now always in the page, empty when the number is fine, and takes no room then. Tests: `lcc-spec-addxp-1039.test.mjs` fails on the old page and passes on this one. Not done: item 4 (the Classic view's stale maximum XP) and item 5 (a note in the baseline documents), which the issue keeps open.
- **One name for the Houses heading, the view switch and Add XP; the Classic reset-all question says specializations; plainer hint for an unmatched specialization** (refs #1061, #1063; wording only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark, and in jsdom, not in the game). The heading over the Houses is "Houses" in both views (was "Command Center board" in the board view and "Task board" in the Classic list, internal product names above "The Landsraad"); the View switch says "Classic list" and "Command Center" on the Landsraad and the Specialization page (was "Command Center board" on the Landsraad page); the Command Center button is "Add XP" like the Classic one (was "Apply XP"). In the Classic Specialization page the reset-all question says "Reset the XP of N specializations?" with the typed sentence "reset N specializations" and the result "N specializations reset to 0 XP", and Grant all keystones says "every keystone of every specialization" (was "track"). The recorded-request golden file `lcc-spec-requests.golden.json` changed for exactly these four strings (asked and said) and for nothing else; no request in it changed. The hint under an unmatched specialization reads "The editor cannot match this specialization yet: see Advanced below." (was "Number not known yet: ..."); the track numbers stay in the Advanced cards and in Help. Help (Specializations) says Add XP. Still open in #1061: reached or earned, and Open or Taken ("house taken by", "taken by X" in the action tips and result lines), which have no decided word; the two results of the Advanced track-number editors keep "track number" on purpose. The fidelity matrix (N8 to N13) says so.
- **Command Center: calm headings, first slice of #1051** (refs #1051; colours only: no layout, size, wording, request, save field or behaviour changed, and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in headless Edge in 24 looks and palettes, dark and light, not in the game). Decided by the operator on 2026-11-19 (build 499: the gold headings were too bright). The eyebrows, section headings, field labels, disclosure titles and Show / Hide buttons of Term and Decrees, the Landsraad board and its Inspector, and the Specialization page no longer use the gold text token; they use two new tokens of `lcc.css`, `--lcc-heading` (the look's text colour) and `--lcc-heading-quiet` (a half step from its quiet colour towards its text colour, so 11 px letters keep 4.5:1 on every surface of every look and palette), which follow the chosen look, Dark / Dayside and palette. Bright gold stays on the plaque and banners, the win outline and its animation, icons and emblems, the active decree marker, the figures, the flags and the buttons that were already gold. The lowest contrast of a moved text over all 24 looks and palettes is 5.03:1. Forced-colours rules are unchanged. A new test, `web-tests/calm-headings.test.mjs`, keeps the moved selectors off the gold tokens and the kept ones on them.
- **House cards: no bullet before "Pledged to Harkonnen" / "Undecided" / "Wavering", and the line keeps off the card edge; the card hover and the Inspector say "House Harkonnen"** (fixes #1083; display only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed; checked in headless Edge at 320 to 1600 px wide, dark and light, and in jsdom, not in the game). The faction dot (a filled circle in the faction colour, an open circle for Undecided and Wavering) of the old "HARKONNEN" line survived #1073 before the words, on the cards and in the Faction column of the list; both are gone, and the faction colour stays on the pledged words. The words are kept short on a card ("Pledged to House Harkonnen" does not fit, and "House" on a card reads like the card's own House), so the longer "Pledged to House Harkonnen" is in the hover of the card and in the House Intelligence band, its tooltip and its screen-reader text. The line now has 6 px of side padding and a letter spacing of .01em (was .03em, which made "Pledged to Harkonnen" 1 px too wide for a 1280 px card once the padding was there); a card too narrow for it on one line (the narrowest in the grid, 129 px at 320 px wide and 136 px at 768 px wide) breaks it between the words instead of running over the edge. Measured, the text never touches or crosses the card edge (smallest gap 11 px at 768, 12 px at 1280) and nothing is clipped. The card tier boxes, icons and every other part of the card are unchanged. Help (The Landsraad board) and the fidelity matrix (B8) say so; web-tests/pledge-line.test.mjs pins the words, the padding and the fit.
- **The empty House Intelligence panel follows the view: "Choose a House on the board to see its tiers and progress." in the grid, "Choose a House in the list to see its tiers and progress." in the list** (fixes #1081; display only, no request or save field changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, and in jsdom). It said "Choose a House on the board or in the list to see its tiers, its progress and what each action would do." Classic has no such sentence. Help and the fidelity matrix (N6) say so.
- **The tooltip of a place with no pin says what is true: "The Saved map has no picture of X yet" when the editor has no picture of that map, "X is outside the picture of Y" when it has the picture and the place lies off it** (refs #1069, #1083; display only). The link tooltip in the list, the Place note in House Intelligence and the Classic hover said "no picture of Y yet" for both, which was wrong for a place just outside the frame of the picture of Hagga Basin, the Deep Desert, Arrakeen or Harko Village. The two Command Center wordings come from one function (`noPictureSays`, lcc-model.js) and the Classic list has its own copy; a test holds them together and holds the list of maps to the Go table (`houseMapKey`). Help and the fidelity matrix (N7) say so.
- **One word for the Specializations: "specialization", never "path" or "track"; one word for where a House is: "Place"** (fixes #1061; wording only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). Decided by the operator on 2026-11-19. In the Command Center: THE FIVE SPECIALIZATIONS OF ARRAKIS, CHOOSE A SPECIALIZATION, SELECTED SPECIALIZATION, THIS SPECIALIZATION (was INDIVIDUAL TRACK), All Specializations (was All Five Tracks), Reset XP (was Reset Track XP; its screen-reader name is "Reset XP for this specialization"), Reset XP of All Known Specializations, the lane badges COMPLETE (was FULL PATH COMPLETE), IN PROGRESS (was PATH IN PROGRESS) and NOT IDENTIFIED (was TRACK NOT IDENTIFIED), and every tooltip, note and screen-reader label. In the Classic list: the heading Specializations, the first column Specialization, Reset XP of all known specializations, and the same sentences as the Command Center about what the editor cannot match; the track-number card and the raw row are the Advanced cards and keep the word track for the save's own track numbers (the pointers to them say "under Advanced below"). The Help page and the result toast that says an XP amount did not fit follow. Where a House is: the list column (Command Center and Classic) and the Inspector section say Place (were Location and WHERE), like the hover card; Region stays for the map region ("Region unknown" for the group of Houses with no region, was "Location unknown"). Not changed, because the golden request files record them: the confirmation questions and result lines of Grant all keystones, Grant max, Reset, Reset all and the track-number editor, which still say "track" in a few places (for example "Reset the XP of 2 tracks?" and the typed sentence "reset 2 tracks"). A new test (`web-tests/specialization-wording.test.mjs`) bans "path" and "track" in the player-facing text of both views, tooltips and screen-reader labels included, outside the Advanced cards, and checks that the two views say the same words. Not changed: the Landsraad page headings, the view switch, reached and earned, and Open and Taken, which the operator's decision did not name.
- **One look for every control that is off: no dashed edge, no fade** (fixes #1049; style only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). A button, a drop-down, a box, a text field, a tab and a switch that is off now share one look on the Command Center and on the classic pages: the quiet text colour, a quiet solid edge, no fill, no shadow and the not-allowed pointer, set once in three tokens (`--dis-fg`, `--dis-bg`, `--dis-edge`) at the top of `app.css`. The dashed edges of the board (filter chips, drop-downs, buttons, tier rows) and of the classic list (buttons, tier boxes, the By region box), and the faded looks of the Term and Specializations controls (opacity .55 to .6) and of a House card that the filters leave out, are gone; a tier box that does nothing now keeps its state colours and takes the quiet edge and the pointer. The label keeps its contrast because nothing is faded. Tests: a new web test reads every style file and fails on a dashed or faded rule for a disabled control. Help (The Landsraad board) and the fidelity matrix are updated.
- **Landsraad wording: a House is Pledged to a faction, Undecided or Wavering, the Landsraad is divided or swayed, and the Sysselraad is secured, never won** (fixes #1073; wording only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed and the recorded-request golden files are unchanged; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). A faction does not win a House: the House is pledged to it. Cards, the Inspector band and ledger headers, the list column, the House filter, the hover card, the legend, the Classic list and Help now read "Pledged to <Faction>" or "Undecided", and "Wavering" when both factions have points on a House that neither has. The plaque reads SYSSELRAAD SECURED. Next term's decree follows the state of the Landsraad: "The Landsraad is still divided" when no faction is in control, "<Faction> have swayed the Landsraad; their decree is yet to be chosen" when a faction is in control and no decree is chosen, a dash ("Not recorded in the save.") when the save does not hold it, the decree otherwise. Tests: web-tests/pledge-wording.test.mjs.
- **House Intelligence: the TOP CONTRIBUTORS block is gone** (fixes #1077; display only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed; checked in jsdom and headless Edge, not in the game). The panel beside the board no longer lists the biggest contributors to the House, and no longer shows a dash for a House the save holds no contributor row for. The heading tooltip and the screen-reader text of Faction house control no longer name contributors. The plaque above the board still lists the biggest contributors under each faction.
- **House cards: the tier boxes look as they did before #1071: an earned, not collected box is the gold box with the diamond again** (fixes #1082; display only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280, dark and light, and in jsdom, not in the game). #1076 (#1071) restyled the tier boxes on the House cards as well as the ledger rows of House Intelligence, but the operator asked only for the ledger rows. The card boxes are restored: earned and not collected is the gold box (#f5c878) with the dark diamond, collected is the green check box, no delivery record is the "?" box, not reached is the numbered box. The ledger rows (teal edge and check for collected, empty ring for earned and not collected, dimmed for not reached) are unchanged, and so are the card wording and tooltips. Help (The Landsraad board, "The marks on a tier") and the fidelity matrix (rows F3, F6, R8) say so; web-tests/card-tier-boxes.test.mjs pins the mark and class of each state.
- **House cards: the count line under "N/5 EARNED" is gone** (fixes #1078; display only: nothing is written, no request, save field or count changed; checked in jsdom and headless Edge, not in the game). Every card in the board grid now has the one line, for example 3/5 EARNED, and no second line of collected and not collected counts. Hover the figure to read them (Collected, Not collected, and any tier with no delivery record); the accessible name of the card already said them in words.
- **Landsraad pages: the source labels FROM THE SAVE, SAVE, SAVE + DERIVED and WORKED OUT HERE are gone, and so are the table and field names in tooltips** (fixes #1057; display and wording only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). The label "FROM THE SAVE" on the House Intelligence head (the empty panel too) and on the header of Term and Decrees, "SAVE" beside FACTION HOUSE CONTROL and YOUR CONTRIBUTION, "SAVE + DERIVED" beside PERSONAL REWARD LEDGER, and the small `save` and `derived` marks beside the headings of the Classic list, board, counts, Term and decree pool are removed, with the style rules that drew them. A heading is only its words. The tooltips said "Read from the save: landsraad_tasks.completed, winning_faction_id ...", "Worked out by the editor from fields the save holds ...", "(save number 73)", "the identifier the save gives it" and "the save's own disabled column"; they now say what is shown in plain words, and the unnamed-contributor note no longer gives the save's number. Help (The Landsraad board) names the source once, in a new section "Where the figures come from" (everything is read from the save; the editor works out the earned tiers, who has won a House's favor and the Sysselraad, and the order of contributors; nothing is guessed), and the table names stay in its Technical notes. The About texts of the Classic counts and board no longer explain marks that are not there. The fidelity matrix (rows S2 and R11) and the tests say so.
- **Landsraad pages: the last standing explanatory lines are a hover or focus tip, a screen-reader text, or gone** (fixes #1062, with the earlier part in #1067; display only: no request, save field, count or behaviour changed; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). "Favor won ≠ earned ≠ collected" beside Set tier in House Intelligence is the hover text of the "Set tier" label. The caption of the Command Center list ("The Houses of the term, by default by House name, A to Z") is read out by a screen reader and no longer drawn while the board is a board (it stays drawn when the board could not be checked, since it then says so). The line under the board ("Your rewards on this board: ...", which repeated the figures of House Intelligence) is the hover text of the heading "The Landsraad". The counts "✓ 0 ○ 0" under "0/5 EARNED" on a House card are no longer drawn; they show once a tier is earned (the card keeps its height, so a row of cards lines up). On the Specializations page the two lines "Grant LV 100 sets this track ..." under Grant LV 100 / Reset Track XP and "Keystones now ... The two keystone buttons change every track" under the keystone buttons are the same info-mark tip as the XP amount rule (#1030): shown on hover and on keyboard focus, with the buttons still pointing to the tip by `aria-describedby`; the keystone count is the KEYSTONES figure of the header. Help (The Landsraad board), the fidelity matrix (rows F6, B16 and R10) and the tests say so.
- **House Intelligence and the cards: a dash or 0, not "Not in the save", "none recorded" or "not known"** (fixes #1056, with the earlier part in #1067; display only: no request, save field or count changed; checked in jsdom and in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). The places that were left in `lcc-board.js`: the specialization line of the card and of the Inspector ("Specialization not in the save") is a dash, with that sentence as its hover text; TOP CONTRIBUTORS of a House with no contributor row in the save shows the heading and a dash, as the banners do, and the reason (including that a House whose favor is won can have none) is the hover text; YOUR CONTRIBUTION with nothing recorded reads 0 ("none recorded in the save" stays in the hover and screen-reader text); the amount of a tier the save does not give is a dash in the ledger; a House goal the save does not give reads "0 / —" on the faction bars (a screen reader still hears "not known"). The longer tooltip of FACTION HOUSE CONTROL (goal, top contributors, missions) keeps its words, since it is hover text. The Classic list and the other pages were done in #1067. Help is unchanged (it never quoted these words); the fidelity matrix (rows B7 and R9) and the tests say so.
- **Reward ledger: no gold bar and no gold NOT COLLECTED on the tier rows; collected, earned and not collected, and not reached are a shape each** (fixes #1071; display only, decided by the operator on 2026-11-19: no request, save field, count or tier state changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). The rows of the Personal Reward Ledger in House Intelligence had a gold left edge and a gold diamond with NOT COLLECTED on each earned-and-waiting tier (the "waiting" style of the first board, #876). Now: a **collected** tier has a 2 px teal edge on the left and a filled teal circle with a check at the right edge; an **earned, not collected** tier has only an empty ring in the quiet colour (no edge, no gold, no words); a tier **not reached** is dimmed, with no edge and no mark. The teal is the token of the "Collected x / y" figure above the ledger (`--lcc-teal-t`, dark and light), not a new colour; contrast against the row measured in headless Edge: edge and circle 8.08 : 1 dark and 5.89 : 1 light, ring 9.31 : 1 and 7.64 : 1, dimmed text 9.31 : 1 and 7.64 : 1 (all above 3 : 1 for a mark and 4.5 : 1 for text). The state stays in words for a screen reader (each row's accessible name and a hidden text, "Collected", "Earned, not collected", "Not reached"); shape, not colour alone, tells them apart (check and ring). A tier with no delivery record keeps its red edge and its words. In forced colours the edge, circle and ring use system colours. The card tier boxes were restyled in the same change by mistake and are restored in #1082 (the gold diamond box again). The Command Center list never showed gold for this state and is unchanged. The **Classic list** keeps its own tint for a waiting tier (the page's warning colour `--c-warn`, in `app.css`): not changed here, since it is the Classic design, not the board's gold; left to the operator. Help (The Landsraad board, "The marks on a tier"), the fidelity matrix (rows F3 and R8) and the tests say so.
- **An unclaimed House card reads "NO FAVOR", not "NOT WON"** (fixes #1070; wording only, decided by the operator on 2026-11-19: the card keeps its owner line, so every square has the same rows and the squares stay aligned; no request, save field or count changed; checked in headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). The line of a House nobody has won was "○ NOT WON", which the operator did not expect or want. It is "○ NO FAVOR" now, muted, in the slot and at the size of the faction line of a won card ("● ATREIDES", "● HARKONNEN"). The accessible name of the card and its hover card still say "favor not won". The new word is `WORDS.noFavor` in `lcc-model.js` and is used by the board card only: the list cell, the House filter and the Inspector keep "Not won" and "Favor not won", which answer the question of their own header ("Favor won by") and their own control; matching them is left to the operator. Help (The Landsraad board), the fidelity matrix row B8 and the tests say so.
- **The faction banners' TOP CONTRIBUTORS list shows the rank numbers 1. to 5. again, before each name** (fixes #1066; display only: the ranking, the data, the names and every request are unchanged; checked in headless Edge at 320 to 1600 px, dark and light, forced colours included, not in the game). This restores what #992 removed, by the operator's decision (the numbers had read as a score when amounts tied; the amounts are gone since #1043, so the numbers now only give the place in the order). The list is still an ordered list (`ol`, role list), now `1. Name` to `5. Name` with no amounts: the number is a CSS counter drawn in a column of its own beside the name, gold on the cloth (the system text colour in forced colours), the same size as the name (12 px or more), and a long or unbroken name breaks inside its own column, so a name never moves under its number and the numbers hang in line in both the wide and the narrow banner. The House Inspector list was already numbered (#1058); the Classic list and the Term section draw no banner. Help (The Landsraad board, Term and decrees), the fidelity matrix row P5, the requirements matrix and the tests say so; the sweep (`scripts/lcc-m7-sweep.mjs`) now fails when a banner numeral is hidden, under 12 px or not beside its name.
- **The faction banners list the TOP CONTRIBUTORS by name only, no amounts** (fixes #1043; display only: the ranking, the data and every request are unchanged; headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px). This reverses the always-visible amount that #972 added on purpose: the amount span of each line, its style rules (including the forced-colours one), the amount in the line's mouse-over text and the amounts in the heading's mouse-over text ("Top 3: 1. Name; 2. Name; 3. Name.") are gone. The names, their order (the biggest first, worked out from the amounts), the one name per line, the wrapping of a long name and the five-line list are as before; the banner never drew rank numerals since #992 and still does not. The House Inspector still lists the contributors of a House with their amounts. Help (The Landsraad board, Term and decrees), the fidelity matrix rows P5 and T17 and the tests say so.
- **Specialization Command header: SPECIALIZATIONS COMPLETED and KEYSTONES, two tiles** (fixes #1042; display only: no request, save field or count changed; headless Edge at 1280 and 390 px, dark and Dayside). The tile TRACKS AT XP CAP is now SPECIALIZATIONS COMPLETED (the same count, 5 / 5 for five paths at the cap; its tooltip says that completed means the XP cap, level 100, and that keystones are counted apart) and the MAXIMUM XP / TRACK tile (44,182) is removed from the header; the XP ceiling is still a fact of the dossier. The two tiles sit side by side in the wide header, in the stacked header and on a phone (the full-width third row and its rule are gone). Help (Specializations), the M5 matrix rows H4 and H6 and the tests say so.
- **The specialization link of the House Inspector opens the Specialization page at the lanes, with that path chosen** (fixes #1041; display and scroll only: nothing is written, no request or save field changed). "Gathering Specialization" (and the other four paths) already chose the path, but the page opened at its top, so the chosen lane and its dossier were below the fold (in a 900 px high window the selected lane began 22 px below the bottom of it at 1280 px wide, and about 1,000 px below it at 390 px). Since #1025 a move to another page scrolls to the top before the new page draws, so the page now scrolls to the row that heads the lanes after it is drawn (the sticky header is cleared); a visit by the tabs and a lane pressed by hand are unchanged. Checked in a headless Edge for all five paths at 1280 and 390 px, and in `web-tests/spec-link-scroll.test.mjs`, which records the order of the scrolls.
- **A decree shows its name and its effect, not the game's id** (fixes #1040; display only: no request, save field or route changed, and the request goldens are unchanged). The line under a decree that read `SpecialVendorActive_Vehicles` above "Access to a Landsraad Vehicle Vendor" is gone, for every decree, in both views: the Command Center Term and Decrees section (the active and next decree facts, the pool rows, the line under the Active decree drop-down) and the classic Term and decrees panel. The name and the effect line stay; a decree the game's table has no name for still shows a readable form of its id with the note that no game name is known. The decree pool's own row number is not the game's id and stays. Help (Term and decrees) and the M6 matrix row T2 say so.
- **Command Center list: cells that wrapped to three lines are one line, and the Not collected column is no longer cut off** (fixes #1059; the "0 OF 5 EARNED" tag goes with #1046; display only: no request or save field changed; checked in a headless browser at 1280 and 390 px, dark and light, not in the game). A House with no place shows a dash instead of "not mapped yet", the Tiers column says "2 of 5" (the words "tiers earned" stay for a screen reader), and "no map picture" is no longer written in the place cell (it is the mouse-over and screen-reader text). The list text is 12 px with tighter padding, so all ten columns fit the table at 1280 px; the place cell keeps its row line and centring. At narrow widths the table scrolls inside its own box and does not widen the page.
- **Landsraad rewards: an item the editor has no name for is "Unlisted item", never its internal id** (fixes #1055; display and wording only: no request or save field changed; checked in a headless browser, not in the game).
