Not changed: a held or winning card showing two rings could not be reproduced (no chosen-line ring is drawn any more, and the selection ring yields to the win outline); the other small targets listed in #910 were not changed: the board filter chips and bulk buttons already meet the floors that the sweep checks (44 px at 640 px or less, 36 px above) and the sweep found nothing under them; the House tier boxes keep the design's 18 by 20 px for a mouse (24 px with a 44 px hit area on a phone or touch screen); the classic list's quick-action buttons keep the 32 px that every button of the page has with a mouse (the WCAG 2.2 minimum is 24 px).
- **Command Center board: the low-severity look and feel leftovers** (fixes #882, #883, #890, #896, #919, #920, #921, #947, #998; refs #888; display, wording and focus only: no save write, request body, route or golden changed; not checked in the game). What changed:
  - In forced-colours mode the selected chips (Show, House), the selected Grid / List button and the selected view button of the Specializations show a readable label (HighlightText on Highlight, with an inner outline as well as the fill); before, the browser laid a black plate behind the label (#998).
  - The key to the board (Atreides favor won, and so on) is 12 px, and the dead 9 px legend rules are gone (#896). The last line of the Imperial Victory Analysis (BY 13 HOUSES, BY FIVE IN A LINE, House count) is 12 px in the normal text colour (#947).
  - A contributor the save names no guild for is shown as "Unnamed contributor", with the number from the save only in the mouse-over text; the mouse-over of a House now says a guild is shown by the identifier the save gives, with capital letters added (#919).
  - On a narrow plaque the two banners end level whatever the length of the names (#920), and the small diamond under each nameplate no longer touches the nameplate's corner (part of #888).
  - The Choose boxes of the board and the Classic list, and the Classic list's detail boxes, are drawn with autocomplete off, so a page reload does not show them ticked while nothing is chosen (#921).
  - A redraw while a write runs draws the House panel's Faction and Set tier selects off, and a faction picked in the panel that the board's own box did not take goes back to the one in use (#882). After Apply tier the focus stays on the Apply tier button (#883), and a House id that arrives as a digit string is read as a number (#883).
  - The folded help of the Classic list, the Reopen mouse-overs and the Help page of Fill and tiers say "the completion and the faction that holds the House's favor" in place of "the winner" and "that faction's win" (#890).
- **Landsraad leftovers of the reviews of #771, #773 and #776: the written text, the grounds of a raise after a faction change, an old queue whose write landed, and the House Intelligence selects left off after a write** (refs #772, #775, #778, #611; a **SAVE WRITE PATH**, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). What changes:
  - The description of an edit that changed several houses with one plain action says it: "landsraad: 3 houses completed for Atreides" or "landsraad: 3 houses reopened", not "changed from the board". Mixed changes keep the old head. A faction name read from the save is cut at 40 characters in a description and in the answer, like a house name; a number in a refusal or a description is written in full (1000000, not 1e+06).
  - On the page, a write that only moved other completion times no longer says "nothing to change"; the notes of a write (a win taken away, times moved) stand before a warning, not inside its sentence; the queue list takes the last "houses: " as the list of houses; the earlier terms text says what "last payout" means.
  - A journal kept by an older build whose write already landed in the file (its hash is the file on disk, and nothing was queued after it) is removed without a notice; before, it was reported as "could not be put back". Any other journal of another version is still set aside and never replayed, and the reason now lists what it held so it can be made again. Every queued change of the build before #655 is set aside this way and has to be made again.
  - House Intelligence: after a plain successful write the Faction and Set tier selects stayed off (the board is read again while the write still counts as running, so the new panel is drawn off, and nothing turned it on). They are on again when the write ends; Set tier stays off for a House with no tiers.
  - Tests: what the character reached under another faction (the board's `my_elsewhere`, the highest and not the sum, none for a character in no faction), and the journal rules above. Docs: after a change of faction the ground under the earlier faction counts as paid (on the Fill and tiers help and in docs/save-database.md); two table rows and one sentence of docs/save-database.md that still said the events decide a delivered tier or that any raise writes the swatch tables.
  - Request bodies, goldens, the one take-back rule and the paid-ground record are unchanged.
- **Landsraad board and editor header: accessibility, touch sizes and contrast** (fixes #889, #619, #823, #830, #831, #832, #887; part of #659; display, wording and focus only: no save write, request body, route or golden changed; not checked on a phone or with a screen reader). What changed:
  - A disabled control (Complete House, Fill, Reopen, the Grid and List buttons, the chips, the tier rows, the Classic list's quick actions) is shown by a dashed edge and quiet text, not by fading, so its label keeps full contrast (it was 2.8 to 3.6 to 1). The not-reached tier box of a House card has a lighter edge in the dark look (3.2 to 1 against the card, was 2.3). The Not started part of the Classic list's bar uses stripes in the quiet text colour. The small source marks (save, derived) are 11 px, not 10.
  - A touch screen, or a window of 640 px or less, now gets 44 px on the header's Discard queue, Theme, version and queue chips and Auto-refresh, and on the Classic list's House name, place and quick-action buttons, About summaries and the bar of its counts (28 px).
  - The queue chip's label starts with its visible words and carries the count ("3 queued, show the queued changes"); the Theme button's label starts with its visible words; the glance tiles' labels start with their visible words; the source marks are read once in a sentence above the board, not inside every heading.
  - The Classic board region is named by its own heading, and each row is a table row with cells.
  - A copy (a save that is not the game's own) is shown by its file name only in the banner and the window title, never with its folder; the full path stays in the tooltip of the file name on the page. The two Movement notes no longer carry test-session detail.
  - The result line and the question of a click scroll into view clear of the sticky header and of the banner under it however tall the banner is (it used a fixed offset that fitted one banner height).
  - When a re-read closes an open bulk question (a House it named is hidden by the filter now), the focus goes back to the bulk button that asked instead of dropping to the top of the page; a test covers that path, and the name of the #818 test and this file no longer list Show all houses among the controls that hide rows.
  - The victory panel and the House Intelligence panel stay stacked as full-width bands below 1080 px of the tab: the operator decided on 2026-11-19 to keep it, and it is recorded as an agreed deviation from the mock (DECISIONS.md section L, matrix row N15).
- **Specializations: leftovers of the Command Center view and of the track numbers cleared** (fixes #902, #899, #853 as far as they were still valid, refs #708; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged, because the track numbers every Specialization write and the Landsraad write work by are now resolved in one place; no route, request body or question changed; not checked in the game). Command Center view: the dossier no longer prints the track number behind a path (#708: a path is picked by its name); the key labels (SPECIALIZATION LEVEL, XP CEILING, ASSOCIATED HOUSES, KEYSTONES and the hero labels) are 12 px, not 11; a lane whose stored level is not the level of its XP says so on the lane ("SAVE STORES LV 10"), chosen or not; Apply XP accepts every spelling of a whole number that the classic list accepts (1e3, 1.0) and sends the same number; choosing another lane keeps what was typed in the XP fields; a Landsraad answer with no task list leaves the Houses "Not in the save" instead of "No House of this term is listed". The hero keystone total was already marked partial for a track that is not identified, and the page already scrolls clear of the sticky header (app.css scroll-padding-top); both now have a test. Classic list: the Track numbers card is drawn again when a number assigned by hand is not the game's own (for example Combat = 9), so it can be set right; its About text says how to clear a clash. Go: `EffectiveTrackNumbers` ignores a key that is not a track and never gives two tracks one number (the first track in order keeps it); the Landsraad write uses the same numbers as the tab (`grantSettings` used its own copy); the error for a track with no number says what to do; stale comments and the README line about a step in game before the XP buttons work are corrected. Tests: one post per Specialization write route on a save where nothing is assigned; golden file gains two cases (a track not identified: its controls are off; Grant all keystones on such a save), nothing recorded before changed. The docs of the Specializations view no longer cite a design that is not in the repository. The hero's column of totals is wider (300 to 420 px, was 275 to 360) so that the three totals read at the larger key labels.
- **Landsraad paid-ground record: a version cut by the size limit, a huge version, the size limit, the file moved aside and a Discard after an unverified write** (fixes #838, #840, #841, #837, #834; part of #799: its third limit is fixed, the other two stay known limits; a SAVE WRITE PATH (the editor's own record of the ground a Landsraad raise paid), so reviewed before it is merged; nothing checked in the game; no request, answer or golden file changed, the record stays file version 1). (1) A record larger than the limit whose `version` is cut by it (the key cut in two, a value that is not whole, a number that ends where the bytes end and may go on: `1|0`, `1|.5`, a string cut open, `nu|ll`, `[2|]`, a value that begins after the limit) was read as damaged, moved aside, and a version 1 record started: a newer record paid again. It now counts as newer: the raise is refused and the file is left as it is. (2) The version is judged by its digits and builds no number, so a version of a million digits no longer costs about a second and 2 GiB of allocation per read. (3) The size limit is derived from the entry bound (4,096 entries of the worst case and the longest save path come to 3.25 MiB, was 1 MiB), so a record the editor wrote itself can never read as too large and be moved aside; a write that would come to more is an error. (4) A version bump has a migration: a record of an older version from 1 up to the current one goes through the steps of `paidMigrations` one version at a time, and one with no step is refused like a newer one, so the day the version becomes 2 no record is moved aside and paid again (a test fails when a step is missing). (5) The move aside of a record that is of no use uses the durable rename with the record's own tries and flushes the folder, so a scanner holding the file for a moment no longer fails the write; a file that is gone is not an error; the newest ten copies are kept and older ones removed; and the banner names the copy by its name (the state's `recordNotice`), not only a log line. A second editor on the same save, which could move a new record aside, is kept out by the save's own lock. (6) A Discard after a write that could not be read back first looks at the file: when it is the one that was written the edits are settled and their notes handed to the record, and while the file cannot be read nothing is discarded (the answer says so); before, the note of a raise that was in the file was lost, and the next remove-and-raise of that house paid again. (7) The route test for the dropped-notes refusal (HTTP 500, `write_failed`, nothing written or queued), a raise-only guard for the note that comes again, the moved-aside table that reports every row, and the comments and docs of the version probe. Not changed: the restore lineages and the same item at two tiers of #799 (documented known limits: a fix would lower the cap and could pay a ground twice, which is the wrong direction), and the notes a failing record keeps in memory are still lost with a restart.
- **Landsraad grant and paid record: one rule in one place, and less repeated work in the board read** (fixes #827, refs #652, #798, #803; no change in what is written or when, and no change to any answer: this removes copies that could drift apart. The code is on the Landsraad write path, so it is read as one before it is merged). The wallet write existed twice (the Add currency route and the Landsraad raise); both now go through one in-edit helper. The highest reward tier of a term that the save shows for a house was worked out twice, in SQL for the write and in code for the board; the SQL now only fetches the rows (the task's tiers, the house's swatch, the character's delivered rows of the house) and one rule picks the tier, so the line before a click and the write count the same paid ground (a delivered row with no time no longer stands on either side). The house prefix is cut in one place for the specialization table and for messages. The record of paid ground that only tests read (`recorded`) and the read counter are out of the shipped code. A board read merges the editor's record with the queued notes once instead of once per house (up to 4,096 entries copied and scanned about 25 times), a raise merges once per house instead of twice, and ServerCustomSettings.ini is read once for both Landsraad multipliers instead of once per key (`Numbers` in the config package). Tests: the wallet helper (balance before, a missing wallet created), the prefix cut, the tier rule and the delivered-row rule, and `Numbers` against `Number`, key by key.
- **Command Center Sysselraad: a won line is no longer shown as final when Houses that cannot be read could still give the other faction a line** (fixes #893; display and wording only: nothing is written and no request, field or Go code changed; not checked in the game). When one faction held a line of five (or 13 Houses) and a House could not be read, the page showed SYSSELRAAD WON as settled even if the unreadable Houses could complete a line, or reach 13 Houses, for the other faction (the example: Atreides hold row 1, Harkonnen hold four Houses of row 5 and the fifth cannot be read). The reading now carries `conflictPossible` (true only when the other faction's Houses and the unreadable ones could still make a line, or 13 Houses; an open House is not counted, and with every House readable it is false), and the wording table says so in one short sentence ("1 House could not be read, so Harkonnen may also qualify, so this may not be final.") in the tooltip and the screen-reader text, with the short tag MAY NOT BE FINAL on the plaque's tally line and under the victory panel's how-line. The headline wording is unchanged. The evaluator's `winner` field is renamed `verdictSide` (it names the side of the verdict, not a result; the winner is `WIN.decide(...).winner` only) and documented. Tests: the issue example, both factions, the 13-House route, the cases where no conflict is possible, and a seeded comparison of 4000 boards against a try-every-reading oracle.
- **Landsraad take-back, raise and faction wording: the take-back no longer vouches for the gap between two factions' stretches, a raise is compared with the grounds its sentence was worked out from, a faction the Landsraad does not have is named, and a repeated reigning faction is not queued** (fixes #1002, #805, #842 and the still-valid last item of #846; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; nothing checked in the game; the record stays file version 1; the board-request golden changes only by the three new request fields of #805, see (2)). (1) #1002: the take-back (#944) used the lowest floor over every faction's entry of the character on the house, so with one entry paid from 1,300 to 2,400 and another from 4,800 to 6,000 it vouched for the whole range and could delete a reward row the game wrote at 3,600. The record now vouches for each entry's own stretch (its floor up to the ground it paid, cut at the highest tier that stands; touching or overlapping stretches are one), and a row is taken only when one stretch holds every tier it comes from; the note a take-back writes carries the floor of that stretch, and merging a note into an entry never joins a stretch that ends below the entry's own across the ground between them (the note's stretch is dropped; the other faction's entry still vouches for it), so repeated remove-and-raise cycles cannot widen an entry over a gap (a test runs 3,000 random sequences of raises and take-backs under two factions and asserts no tier is ever vouched for that no raise paid). Nothing is taken in a gap, and a waiting row there stays with the reason "unsure". (2) #805: a raise now also sends `was_paid`, `was_paid_rows` (the ground the editor paid before, and the ground whose reward rows are delivered) and `was_elsewhere` (what the character reached under another faction) as the page showed them, and the server refuses the raise as a conflict, with nothing written, when any of them is another number now, the way `was_events` is compared; a request without them is not refused, and a removal never depends on them. This fixes a second tab that showed "Writes 4 missions" while the write, worked from the save, paid fewer (it could never pay more). The board-request golden (`web-tests/lcc-board-requests.golden.json`) is regenerated for this on purpose: its eight raise scenarios gain `was_paid`, `was_paid_rows` and `was_elsewhere` and nothing else changes in it (every other field, the questions asked and the result lines are as recorded; the removals and the requests that change no contribution carry none of the three). (3) #842: a house won by a faction the list does not offer (Smuggler, from an earlier editor write) reads "Taken · Other faction", not "Faction 4" (only when the list of factions is loaded and does not offer the id; with no list the number is shown), and the glance line counts it apart ("Other faction 1", shown only when there is one) instead of in neither faction's count nor "still open"; the refusal of a change for a character that is in such a faction names it instead of saying it is in no faction (Neutral keeps its sentence). The faction is still not a choice anywhere. (4) #846: a request that only repeats the reigning faction the row already holds (the page sends it back when the save holds an unoffered one) is dropped, so no "reigning_faction_id = 4" edit is queued and the pending line names only what changed. The other items of #846 no longer apply (the Reigning faction drop-down has no empty choice since #960, so its text and the remembered choice cannot send an empty faction; the first words of the entry "Landsraad: the seven older write routes are retired, and one rule says which factions the Landsraad has" (fixes #657, the work of #839) are reworded to say that the page changes in its Term panel). Ports #827 and the still-valid parts of #865: one wallet write, one tier rule, one prefix cut and less repeated work in the board read.
- **Command Center board on a touch screen: a tap of a tier box on a House card only chooses the House, and the board's controls reach the agreed touch sizes** (fixes #880, #906; part of #905; front end only: no request body, route, Go code or request golden file changed, no save is read; the tap of a tier box on a touch screen no longer writes, the mouse click and the keyboard still do; not checked on a real phone or in the game). On a coarse pointer (`(pointer: coarse)`) a tap of a tier box on a House card, which could not show what it would pay because the mouse-over text is never shown on a phone, now chooses that House and opens it in the House Intelligence panel; the panel's tier rows say what each tier grants and write through the panel's own buttons, with the same questions and requests as before. A mouse click still writes at once (the hover card is the preview), and Enter or Space on a box, and a click sent by script, still write on every device. The touch sizes are 44 px at phone widths (640 px and below) and 36 px for touch above that: the tier boxes keep their drawn 24 px and only the invisible hit area grows (44 or 36 px high, 26 px wide so two neighbouring boxes never overlap; five boxes cannot be 44 px wide side by side in a card about 165 px wide), the House choice box is a 44 or 36 px hit area with the box drawn where it was, and the layout buttons, buttons, Inspector actions, Back to board, selects, filter chips, list sort buttons and House name buttons reach the floor. The headless sweep `scripts/lcc-m7-sweep.mjs` measures the hit area (extensions included) at 320, 390, 640 and 768 px, takes the 44 px or 36 px floor by width, fails on a control under it or on overlapping tier boxes, and taps a tier box with a real touch event and a real mouse click to prove the difference. In the Term and Decrees section a jump to it, or the focus on its heading, now stops below the sticky header and the warning bar (`scroll-margin-top`, #905). Items of #905 that no longer apply: the Sysselraad Strategy outline and its order chips are gone (#958, #967), the Decree Preview select is gone (#923), and the Earlier terms table already scrolls by keyboard with a visible hint and matrix row T8 already says the pool keeps the order of the save.
- **Landsraad: removing a tier takes back the rewards of the removed tier that are not collected yet** (fixes #944; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; requested by the operator on 2026-11-18; not checked in the game). Before, removing a tier (the tier box, Set tier with Apply tier, or any request with `lower: true`) lowered the contribution and left the reward rows the tier had created waiting at the House representative, so the reward still read "Not collected" for a tier that was no longer reached. Now the same edit also takes back, for the character's own rows of that House only, the waiting reward of each removed tier (`landsraad_house_rewards`, amount above 0, written in this term): the amount the tier paid comes off the row and the row is deleted at 0. One rule, `planTakeBack` in the new `internal/ops/landsraad_takeback.go`, is used by the write and by the board's read (each reached tier carries `takeback` and `takeback_why`), so the question before the click and the write cannot disagree. A reward is taken back only when the editor's own record says the editor paid that tier and the reward can be tied to the removed tier with certainty; a collected reward stays and is counted; a waiting reward the character's own missions in the game cover stays and is counted apart; any other reward that stays is counted as "left alone" with its reason (the same item is also the reward of another tier, less waits than the tier paid, the row is of an earlier term, or the editor has no record of paying the tier). Rewards are counted per row, not per tier. The record vouches for a range of ground, so it also carries where the editor started paying on the House (an optional `floor` field of the entry, merged by the lowest value): a reward is taken back only when every tier that gives it lies above that floor, so a reward the game left at a tier the editor never paid stays; a record without a floor (one an older build wrote) vouches for no tier, so every waiting reward stays until the next term. An older build ignores `back` and `floor` and drops them when it next rewrites the record, so after a round trip with an older build a raise can pay a mission once more. The House swatch (top tier) also needs the character's one-time row of this term, which goes with it so the swatch can be won again. Nothing else is written: no wallet, no specialization XP, no faction progress, no progress event or cursor (#655). What the removal paid stays paid and is never paid twice: the removal records, in the editor's own paid-ground file (version 1, a new optional `back` field), the tier it took rewards back up to, so a later raise to the same ground writes the reward rows again and pays no mission, no Solari, no House Scrip, no XP and no faction progress, however often the tier is removed and raised. The page asks before a removal that takes back or names a reward and says exactly which; the answer carries `rewards_taken_back`, `rewards_kept_collected`, `rewards_kept_unsure`, `rewards_kept_earned` and `swatches_taken_back`; the board read carries `my_paid_rows` beside `my_paid`; the line under the highest reached tier box comes from the server's outcome for the tier. The sentence "takes back nothing the tier paid" and the sentence that clicking the highest tier twice pays once are replaced everywhere (the tier tip, the question, the result, the toast, the Landsraad About text, the Help page Fill, Complete and tiers, the design notes). The request-equivalence golden of the board (`web-tests/lcc-board-requests.golden.json`) is unchanged. The reward rows and the tasks of the synthetic Command Center fixture gain the new keys (`takeback`, `takeback_why`, `my_paid_rows`).
- **Command Center banners: the numbers before the names in TOP CONTRIBUTORS are gone** (fixes #992; display only: nothing is written and no request, field or Go code changed, the request golden files are unchanged; requested by the operator on 2026-11-18, to be tested; not checked in the game). A banner line began with "1.", "2.", "3." drawn by a style rule, which read as a score and was misleading when several contributors had the same amount (three lines all showing 1,000). The list now shows the name and then its amount, with no number and no space kept for one. The order is the same (biggest first, equal amounts by name), a long name still wraps whole and is never cut, the amount stays on screen and the full name is still in its mouse-over text. The test checks that no counter, hanging indent or drawn mark is left in the style sheet.
- **Command Center plaque: one gap between every two neighbouring seats, and the seat pictures are no longer cut by their own box** (fixes #964, #965; display only: no save write, no request body and no Go code changed, the request golden files are unchanged). The 25 seats were spaced by their centres, 52 units apart, but the gold ring of the 13th seat reaches 33 units from its centre, so it touched or overlapped its two neighbours (the right one is drawn over it, which looked like a ring cut off on the right) while every other gap was 12 units: measured edge to edge in a headless Edge render at 1600, 1280, 1024, 768, 390 and 320 px wide the gaps differed by up to 9.6 px. The seats are now spaced by their outer edges: every gap is 11 units (the other seats are 51 apart, the two beside the gold seat 64), all 24 gaps are equal to the hundredth of a pixel at the six widths for 0 to 12 won seats a side and for a 13-House win, a won seat takes no more room than an open one, the won ring is drawn inward so it has the same outer edge as the still ring, and the faction pictures on the seats are not clipped to their own box. The two ends stay inside the track and are inset by the same amount. A new test (web-tests/lcc-seat-gaps.test.mjs) reads every circle and picture of every seat; the old test that required equal centre spacing is replaced. The clipped right edge itself was not reproduced in a headless render (see the pull request); checked in a headless render only, not in the game.
- **Term and Decrees: the Reigning faction drop-down no longer offers none, and the save write refuses a decree with no reigning faction** (fixes #960; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not checked in the game). In the game a decree is only in force because a faction won the term, but the Reigning faction drop-down offered none and Apply could write a decree with no faction. The drop-down, in the Command Center section and in the classic panel, now offers Atreides and Harkonnen only. While no faction is selected (a character in neither faction and a term with no winner) it shows a greyed Choose a faction that cannot be picked, and Apply says Choose a reigning faction first and sends nothing. The Active decree drop-down keeps none, which clears the decree and keeps the faction. On the server the term write (SetTerm) is judged on the row as the edit would leave it and refuses, as a bad argument with the reason, any request that names the decree or the faction and would leave a decree set with no faction (an empty string, null, or a decree sent alone for a term that has no faction); clearing the decree and the faction together, clearing the faction of a term with no decree, and a request for the end time or the elected decree only are still accepted, so a save the game wrote with a decree and no faction can still have its end time changed. The one recorded request that used the removed option, Apply with no decree and no faction, is now a recorded refusal (nothing is sent) and Apply with no decree and the faction kept is recorded beside it; every other recorded request is unchanged. The existing test that cleared the faction under a set decree now clears both. Two cases need no choice and are not refused: the save holds a faction the list does not offer and the drop-down is left alone (the decree is changed and that faction stays), and none for the decree on a term that has no faction (the request names the decree only, so a save the game wrote with a decree and no faction can still have its decree cleared). The placeholder help text, the About text and the mock-up note say so.
- **Command Center plaque: the decree on the plaque follows the term's active decree after Apply, Make active and a refresh, and a draft says it is not applied yet** (fixes #925; display and redraw only: no save write, no request body and no Go code changed, the request golden files are unchanged and the classic list is untouched). A decree looked at in the Decree Preview drop-down stayed on the plaque after the term's own active decree was changed, because the preview was kept over every redraw and the page also put the drop-down back to the sample; the plaque then showed the sample and not the saved decree. The preview is now let go as soon as the term's active decree is another one than at the last drawing, and the drop-down follows the board's own state. A short line under the Active decree drop-down of the Term and Decrees section now says "Not applied yet" and names the decree the term still has and the one Apply would set, until the choice is written or put back. Tests: `web-tests/lcc-plaque-follows-decree.test.mjs` (Apply, Make active, a redraw in between, the preview, the hint).
- **Command Center plaque: the headline says "Majority at 13 of 25 Houses" and no longer reads as 13 seats won** (fixes #912; display only: no save write, request or Go code changed). The old line, "Voting Session - Seat 13 of 25", sat above a tally of Atreides 1 and Harkonnen 0 and looked like a count; the 13 is the gold middle seat, the majority mark. The line under it (who leads, how many Houses are open) is unchanged, the screen-reader label for the track says the same thing, the plate behind the line is wider so the longer line stays on one line on a wide board and wraps in balance on a narrow one, and no text goes below 12 px.
- **Command Center board: readable light-theme text, an emblem on the phone victory panel and folded notes in the board's colours** (fixes #885, #886, refs #888 (its first item only); display only: stylesheets, a test and the design note, no markup, no save write and nothing a click sends changed; not checked in the game). (1) In the light theme 72 pieces of 12 px text on the cards and in the Actions panel were under 4.5:1 against the darkest end of their surface (the owner line of an Atreides or Harkonnen card 3.90 and 3.41, the collected, waiting and no-record counts, the Reopen button 3.51, the ACTIONS heading 4.39, the House Harkonnen faction label 4.07, the collected mark 4.38). Three text tokens now stand for the teal, the red and the gold as text: darker than the accents on the light surfaces, the same on the dark ones (the red a little lighter, so the Reopen button also passes in dark, 4.12 to 5.18), while borders and fills keep the accents. Every listed pair now measures 5.0 or more; a new test computes the ratios from the tokens and fails below 4.5. Two House nameplates of the plaque, which were not in the list, still measure under 4.5 at their darkest end and stay open. (2) On a phone (640 px and below, or a coarse pointer up to 900 px) the victory panel hid its diamond emblem, so the AWAITING VOTING SESSION tag touched the SYSSELRAAD heading; the emblem is now half size, which gives a gap of 44 px. (3) The four folded notes of the House Intelligence panel were drawn in the browser's link blue; they now use the board's gold text with a drawn triangle that turns when open, an underline on hover, the board's focus ring and the system text colour in forced colours. The design note's matrix is corrected where it was stale (S9 and S10 are closed disclosures, B5 has no crest on a card at 640 px and below, B13 is built) and has new rows for this work. The other items of #888 are not part of this change.
- **Vehicles and Bases: Details and Open no longer send the list that was clicked in back to its top** (fixes #824, refs #706, #707; page only, no save write). Since #707 a click that shows other rows in a table box (Details on a vehicle, Open on a base, a base tab or inventory group, an inventory group) dropped the scroll offset of every box, including the list that holds the button just pressed, whose rows had not changed. In a long list the person was thrown back to the top and the focused button was hidden inside the box. The box that holds the pressed control now keeps its offset; the boxes that show other rows still start at their top.
- **Go to a place: a trading post with no known name is "Unnamed trading post", and the numbers and keys of the places are in a tooltip, not in the name** (fixes the second part of #618, refs #500, #519; display only, read-only: no save write and nothing a move writes changes; the Landsraad and Specialization parts of #618 are not in this entry). A trading post whose key the editor has no name for was listed as `TradingPost (key 00271320...)` or `TradingPost (marker 5)`, and a base, a vehicle and a respawn point carried the game's number (`Sandbike #1234`). The answer of the list now carries a name to show (`display`) and the ids (`detail`), and the page shows the name and puts the ids in the tooltip of the entry and of the "Selected" line. Two places of a heading with one name are numbered `(1 of 2)`. The `label` of a place is unchanged: the page keeps a chosen place by its heading and label, and a move sends only the three coordinates, so no label is ever sent back. The filter matches the shown name as well as the label.
- **Go to a place: a hub the character stands in is no longer counted as a place of another map that is listed** (fixes #825, refs #607, #764; page and list only, read-only: no save write and no change to what Teleport sends). "N places on <map>" and the headings were decided with `otherMap`, which says whether a hub is another map than the one the character is on, but the count and the grouping are about the map chosen in the Map box. With the character in a hub (Arrakeen or Harko Village) and Hagga Basin or the Deep Desert chosen, that hub was counted as a place of the chosen map and listed under Taxis, one place too many and under the wrong heading. The list now also says, for each place, whether it is off the map listed (`offList`), and the page counts, groups and orders on that: both hubs are counted apart and listed under Other maps, and the hub the character stands in keeps its distance. `otherMap` is unchanged and still decides the warning about a move between maps and the missing distance. The cap on a long list keeps every place that is off the list, as it kept the hubs before. Tests: the list from a character in a hub with each other map and with its own map (`offList` and `otherMap` per hub, order, cap), and the page (`web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`); both fail before the change.
- **Live-save gate: a slow preparation of the automatic write no longer keeps the edits from ever being written while the game is open** (fixes #821, refs #612, #779, #792; a SAVE WRITE PATH (it changes when a write is let through, not what is written), so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). With a game process present the gate holds a write until the editor has watched the save file unbroken for a minute, and a watch that was not asked again for more than 30 seconds starts over. A write keeps the save's lock across the hash of the file, the integrity check, the encode with its round trip, the backup and the temp file, and the one extra question #792 added after the encode only helped when those steps together stayed under 30 seconds: when they took longer on a large save, that question was itself the first one after the gap, the watch started over and the write was refused, on every attempt, so the queue stayed unwritten while any game process existed. The gate is now asked every ten seconds from a small timer for as long as the write runs, so the watch stays unbroken however long any one step takes. The answers of those questions are not used (they only keep the watch), and the gates that decide, before the backup and right before the rename, are asked as before, so nothing is written that the gate would not allow, and a session that starts meanwhile still refuses before a backup is taken. Tests: a write whose preparation is stalled for over three watch gaps (on a clock that runs faster than the real one) is written with the timer on, and, with the timer off, is refused by the watch with no backup taken and the edit still queued.
- **Saved map: the character is drawn from the body's row again when the controller's row gives no place** (fixes #822, refs #699, #723; display only, no save write). Since #723 the map read the character's place through the shared reader and kept the body's row only for a failed read, so a save whose controller row was missing or had no map, with the body's row whole, drew no character on any map. The body's row on the map shown is now used whenever the reader gives no answer, as the summary does.
- **Live-save gate: with only the automatic pass's edit queued, the page, Check again and the write now give the same answer** (fixes #819, refs #612, #774, #792; the status the page shows only: what is written, and when, is unchanged, so not a save write path change). A queue holding only the automatic pass's edit is written under the unattended rule (it waits for every game process), but the status the page polls, the overview and Check again still asked the rule of an edit made by hand. With the game open at its menu the banner said edits are written at once, and every write (the autosave tick, Write now) was refused with the sentence that the pass runs without anyone watching. The status and Check again now ask the rule the queue will be written under (Save.GateState): a pass-only queue shows as blocked with the reason the write gives, and a queue with an edit made by hand, or an empty one, keeps the rule of a hand edit. A test queues the pass's edit with the game at its menu and checks the status, Check again, the refused write and the hand edit that turns it into a hand queue.
- **Landsraad board: a save whose map markers cannot be read still gives the board, the term and the decrees** (fixes #820, refs #744, #761, #639; a read only, no save write and no change to what any write contains). The board asks the save's map markers for the places of the house representatives (the pin, the area and the distance), and a failure of that one optional query failed the whole read, so a save whose markers table lacked a column the query names, or had no usable markers table, left the board, the term panel, the decrees and with them every Landsraad write from the page unavailable. The read now carries on with the fixed place of each house (the same places a house with no marker already gets) and answers with `locations_from_markers: false`, so the page can say that the places are the fixed ones and not the save's; with readable markers the field is `true` and everything is as before. The other Landsraad queries (the tasks, the rewards, the decrees) still fail the read when they fail, as they are not optional. Tests, on both layouts of the markers: a save with no markers table, one without the `area_id` column and one without the `marker_type` column (renamed) each read, with the term, the decrees and every house at its fixed place (a house with no fixed place stays unknown), and a save with readable markers says so.
- **Repair every base to max no longer raises the pieces and placeables of a base backup** (fixes #815, refs #613, #770; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). Since #769 the repair target of a type comes only from standing pieces of your own bases, and backups (the picked-up state) are left out of it, but the save-wide write still raised every piece and placeable of the save to that target, so an imported base backup you keep to place later was changed too, without being listed anywhere. The two updates now leave the pieces and placeables whose actor is a backup as they are, as they already leave a placeable hologram. Repair this base to max is unchanged (it writes only the pieces of the base asked for). The tip of Repair every base now says that the pieces and placeables of a base backup are left as they are. A new test checks the health of a backup's pieces and placeables after both repairs.
- **Create a vehicle: a redraw that fails no longer loses the typed text or leaves an unhandled error** (fixes #813, refs #738, #782; page only, no save write). The two redraws that keep what was typed in the item, Qty and fuel boxes (Create when the form changed after the preview, and the answer to a part picked in the preview table) put the text back only when the redraw succeeded and had no handler for a failed one, so a failure while the page was drawn again left the boxes empty and an unhandled promise rejection with no message on the page. Both now go through one helper that puts the text back whether the redraw succeeds or fails and says a failure in a toast. Test: `web-tests/vehiclecreate.test.mjs` makes the redraw fail in both places and checks the text, the toast and that no rejection is left; both fail on the page before.
- **Repair tips: both repair buttons now say that placeable holograms are left as they are** (fixes #812, refs #770, #786; page text only, no save write and no change to what a repair writes). Since #786 both Repair this base to max and Repair every base to max leave a placeable hologram (an unfinished placement) as it is, but the tip of each still said that placeables are raised, and the Structures "About this" text did not mention it. Both tips and the About text now say it. Test: `web-tests/uitext-bases.test.mjs` pins the sentence in both tips and in the About text; it fails on the page before.
- **Landsraad board: the record of the paid ground is no longer destroyed when it is of a newer version or damaged** (fixes #811, refs #777, #799; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). A record file of another version, of another save or not whole JSON was read as "nothing recorded", and the next write replaced it with a file holding only the new notes, so the record a newer build had written (after a downgrade), or a file damaged by hand, was lost with no copy, and every raise of the other houses then paid again. A record of a NEWER version is now never replaced: a record whose `version` is above 1, or is there and is not a whole number (text, a fraction, an exponent, a number too large to hold, null), counts as newer, whatever the size of the file (the version is read before the size is judged), and a raise over it is refused with nothing written (a write_failed answer that says why and what to do), the board still reads and a tier removal still goes through, and the file stays as it is. A record that is damaged, too large (with no version that can be read in its first 1 MiB), of an older format, without a version or of another save is moved aside (next to it, its name followed by `.damaged-` and the time, never over an earlier copy) before the new file is written, and the write is an error that keeps the notes if it cannot be moved. Tests: a newer record (same shape, changed shape, another save, a version as text, 2.0, 2e0, 1.5, a number too large to hold, a version above 1 in a file over 1 MiB) is not overwritten, stays byte for byte and a raise over it is refused, while the board read and a tier removal still work; fifteen kinds of unusable file (damaged, empty, no or negative version, byte order mark, trailing garbage, array, null, too large) are kept aside with their content and the new file holds the new note; two moves in the same instant both stay; a raise over a damaged file writes and keeps the damaged file; a damaged file that cannot be moved aside is a write error that keeps the notes; the version tests fail on the code before. docs/save-database.md says so.
- **Landsraad board: a raise after a failed write of the paid-ground record no longer pays the same ground again** (fixes #810, refs #777, #799; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). When the editor's record of the ground it paid could not be written (another program holds the file, or the disk is full) after a write, the save kept the notes of the written edits for the next write, but a raise in between did not count them: it saw the old record and an empty queue, so a tier removal followed by the same raise paid the ground a second time (missions, Solari, House Scrip, specialization experience, reward rows, the faction's progress). A raise, and the board's read of what a click grants, now count the notes the record has not taken yet, before the queued ones, until a write hands them over. Past the bound of 1,000 kept notes the oldest were dropped without a word; the save now says so for the rest of the session, even after the record works again, and a raise is refused with nothing written (as a write_failed answer) while it does (a removal pays nothing and still goes through). A kept note that comes again after a restart only merges, in what a raise and the board count as it does in the record. Tests on a record that fails on write only: a raise after a removal pays nothing and changes no wallet, reward row or progress, a higher raise pays only the new tiers, the kept note is recorded at the next good write, and the refusal after a dropped note. What a raise pays when the record is whole is unchanged. A restart still loses the notes the record had not taken (#799).
- **Landsraad board: a bulk question no longer writes houses that a filter or a jump has unticked** (fixes #818; page script only, a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; what a write contains is unchanged, only which houses a bulk Yes may include). "Complete N houses" and "Fill N houses" kept the list of houses they were asked for. A filter (a state tile or the House: Open or Taken choice) or Jump to house hides rows and unticks them, the line under the board then named the smaller choice, and the question stayed open: its Yes wrote every stored house, including the ones the page showed as not chosen. Now the open bulk question is closed when the choice loses a house it named (the status line says so and that nothing was written), and, as a second guard, the answer writes only the houses that are still chosen: if the choice shrank in any other way it asks again with the new count, as it already did for a house that changed. A question about one clicked house names that house itself and is not affected. Web tests ask for all, apply a filter or a jump, and check that the question is gone, that it asks again when the closing is switched off, and that no house outside the choice is sent.
- **Landsraad board: a click on a house's place goes to the Saved map in one step, and a place with no map picture says so instead of opening an unexplained window** (fixes #807, refs #758, #639; page text and behaviour only, read-only: no save write and no change to what the page writes). Where the Saved map has a picture of the place, the click now opens the Saved map centred on the house. Where it has none (Harko Village, Arrakeen, a house with no place yet) the place is shown muted, its tooltip and accessible name say why ("The Saved map has no picture of Harko Village yet."), and a click only repeats that in the status line. The map window with its outline, its "From you: not worked out" line and the small "Open in Saved map" link is removed: the hover peek and the opened row show the same lines, and the distance line now reads "not known (your character is on another map, or has no saved position)".
- **Landsraad board: the leftovers of the review of the mission grant** (fixes #752, #753, #754, #755, #762; refs #652, #745; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game; what a raise pays is unchanged). (1) The write log (and the list of pending changes) now names what the missions wrote: `landsraad Kenola: your contribution 1300 -> 6000, completed, its faction reached the goal; 2 missions: +12,000 Solari, +2,000 House Scrip, +6,000 Gathering XP; 4 reward rows`, for one house and for several (the totals, with one XP total). The write log cuts a description at 200 characters, so the wins taken away, the times moved and the experience that did not fit come before the amounts, and the amounts and the list of houses are what the cut takes. A raise that paid nothing names no amounts. (2) A specialization track at its last level is no longer silent: the experience that did not fit is in the answer (`specialization_xp_not_added`, by line), in the write log and in the result line and the toast ("Sabotage is at its last level, +N XP not added"). The line before a click no longer promises more experience than the track has room for (the board read carries the room of each track; two houses of one line share it). (3) The settings file is read as the game reads it, without regard to the case of the section and the key (`landsraadcontributionmultiplier=2.0` was taken for "key absent" and counted one mission too few per step); a file that cannot be read now says "ServerCustomSettings.ini could not be read, so the Landsraad amounts cannot be worked out. Nothing was changed." instead of advice to correct a value, a doubled full stop and the folder of this computer; a multiplier of 0 and one near 0 stay refused and are pinned through the route. The About text says the amounts come from the settings file on this computer. The refusal for too many missions says "for one house". (4) A raise or a tier removal on a house that was taken or reopened since the page was drawn, or whose earlier progress (the ground the character's own events cover) is not what the page showed, is refused with nothing written (before, only Complete and Reopen were checked); a request that does not say what the page showed is not refused for it. The unknown-house and track-number refusals now apply only when a mission with experience is written, so a raise inside one mission step, or with an experience multiplier that rounds to nothing, is not refused for what it would not write. (5) Texts: the two swatch tables of the newer layout are listed as read and written in `docs/save-database.md`; the House Scrip and the experience are given as what a mission with a Recollection turned in pays (whether a mission without one pays them is not known, #745) in the About text, the code comment, the docs and the earlier changelog entry; the earlier entry no longer says "fixes" for the pull request number, names the behaviour change (a plain tier click now raises the faction's progress and can take the house) and says that the grant counts ground raised from now on (a house raised by an earlier version already has events that cover its tiers). Decisions: events of the character count as ground under any faction (a change of faction does not let a Fill pay again); Fill faction goal on a house another faction took retakes it and names the win it takes away; the cursor row (`landsraad_task_progress_processed`) is neither read nor written since #776, so its table row stays "Not written". Tests: write log and answer, the track at its last level and two houses of one line, five more steps with an injected failure (the faction row on insert and on update, the completion, the House Scrip wallet, the clock repair), a check that no progress table is touched, the state and events checks, the second character, the faction change, a contribution not on a step, a multiplier of 0 and near 0, a covered raise with a setting it does not need, the settings file in another case and one that cannot be read, and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board-write.test.mjs`).
- **Landsraad board: Fill faction goal counts a house once and never leaves the faction lower than a plain Fill** (fixes #751; refs #652, #776, #798; tests and a simplification: what is written is unchanged, and in one state the answer now reports the house as completed (below); a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged). Both differences the review of #652 found were already corrected in the code that was merged with it (the house a raise takes is counted once, and the faction gets what the character gained, at least the goal, as a plain Fill does); nothing pinned the cases around them. New tests: a house another faction had taken (the faction goes to the goal and not above it, the answer says one house completed, a faction already above the goal is not lowered) and ground the character's own events already cover (nothing is paid again, the house is taken once, the faction is at the goal at least, never lower than a plain Fill). One branch of the faction's share, for a house that is not open, repeated what the completion does and was removed; what is written is the same, but for a house already taken for your own faction with its progress below the goal, Fill faction goal now answers `completed: 1` and the write log says "completed for" the faction (as Complete alone does), where it answered `completed: 0` before; a test pins that state. The safest choice stands: never count twice, never lower; the editor-owned record of paid ground (#798) and the guards of #776 (no progress event, the game's cursor is not touched) are unchanged.
- **Game log unreadable: the queue reason no longer quotes the folder of the log, and its test no longer fails on a random number** (fixes #567; a reason text and a test, no save write path, no change to what is written). The reason of an edit that is queued because the game's log cannot be read quoted the whole error, which holds the full folder of the log (and so the name of the account on this computer); the same went for the reason of a failed process check. Both now go through the same cut as the other messages of the editor: the folders go, the file names stay. `TestUnreadableLogQueuesTheEdit` looked for "256" in that reason, so it failed whenever the random folder of a test run held those digits; it now looks for the wording of a size and for the folder itself. New tests build a folder whose name holds "256" and "larger", check that the cut of the reason keeps the file name and a whole character, and check the reason of a failed process check.
- **Landsraad board: the editor remembers the ground it paid, so a tier removal followed by the same raise no longer pays again** (fixes #777, refs #655, #776; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). Since #776 the editor writes no progress event, so nothing remembered its own raise: removing a tier and raising it again paid the missions again (Solari, House Scrip, specialization XP), delivered the reward rows of the tiers crossed again, added the same ground to the faction's progress again while the house was open (a house could be taken by toggling one tier), and the tier box, being a toggle, paid twice on two clicks of the highest tier. The editor now records the ground it paid in a small file of its own, `landsraad-paid-<hash of the save path>.json` in its own folder next to the pending-edits journal, **outside the game's save and the game's tables**. Per term, house, faction and character it keeps the highest contribution whose ground the editor paid. The file is written (a durable replace, the same helper the settings use) only after the edit that paid it has been written to the game's file; the note travels with the edit through the queue, the journal and a put-back onto a newer file, and goes with it when the queue is discarded. A raise now pays only the ground above the highest of the contribution before, the character's own events, the highest contribution under another faction and this record (the queued raises that are not written yet count too); removing a tier does not clear the record. A raise above the record pays only the new tiers. The answer of the write and the line shown before the click say how many missions were not paid because that ground was paid before (`ground_paid_before` and `missions_paid_before` in the answer). A record that is missing, not readable, not of this format, too large or of another save is read as nothing paid, is never changed by a read, and leaves the guards of #776 as they were. The record counts only as far as the save shows it: it is capped at the highest tier whose reward row of this term stands (through the house swatch for the top tier), so a save put back from a backup, or a reward row the game wrote for one tier, does not make the editor withhold ground it never paid in that save; when that cap cut the record and the raise pays, the raise replaces the record instead of being merged into it, and a restored backup is not signalled: the restored file's own rows decide how far the record counts, so undoing a restore, or restoring a backup taken after an earlier paying write, keeps the record that file still holds. The term is part of the key, so a write in another term (a backup of an earlier term put back and raised once) leaves this term's record alone; only the bound of 4,096 entries evicts, oldest terms first and never the term being written. Entries are checked when read, and the ground is held to the largest tier. A file that cannot be read this time (another program holds it) is tried again once after 50 ms and then left as it is, with the note kept in memory for the next write and a warning in the answer and in the banner, never rebuilt from the notes alone; a raise that finds the record unreadable is refused with nothing written and a message that says so (a tier removal, which pays nothing, does not read it and still goes through); notes of a write finished at the next start are delivered too, as repeats that only merge, so the first part of a batch cannot lower what the whole batch recorded. The record is read once per request and once per board read. The line that says missions were not paid now reads "the save shows that ground as already paid", and the text of a failed write no longer carries the folders of a failed rename (both paths of a rename, and any path quoted in a wrapped error, are cut to the file names). **Known limits:** a raise made on a restored older file replaces and lowers the record, and the later file then pays some ground again; the same item at two tiers can make a stale record look supported (a raise pays less, never more); a queue discarded after a write that could not be verified loses its note (the guards of #776 apply, as without the record). **In-game check still open: confirm the game keeps a collected reward row for the whole term** (the cap relies on it). Also: the count of rewards waiting in the line before a click no longer counts the top tier when its house swatch was already won in this term (the write skips it). Tests on temporary databases: the record is written only after the file is written and not for a discarded or refused edit, a removal and the same raise pay nothing again (wallets, reward rows, the faction's progress) and say so, a higher raise pays only the new tiers, a repeated click on the highest tier pays once, a damaged, empty, oversized, foreign or wrong-version file is ignored and left alone, a restored backup is not blamed for a raise it never had (a put-back save raised in two steps, a game-written tier row with a put-back editor record, undoing an editor restore keeps the record, and the backup from before a second house's write keeps the first house's), the cap reads the same in the write and on the board and at both edges of the term's start, an unreadable record refuses a raise with nothing written, a note that comes again never lowers the record, a sink that fails leaves a warning that outlives the write, the kept notes are bounded with the newest kept, a file that cannot be read is retried and never rebuilt, a term going backwards or an implausible entry does not stop recording, the record is read once, the notes survive the journal and a put-back and are dropped with a discarded queue, and the page counts the ground and the swatch as the write does. Five tests that stated the old behaviour (a repeat after a removal pays again) now state the new one.
- **Regions card: the game's "no area" (area id 0) is no longer counted as "1 more without a name"** (fixes #788; page text of a read only, no save write). On the Hagga Basin map the card read "12 of 12 discovered" and then "and 1 more without a name", where the extra one was area id 0, which the game uses for no area at all: it has no name and no place on the map. The read of the character's discovered areas now leaves id 0 out; a real id that has no name is still counted, and every named region is listed as before (the total is still the 12 named regions). Tests on a temporary save: a discovered row with area 0 alone, with a named area, with an unnamed real id, and an unnamed real id alone.
- **Create a vehicle: a repeat whose check cannot read the save is refused instead of written** (fixes #790; a save write path, a fail-safe change, no change to what a Create writes). When a Create came again with the same form id, the editor looks up whether the vehicle of the first answer is still in the working copy. If that read failed (a timeout, a locked or unreadable file) the failure counted as "the vehicle is gone", so a retry of a Create whose answer was lost could write a second vehicle. Now a read that failed refuses the repeat with a short answer (the check could not read the save, nothing was written), keeps the first answer and writes nothing; the same retry is answered with the first vehicle once the save can be read. Only a read that worked and did not find the vehicle makes the repeat a new request. The prune after a Discard or a reload keeps an answer when its read failed, as before, and is now pinned by a test.
- **Live-save gate: leftovers from the review of #774** (refs #779; a save write path, one extra question to the gate, the rest tests, wording and docs). The automatic write now asks the game check again after the encode and before its backup, so a write that is refused there (a session began while the file was being prepared) takes no backup of its own and cannot push older automatic restore points out of the 60 kept, and the watch of the file stays continuous when the encode, and the backup with the temp file, each take less than 30 seconds (if the encode alone takes longer, the new question restarts the watch and refuses before the backup, which is safe). Two checks of the watch that read the clock a moment apart and take the lock in the other order no longer start the watch over (a reading less than a second before the last one counts as the last one; a real step back still does). The tests now prove what they claimed: a file whose time changes to another old time is held by the watch itself, the close and reopen step keeps the time unchanged, and the watch's length as shipped is asserted to be one minute. Wording and docs: the check's message that said the save file has not changed for a long time now also names the editor's own write, the header comment of the session rule names the file's time, the README and SECURITY.md say that the automatic pass at launch also waits for every game process (and that a pass started by hand does not), a by-hand pass is logged as `run by hand: automatic pass: ...`, and a pass edit joined by a hand edit is written under the hand gate, also by a later tick of the autosave, which is accepted and now stated.
- **Create a vehicle: the repeat memory is kept unless a vehicle is gone, and a refused Create keeps its answer** (fixes #760; a save write path, three follow-ups of the double-click fix #740, no change to what a Create writes). (1) A retry of a form whose queued Create was thrown away already wrote again when another form had taken the same vehicle id since; that is now pinned by a test for every route that reads the file again. A retry could still be answered with another vehicle that has the same id, because vehicle ids are reused: when a vehicle written by Create a vehicle (with or without a form id) or by Duplicate vehicle takes an id, an answer that names it is dropped, and an answer is only used while the vehicle it made is still there (the same id, class and entities, not just the same id, so a vehicle the game wrote under that id is not taken for it). (2) The memory used to be emptied by every reload, so a retry after a lost answer that came right after a reload that dropped nothing (the automatic refresh with nothing queued, a Discard with nothing queued, a Reload now that put every edit back, a refused Reload now or Restore) wrote a second vehicle. Now a Discard that threw queued edits away and a Reload now that could not put every edit back drop only the answers whose vehicle is no longer there, so a Create that was put back or is already in the file keeps its answer; a Restore that succeeded, which replaces the file, empties the memory. (3) A Create refused because the queue is held no longer forgets the answer of the Create the held queue still has.
- **Repair every base no longer raises placeable holograms** (refs #770; a save write path, one filter added to the statement). Repair this base already left a placeable hologram as it is; Repair every base wrote the repair target onto it, so on one measured save 23 of the 37 placeables it raised were holograms going from 0 to the built health. Repair every base now has the same filter, and both buttons are tested with holograms (of a type with and without a target) as well as with the cap rules of #613. Also: the comment on the real placeable health figure (20,000, not 2,500) and two older design notes now describe the repair target as it is since #613. What a repair of real pieces and placeables writes is unchanged.
- **Create a vehicle: a pick in the preview table no longer wipes what was typed meanwhile** (fixes #738; page only, no save write). The answer to a pick drew the whole card again from the draft, so text typed in "Items for the cargo hold" (the item box and its Qty), or a fuel or quantity typed while the request was on its way, was gone when the answer landed. Now the fuel and the quantities go into the draft first (Create then sees that the preview was asked for another figure and asks for a new one, as it does for any later edit), the item and Qty boxes and the text of any box that was changed are put back after the redraw, and the caret stays where it was in the box being typed in (keys typed while the page is being drawn are kept too, and a number box goes on at its end). A refused pick keeps the typed text too, and so does Create when it finds that the form changed after the preview: the item and Qty boxes are no longer emptied by that redraw. Web tests added (typing during a held request, a refused pick, untouched boxes).
- **Base storage: deleting an item no longer closes the container that is open** (fixes #731; page only, no save write). The container opened with Open in a base's Inventory list was drawn by that button alone, so every redraw of the page (after a delete, or the page refreshing itself from the file) left the area empty and the container had to be opened again. The open container is now kept (which one, in the page), and drawn again from the save together with the rest of the page (it is built before the page is drawn, so it is never shown empty for a moment), so several items can be deleted in a row and the typed text and table offset of its boxes stay. After a delete the keyboard focus goes to the Delete button of the row that took the place of the deleted one (the last row, or the Add item box when none is left), and each of those Delete buttons now names its item for a screen reader ("Delete Spice Melange"). Moving to another base, sub-tab, group or page still starts with the container closed. Web tests added.
- **Public text: the leftovers in old issues and pull requests are edited, and the check over issue, pull request and comment text has a page** (fixes #653, audit F1 groups G-U1 and G-U3; docs and a test only, no change to the editor and no new workflow). The check itself (`public-text.yml`, `public-text-discussion.yml`, `public-text-push.yml`, `cmd/publictextcheck`) was merged earlier (#676, #710, #711, #722). What remained: eight issue and pull request bodies and one comment named another project of the owner's in words the rule keeps out of public text, and one issue held a folder path under a personal profile. They were edited in place with the owner's approval (2026-11-15) to neutral wording, and a scan of every issue, pull request, comment and review now finds nothing but the AI attribution line in the bodies of nine old merged pull requests (left as they are: the text became squash messages long ago). GitHub keeps the earlier text in the edit history of each item. New page `docs/public-text-check.md` says what each workflow reads, how the text reaches the program (the payload file, never a shell expression), how to scan old text and add a word, and the one step left to do by hand: adding `public-text` to the required checks of the branch rule for `main`. A new test keeps the page naming every public-text workflow and check.
- **The write gate no longer rests on the text of the game's log alone, and the automatic refill at launch waits for the game to close again** (refs #612, audit D2-1, D2-2, D2-3 and G-007; a SAVE WRITE GATE, so reviewed before it is merged; tests only, not checked in the game). While a game process exists, the editor decided "a world is loaded" only from marker lines in the game's log; if a game update reworded them the status came out empty and an edit was written while a world was loaded, which the game overwrites about every 20 seconds. Now: (1) the save file's own modification time is a second signal that depends on no text: it must be older than the wait the log asks for (60 seconds after a clean exit, 3 minutes otherwise), except for the file the editor itself just installed (size and time are remembered right after the rename and dropped when the read-back does not confirm them); a `-wal` or `-journal` file next to the save counts too; a time that cannot be read, or lies in the future, is not quiet; (2) a game process with a readable log that holds no session line at all is unknown, not "at the menu": the file must have been unchanged for 10 minutes first; (3) the wait after the last write goes from 23 to 60 seconds (23 was 0.3 seconds above the largest gap between writes seen), and the short wait after an end marker is used only when exactly one write follows it, as in the 19 exits measured (a second write means the marker was not the end of the world; the 3-minute and 6-minute rules apply); (4) an end marker (a browse line, the exit line, the closing of the log, the delayed close) ends a session only at the start of a log line's text, as the game writes it, so a line that merely contains the words does not (starts and the write line keep their wide reading); (5) the quiet is observed: the first check, one after a gap of more than 30 seconds (a sleep, a clock set forward or back) or after the file's time changed other than by the editor's own write holds for a minute while the file is watched, so an old file time after a sleep is not taken for a long quiet; (6) a write let through while the log said nothing at all is reported as "unknown", not "at its menu"; (7) the automatic refill at launch, and its retries, wait for every game process to be gone, whatever the log and the file say: nobody watches that write, and #499 had removed this rule and left the log as its only signal. The edit the pass makes carries that rule with it (it is named for the gate): if the pass's own write is refused it stays queued and neither the next tick of the autosave nor a restart that restores it from the journal writes it while a game process exists; an edit made by hand in the same queue makes it a hand queue. **Run the automatic pass now** has a person looking and keeps the gate of an edit made by hand. Edits made by hand with the game at its menu are still written once the waits pass, so the visible change is a wait of about a minute instead of 30 seconds after leaving the world, and the refill at launch running only after the game is closed. Not known, and not guessed at, so the issue stays open for them (`docs/design/2026-11-15-live-save-gate.md`): how often the save file's time moves while a world is loaded, and what the log holds when the game is started at its menu and no world is loaded; the margins are chosen so that a wrong answer waits longer. Tests on synthetic log lines and temp files: the reworded log with a young and an old file, the old end with a young file, the editor's own write and a write by something else, the wal file, the file that cannot be read, two writes after an end, the strict browse lines, the pass with the game open and closed and asked for by hand, the pinned tracker kept as it was. UAT: with the game at its menu, open the editor with the refill on and see it wait with "the game is open"; close the game and see it run; leave a world and press an edit: it is written about a minute later.
- **Landsraad board: a raise writes no progress event and leaves the game's progress cursor alone** (fixes #655, refs #609, #652, #656, #772, #777; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). A tier click, Fill, Fill faction goal and Fill chosen used to add a row to the game's progress queue (one event with its link to the character, placed above the game's cursor, and the cursor moved to it). What the game does with an event it has not processed is not known, and an event in a shape it never writes could be applied a second time (the contribution added again, a reward handed out again). The editor now writes the rows it can stand behind and nothing in that queue: the character's contribution, the faction's contribution and the completion it brings (`landsraad_tasks`), the reward rows of the tiers crossed (and the one-time swatch row), the Solari and House Scrip of the missions and the specialization XP. At its next cursor step the game should find only events of its own; whether it then keeps a contribution raised here, or works it out again from its own events, is not checked in the game. Events an earlier build wrote stay in the save and still count as ground paid. A queued edit of the previous build (a journal of the old version) is set aside with the notice and not replayed, so it cannot write the event after all. **Reading a tier:** a reached tier now reads from its reward row of this term (waiting, delivered once collected, or reached with no delivery recorded), whatever the character's events say; the same item at two tiers cannot be told apart, and a contribution raised by a route that writes no reward can show a tier as delivered when the game or another tier left a row of this term (#772). **What changes for you:** the editor leaves no event of its own, so it does not remember what it paid once the contribution is lowered: a tier removal followed by the same raise pays the removed tiers again (the missions: Solari, House Scrip and specialization XP; the reward rows of the tiers crossed, where the top tier's swatch is not given twice when it was won in this term, but where the save has no swatch tables, or the swatch was held from an earlier term, the top tier's own item is delivered again), and, while the house is open, adds the same ground to your faction's progress again, which can take the house; the tier box is a toggle, so clicking the highest reached tier twice pays twice. Ground the character's own events cover, and the highest contribution reached under another faction, is not paid again (so a change of faction does not make the same raise pay twice). The answer of the write no longer carries an `events` count, and the Tiers help text says all of this. Tests on temporary databases: a raise leaves the events, their links and the cursor as they were (with and without events of the game in the save), the reward rows and wallets are as before, a tier raised above the game's events reads by its row, a swatch won this term is not repeated, a faction change does not pay again, repeated removal and raise adds the faction progress again (stated), the top tier after a removal without swatch tables is delivered again (stated), and an old-version journal is set aside, not replayed.
- **Landsraad board: a tier reads as delivered only when your own events cover it, the same rule the write uses** (fixes #656, refs #609 and #652; the board's read side only, nothing new is written). **Superseded by #655:** the board no longer asks the events; a reward row of this term decides a reached tier, and the events only decide what a raise pays. The write already pays each tier of a raise its own item (#652): the same item at several tiers, such as Solari, pays at every tier and adds to a waiting row, a reward of an earlier term collected later does not block this term's tiers, and a tier is paid once. The board and the tier list still read a row of the same house and item as the delivery of any tier of that item, so a reward collected in this term for an earlier term, or the waiting amount of another tier, showed this term's tier as delivered or waiting, and a collected row read as not delivered in one field and delivered in the other.
