One rule, `tierState`, now gives both `reward_state` and `delivered`, for the item the tier shows (the swatch included): a reached tier is waiting or delivered only when the progress events of your character for the house cover its threshold and the item's row was written in this term (amount above 0: waiting; 0: collected); otherwise it reads no_row. Where your character has no event at all for the house (a house filled before #652, or a save without the progress tables) nothing can cover a tier, so a row of this term decides alone (waiting, or delivered when collected) and a row of an earlier term reads no_row. The About text of the board says so; remaining edges are on #772. The warning for rewards left out as already given is in the result line and the toast since #652 (#656 G-017). Tests: `internal/ops/landsraad_tiers_test.go` (Solari at several tiers, a reward of an earlier term waiting or collected, a house raised twice, a removal and the same raise, the board states after the raise and after the collection); the fixtures of the board and tier-flag tests got the character's events for Maros, because they stood for a delivered tier by its row alone (the Kenola cases got events too, so the character filter is tested again).
- **Landsraad writes name the houses and factions they touch, say what they take away and what they move, and the single Complete checks term and faction** (refs #611, delta audit group D1; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged). D1-1, D1-2 and D1-9 were fixed earlier (#628). D1-3: completing a house another faction already won now says so; the answer of the board apply (the page's write), Complete chosen, Fill faction goal and the single Complete names the faction whose win was replaced (`replaced`, `previous_winner`), and the result line on the page and the write log say it too. D1-4: a term's `end_time` earlier than its `start_time` is refused (nothing is written); the clock repair still puts every completed house of the term whose completion time lies outside the term's window back inside it, but the answer (`repaired`), the result line and the write log now say how many it moved, also when no house changed. D1-5: the single Complete (not used by the page) refuses a house of an earlier term, the faction None, an unknown faction, and, new, a save with no Landsraad term at all (not found; before it completed the house with an empty completion time); the single Reopen refuses an earlier term and a save with no term. Nothing is written in these cases. D1-6: the board apply (every click on the page), Fill faction goal, Complete chosen, Reopen chosen and the single Complete describe themselves with house and faction (for example "landsraad Kenola: completed for Harkonnen" or "landsraad: 3 houses changed from the board; houses: Kenola, Maros, Vernius"): the facts come first and the list of houses last, because the write log cuts a description at 200 characters; the queue list, the chip text and the held and restored banners show them in words. D1-8: the message of a held queue says to discard the queue and make the changes again. D1-7 is documentation only: `docs/save-database.md` says by route which writes read and write the one-time reward tables of the newer layout (only a raise through the board apply) and that how the game ties a completion to them is unverified. No other behavior changes. Tests on temporary copies for every changed write.
- **Repair base and Repair buildings take their target only from standing pieces of your own bases, and never above a cap per type** (refs #613, audit G-009 and G-021; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). The target of a piece type was the best health of that type anywhere in the save from 0 to 1,000,000, so the pieces of an imported backup (and any piece no base owns) could set it, and one wall of a type with a very high health was copied onto every wall of the type; the real figures are about 7,000 for a foundation and 20,000 for the largest placeable. Now only pieces that a base's totem owns and whose actor is not in the picked-up state count, placeable holograms do not, and the target is held to a cap: for a piece type seen on five or more pieces of a real save, 1.25 times the most seen on it; otherwise 8,750 for a building piece and 25,000 for a placeable. A normal repair gives the same target as before on that save (checked for all 70 piece types and 43 placeable types). Nothing is ever lowered, and the hover text of the two buttons and the Structures help say where the target comes from. The check on an imported file keeps its wide ceiling of 1,000,000, which is not lowered because one save is too few to say what the game's largest value is. Also: the durability check of an import no longer lets a bad durability through when the item's stats hold another key of another shape. Left open on #613: the shared limits package (G-045) and the other unchecked columns of G-021.
- **CI: the race test run gets 20 minutes per package, not go's default 10** (fixes #756; CI rule only, no change to the editor and no test dropped or skipped). The `go` job's race-and-coverage run of `internal/ops` took 412 to 592 seconds on the hosted runner (31 seconds without race and coverage; 50 seconds on a 16-core development machine, 935 tests that run one after the other, none above 3 seconds), so on a slow runner it reached the 10-minute limit and the job failed with "test timed out", and a rerun passed. Every test still runs under the race detector. The limit is per test binary: a hung test now ends the step after 20 minutes with every stack, inside the job's 30. (#756)
- **Landsraad tab: the tier boxes are off while another faction is chosen, the glance cards come first, and three wordings are clearer** (fixes #665, #667, #668, #669, #670; page text and a write guard, no new write). With a faction other than your own chosen in Faction for Fill and Complete, a tier box used to stage a change to your own contribution anyway, while Fill was already off; now the tier boxes are off too, with the reason in the tooltip, and a click sends nothing. The line under the glance cards now reads "Houses taken by each faction: Atreides 0 · Harkonnen 2 (your faction) · Smuggler 0 · 23 still open". The current-term glance cards now come before the Term and decrees panel, so the decree controls sit below them. When no house has a place, each Location cell says "not mapped yet" (not a dash) and the note after the disabled Group by region button is a full sentence with a gap from the button  A tier box that is off is dimmed with a dashed border and no hover colour, the status line and tooltip say only the reason, and its name points at the row's caption instead of repeating the reason. The faction is marked "(your faction)" everywhere (glance line, drop-downs, house status), each count in the glance line stays whole when the line wraps, and a house with no place says "not mapped yet" in every place it is named (#665, #667, #668, #669, #670).
- **Go to a place: the count is of the places on the map shown, and the hubs of other maps are counted and listed apart** (fixes #607; page text and the list cap only, no save write and no change to what a move does). The first line of the list said, for the Deep Desert, "10 places on DeepDesert" while two of the ten were the Arrakeen and Harko Village taxi service rows, which are other maps. It now counts only the places on that map, in the words the Map box uses ("8 places on the Deep Desert, 2 on other maps, nearest first"; "nearest first" only when there is more than one place), and the hub rows are listed last under their own heading, "Other maps (moves there are untested)", instead of inside Taxis. A map with nothing of its own says "No places on the Deep Desert, 2 on other maps". The Map box ("This map (the Deep Desert)") and the note about a body on another map use the same names. The list is cut at 200 rows as before, but the hub rows are kept and the places of the map give way, so a long list no longer hides them. The Movement card's About text no longer says the hubs are under Taxis. The rows, their "another map" tag and the warning when one is chosen are as before. (#607)
- **Landsraad decrees show the names the game shows, not readable forms of its internal ids** (fixes #608; the page text and a read only; no save write). The decree pool, the Active decree drop-down, the term's active and elected decree and the Make active labels now use the game's own decree names, for example Scholarly Collaboration, In Honor of the Fallen (Ranged) and (Melee, the two decrees the game gives one name), CHOAM Production Contract, The Right of Salvage, CHOAM Refining Contract and Exclusive Vehicle, Weapon, Armor and Tool Import Rights. The game's one-line effect of each decree is shown as text under its name (in the pool row, and under the Active decree drop-down for the chosen decree; it is also in the tooltip), and the internal id stays as a small line below. The Active decree drop-down is sorted by name. The plain SpecialVendorActive row has no name in the game's decree table, so it keeps the editor's own "Special Vendor" with the note "editor's name, not the game's"; an id the editor has never seen shows as a readable form of the id with the note "no game name for this id", and never breaks the page. The note also shows in the drop-down option, the summary line and the Applied line, the Ranged and Melee tooltip says the bracket is added here, the Make active and Set flag buttons name their decree for a screen reader, and the About text and the hidden row's heading ("Hidden: the plain SpecialVendorActive row (no game name, no category, effect unverified)") no longer describe the vendor rows by their ids. The names come from the game's own decree table (internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md, "decree-names.json") and a test fails when a decree id of the test save has no name. (#608)
- **Landsraad board: a raise grants what completing the missions it stands for grants** (fixes #609, refs #652 (the pull request), #656, #641; **a behaviour change as well as a fix**: a plain tier click now also raises the faction's progress and can take the house; a SAVE WRITE PATH, so reviewed before it is merged; not yet seen in game). A tier click, Fill, Fill faction goal and Fill chosen that raise your contribution to a house now write, in one transaction with the usual backup and write gate: the faction's progress raised by the same amount while the house is open and the house taken when that reaches the goal; one progress event with its player link and the progress cursor moved to it; one reward row for each tier your own events had not crossed (the top tier is the house swatch while it is not held, with its one-time row); 6,000 Solari (currency 0) and 1,000 House Scrip (currency 1) for each mission, and 1,500 times `LandsraadSpecializationXpMultiplier` experience for each mission on the specialization line of the house (a line with no row gets one; a track stops at its last level). The House Scrip and the experience are the Recollection reward of the mission card, so they are written as a mission with a Recollection turned in pays (whether a mission without one pays them is not known, #745). It counts ground raised from now on: a house raised by an earlier version already has events that cover its tiers, so a new Fill pays nothing for them, and tiers that version left out stay unpaid. (Since #776 the editor writes no progress event and no cursor move; the sentences below about the event are the first build's.) A mission is 1,000 times `LandsraadContributionMultiplier`; both multipliers are read from `ServerCustomSettings.ini` (1 when the key is not in the file, and a value that is not a number from 0 to 10 refuses the write with the reason and writes nothing). The house to line table (25 houses, five to a line, from a public community tracker) and the track numbers (1 Crafting, 2 Gathering, 3 Exploration, 4 Combat, 5 Sabotage, measured; a number learned on the Specialization tab takes its place) are fixed in the editor; a house with no known line is refused. **Per-tier rule (#656, the default chosen):** "already delivered" is decided per tier by the character's own events for the task (the sum of `player_progress` of its `landsraad_task_progress` rows), not per item: only the tiers above `max(contribution before, that sum)` are delivered, so a reward collected in a later term or the same item at two tiers no longer hides a tier, and a second Fill, or a Fill after a removal, grants nothing. The event records the contribution beyond the events so the sum of the events is the contribution again. The result line and the toast name the missions, the Solari, House Scrip and experience, and warn when tiers were left out because they were already delivered (#656 G-017). **Not written, and said so:** your character's own experience (350 per mission on the card) and faction standing; the daily and weekly mission limits that community guides quote (625 specialization XP a day, 35 missions a week, unverified) are ignored, like every other Landsraad write. Older routes (`/api/landsraad/mine`, `/faction-goal`) are unchanged and write none of this. Tests: `internal/ops/landsraad_grant_test.go` (every case on a copy built from the real schema: the grant, the stock and learned track numbers, a second Fill and a Fill after removals, a collected-then-raised house and one item at two tiers, five failures at five steps that leave nothing behind, the refusals), the updated reward and apply tests, `internal/web/landsraad_grant_route_test.go` (the settings file), `internal/config/setting_test.go` and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board-write.test.mjs`). **After review:** the board says before the click what Fill, Fill faction goal and each tier will grant (missions, Solari, House Scrip, specialization XP by line, rewards), in text next to the control and tied to it; a click on one house that would grant 10 missions or more asks first, and the bulk question carries the totals and names its button by the action; the result line says "written" (or "to write" while the game runs and the change is only queued), never "granted", says the pay-out has not been checked in the game yet, and a removal says it takes back nothing the tier paid; a warning is drawn as a warning, stays longer and is announced; the closed row's meter is a picture and the tier clicks are the boxes of the opened row. The write refuses, with a reason and nothing written, when two specialization lines resolve to the same track number (#750); a house taken by the raise is counted once, and Fill faction goal leaves the faction at its progress plus what you gained and at least at the goal, as a plain Fill does (#751); a contribution multiplier of 0 is refused instead of counting one mission, both unusable multipliers are named, and every refusal says what to do. One change may write at most 1,000 missions, counted over every house in the request; a multiplier so small that the missions cannot be counted is refused, with nothing written; the text before a click says "writes", not "grants".
- **Create a vehicle: a double click wrote two vehicles** (fixes #740; a save write path, a repeated Create is now answered with the first one). The second press came before the first answer and nothing stopped it. Create is now off while its request is on its way, and a second press does nothing. Each form also sends one id with Create: when the editor gets the same request with the same id again (a retry after a lost answer, say) it answers with the vehicle it already made and writes nothing. The editor keeps these answers in memory only, the last 16, so closing and starting it again forgets them. The same id with a different request writes again, and so does a Create that was refused. An answer is also forgotten when its vehicle is gone: after Discard, Restore or a reload that could not put the edit back, the same Create makes a new vehicle. (#740)
- **The player page, the summary and the saved map take the character's place the same way the actions do** (fixes #699, audit finding L3-04; reads and page text only, no save write). Since #675 Bring, Create, Duplicate, Teleport and the place list read the character's place through one function (the map is the controller's; the position is the body's only when the controller, the body and the player state agree). Three readers still took the place from the body alone, so for a save with the body on another map than the player (the #654 case) the Movement card named the body's map as "This map" and filled in the body's coordinates while the server measured from the player and refused a move with "This map" chosen, the summary showed the body's map, and the saved map of the player's own map showed no character. All three now use the same function: `/api/player` (`actor`), `/api/summary` (`map`) and `/api/livemap` (`character`) give the controller's map and the position the actions use, and say so when the actors do not agree (`parked` holds the sentence the actions refuse with; `actor.bodyMap` the map the body is on). The Movement card shows a short note in that case. Saves where the three agree are unchanged. Tests: `internal/ops/place_readers_test.go` (each of the three, with the body on another map and for an ordinary character; they fail on the old code) and `web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs` (the card fills in the player's position, names the player's map, and shows the note only when the answer says so).
- **A table box starts at its top when it shows other rows: another inventory group, the inventory filter and Clear, another base, a base's sub-tab or inventory group, an opened vehicle** (#697, audit finding L3-02; page only, nothing in a save write path changes). #687 kept the scroll offset of every table box across these redraws, which put the old offset onto the new rows and could hide the first rows of a short list. The page offset is still kept; a redraw that shows the same rows (the give queue, the kit editors, a refused change) still keeps the box offsets too.
- **Teleport: a body that is not standing in the world is refused on every map, not moved between maps** (fixes #692, audit finding L2-04; a save write path, a move that was allowed is now refused). A save can hold the character's body on another map than the player (for example the travel map) with a state that is not 0. The same body on the player's own map was refused ("not standing in the world"), but Teleport with a named map wrote the controller, the body and the player state to that map and left the state as it was, with a vehicle the body sat in left behind. One rule now: a body whose state is not 0 is refused whatever its map, with a sentence that gives the state (and the two maps when they differ) and says nothing was changed. A body that is on another map and standing (state 0) is still moved by Teleport to a named map, all three actors together, as before. Setting the state to 0 in the cross-map move was not chosen: it needs a check in the game on a copy first. The comment, the code and the test now say the same thing. Tests: `internal/ops/character_place_test.go` (the place of a body on another map that is not standing is not the map-only case; Teleport refuses it with and without a map and changes nothing; a standing body on another map is still moved).
- **Building sets: the count line says learned only for sets the save lists as learned** (closes #698; page text and test only, no save write). The line used to count a set held only in an inventory as learned, so giving yourself a set made the line say one more was learned while the State column and the game said it was not (a set is learned when it is used in the game). The line now reads, for example, "1 of 3 learned (2 more only in an inventory)" and drops the bracket when there is none; the filter button keeps its own wording, "Learned or in inventory". Cosmetics wording is unchanged. The test in `web-tests/polish.test.mjs` pinned the old count ("3 of 4 learned" with one set only in an inventory) and was corrected, and a new test fails on the old behaviour.
- **Public-text check: commit comments, releases, created refs and pushes to main** (fixes #704; CI rules and tests only, no change to the editor's behaviour; a CI-control change, so reviewed before it is merged). The workflow `public-text-discussion.yml` now also runs on the `release` (published, edited) and `create` events, and `cmd/publictextcheck` reads the tag name, name and notes of a release, and the name of a branch or tag that was created without a pull request. A new workflow, `public-text-push.yml`, runs the commit-message check on a push to `main` (the head commit alone for a new or forced ref). Both report under their own non-required check names, so the required `public-text` check is unchanged. The one-off `-items` scan now applies the attribution rule to the title and body of a pull request (an item with `headRefName`), as the event check does. Left out on purpose and said so in the workflow's comment: comments on a commit (GitHub has no workflow trigger for them), discussions (turned off), and label and milestone text. Like the existing ones, the workflows are read-only (`contents: read`), do not use `pull_request_target`, and pass only numbers, ids and commit ids through expressions. Tests: `cmd/publictextcheck` (a payload per new event, clean and failing, and the attribution rule in the items scan) and `internal/hygiene` (the events and the push workflow are pinned, and a test fails on any expression in a public-text workflow other than a number, an id, a commit id or the digest secret).
- **The game's own numbers and keys are no longer shown across the Player tabs** (fixes the non-Landsraad part of #618, refs #608; display only: no save write, and every button sends the id it always sent; the Landsraad and Specialization parts of #618 are not in this entry and the issue stays open). Vendors were listed by a `Vendor ID` column, now `Vendor 1`, `Vendor 2` (the same in both tables and in the confirmation question), with the id in a tooltip and in a new "Vendor ids" list for support. A land claim was headed with its internal class name and totem number, and the editor had a `Totem ID` box; both are now `Claim`, with the totem in a tooltip and in a "Claim ids" list. The Bases list, the Base backups list and the stored-vehicles list lose their `ID` column, and the Placeables lists their `Id` column, and the number moves to a tooltip on the name; an open base is headed by its name and no longer `(#8)`; an open container is headed `Inventory: <name>` and no longer `Inventory <id>`; Create a vehicle says `Created vehicle:` and `Would create vehicle:` with no number; and the queue list says `Journey step <name>: completed` (the name once the Journey tab has been opened, otherwise `Journey step: completed`) and `Move a vehicle next to the character`, with the id as the tooltip of the line. The queue line of a decree's pool flag is Landsraad and is left for its own redesign.
- **Go to a place: every trading post of the game's table is named, and a test keeps it so** (fixes #869, refs #618, #500, #519; display and data only: no save write). No key was found missing: the game's table of fixed places (`DT_StaticLocationData`) has 14 rows with the trading-post tag (11 trading posts of four places, and the 3 taxi service points of the Deep Desert level), and `place-names.json` already names all 14 from the game's own text; the four `TradingPost` markers of the current single-player save and of 75 older saves and backups read here all carry keys the file names, so none of them shows as "Unnamed trading post". A test now fails when a trading-post key of the game's table, kept as a checked-in list so it runs without the game's files, has no name (or a blank one, or the wrong kind) in the file, and when the file and the list differ, so a new key cannot slip in unnoticed. The fallback stays for a trading post a later game build adds: its key is shown in the tooltip, and a test pins that. No name was added or changed.

### Added
- **Landsraad: every house has a place, from the game's fixed map places when the save has no marker** (refs #639; read only and page text, no save write; follows the entry below, whose "no table of positions" is replaced by this). The save holds a marker for a house representative only after the character has found it (18 of the 25 houses on the save checked), so 7 houses showed "location unknown". The editor now has a small table of where each of the 25 representatives stands, taken from the game's fixed map places as shown by the reference map (map, position, and the Hagga Basin area), and uses it only for a house with no marker in the save: a marker always wins. Result: all 25 houses have a Location (18 on the Hagga Basin, 3 in Arrakeen, 2 in Harko Village, 2 in the Deep Desert), so the board groups by region and shows the pin and the distance from you on the Hagga Basin and the Deep Desert. A Deep Desert house names its grid cell (B8 for Maros, A2 for Wayku; the Saved map draws no grid, so the cell is a name to find it by, not a mark on the map), also in the Location column, and "Open in Saved map" is now offered for them. Arrakeen and Harko Village show their name only: the Saved map has no picture of a hub (and no mapping from a position to one), so there is no pin, no distance and no map link there; their dialog is titled "Place", says so in a normal-size note and has no "From you" line. Tseida's place (The Anvil Tradepost) is marked "from public guides, not confirmed in the game" in the dialog, the peek and the card. Group by region now draws a heading per region with its count; the Location button's accessible name includes the house, "Open in Saved map" names it, and focus lands on the map when it opens. Three houses are not in the reference map's list of representatives: Maros and Wayku (Deep Desert; their position comes from its Deep Desert data files and falls in the grid cells B8 and A2 that the public guides give) and Tseida (its place is The Anvil Tradepost, which two public guides give for it). "Location unknown" remains only as a defensive fallback for a house that neither the save nor the table holds; a test with a save that has no marker at all proves that none of the 25 houses reaches it and that each place opens on its map (the two hubs excepted, see below). Opening a hub place in the Saved map needs a picture of each hub and its position mapping, which is not built yet (#758). The positions of the 18 houses with a marker were compared with the save and agree within 2 centimetres; the sources are credited in the notices (the public guides: location facts only). Tests: Go (`landsraad_locations_test.go`, both save layouts: a house with no marker gets its fixed place, a marker wins, hubs and the Deep Desert, an unknown house stays unknown, and the table holds all 25 houses) and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board.test.mjs`). Risk: low (read-only display).
- **Landsraad board: every click writes at once, and the plain Special Vendor decree is out of the pool list** (closes #641, refs #634; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). The tier boxes, Fill, Complete, Reopen, Fill faction goal and the bulk buttons no longer stage a pending change: each click is sent as one edit (one backup) and the row is read again from the save. The pressed control is disabled while its write runs, a second click waits, and a short result line and a toast say what was written, or give the error text as it came. A bulk button for more than one house asks first ("Complete 7 houses?", Yes or Cancel); one house is written without a question. The Pending chip, Undo, the bulk Write button and the writes badges are gone. Reopen clears the completion, the winner and the completion time but does not set back the progress that Complete raised or a previous winner, and removing a tier does not take back the reward rows that giving it wrote; the tooltips and the About text say so. The plain `SpecialVendorActive` decree (no category, effect not verified) is shown only in a closed list under the decree pool, with the same controls; its row is never changed or deleted.
- **Landsraad: the place of a house comes from the save's own map markers** (fixes #639; read only and page text, no save write; the link from a place to the map view is not part of this change). Each house representative is a map marker of the save ("HouseRepresentative" and the house name), so the board's Location column now shows the map and the named area of the marker (the Hagga Basin area, or the map's name on the Deep Desert, Harko Village and Arrakeen), the pin and the distance from your character on the two maps that have a picture, and the quick peek and map dialog read the same place. No table of positions is stored and nothing is invented: a house whose representative has no marker in the save shows "location unknown" with the reason in its map dialog, and a hub, or a marker beyond the edge of the map picture, gets no pin, distance or map link, only its name or area. The save spells one house differently from its marker (Mikkarol, Mikarrol); the pair is matched by name. On the save checked, 18 of the 25 houses have a marker (13 Hagga Basin, 2 Harko Village, 3 Arrakeen); Imota, Kenola, Lindaren, Maros, Novebruns, Tseida and Wayku have none. Tests: Go (`landsraad_locations_test.go`, both save layouts) and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board.test.mjs`).
- **Theme menu: a filter box in the Colours list** (refs #683; the theme menu only: nothing is written to a save, no action route changes). A box at the top of the Colours list keeps the palettes whose name, group or theme word (Dark, Dayside) holds every word typed, case and punctuation ignored. Headings with no match go; None and the chosen palette stay (the chosen one says "(chosen)" when it is shown only for that); a line says "N of 18 palettes", or "No palette matches ... Clear the box to see all 18" when none does (a polite status for a screen reader). A Clear button (44 px) empties the box. Arrow keys walk through the box and the shown items, Enter in the box moves to the first match (it picks nothing), Escape clears the box first and closes the menu on the next press, and a letter typed on a palette item moves to the box. The box stays at the top while the list scrolls, and an item the arrows reach is scrolled clear of it. The 18 palettes and the kept choice are unchanged. The 42 dye palettes asked for in #683 are not in this change: they wait for the question of the colour source's licence (a separate draft pull request).
- **Create a vehicle: replace a part directly in the preview table** (closes #672; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). Each row of the preview table (Slot, Part, Durability) now has a Part box that lists the parts that fit that slot of that class, with the same labels as Change parts (a part that gives a cargo hold shows its slots, a unique part says so, an optional slot can be set to empty). An optional slot that holds no part gets a row too, so a part can be put there from the table. Picking a part asks for the preview again with that part changed (after a short wait of 600 ms with no other pick, because the arrow keys on a closed list change the value at every press; the parts picked in that time are asked together), and the sentence (health, fuel, cargo hold) and the table (parts, durability) are drawn again in place: the page offset and the keyboard focus stay on the box that was changed (`render('scroll')`, as in #650). The parts are the draft's parts: Change parts and the table show the same part for a slot. When the request is refused (for example, a part that leaves no cargo hold while items are listed) the parts go back to the ones the old preview shows, the old preview stays, and the reason is said under the sentence as an alert and with the box (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`); nothing is written. A success is said in one short toast for a screen reader ("Utility 1 is now Buggy Storage Mk6 (20 slots). Health 700, cargo hold of 20 slots."). While a pick is waiting or its preview is on its way a line says "Updating the preview..." and Create waits (a note, not an error, if it is pressed). Every change that decides what the preview is (class, tier, a part in Change parts, an item added or removed, Preview, Cancel, a created vehicle) drops the preview and every answer still on its way, and an answer is used only when nothing changed after its request, so a late answer cannot bring back a table the form no longer makes. Create also compares the form's request with the one the shown preview was asked with and, when they differ (for example the fuel or a quantity was edited after the preview), drops the preview and asks for Preview again instead of writing. So Create writes exactly what the table shows. Cancel discards the draft with the edits. The rows follow the class's slot order, a muted line says that a pick swaps a part and writes nothing, the box has no game id as a tooltip, and the box fits a phone width and has a 44 px tap height on a touch screen. The status sentence stays the live region; the table sits outside it and each box is labelled (for example "Part for Front hull"). No rule changed: the fitting parts come from the existing catalog and the refusals from the same checks Preview and Create already share. The server's refusal for items without a cargo hold now reads "...so items cannot be put in it. Give it a storage part, or remove the items" (one sentence break, "part" not "module"). Tests: fifteen page tests in `web-tests/vehiclecreate.test.mjs` (the boxes and their parts, the redraw with offset and focus, the arrow keys, picks asked together, a refusal, Create equal to the preview, Create waiting, five late-answer cases, a fuel edit after the preview, Cancel) and three Go tests in `internal/ops/vehicle_spawn_test.go` (every part the catalog offers is accepted by the preview and shows in its rows, a swap changes the figures and Create writes the previewed parts, a part that does not fit is refused with the same plain reason by Preview and Create and nothing changes).
- **Public-text check: four gaps closed** (fixes #689, #700, #702, #703; CI rules and tests only, no change to the editor's behaviour; a CI-control change, so reviewed before it is merged). Reserved words: the text is folded first (every dash and the minus sign become a hyphen, full-width letters become ASCII, combining marks are dropped, a format character such as a zero-width space or the soft hyphen becomes a break), and every run of up to four parts of the words (the pieces between hyphens and underscores) and of the words as written is hashed joined by a space, a hyphen, an underscore and nothing, so a listed name with a part added (a branch or file name), written with a space, with markup, with another dash or with a hidden character in it is found; a listed single word written in parts is found too. A phrase is found over a line break (LF or CRLF) and the hit is on the line of its first word. Profile paths: one rule set now sees the users or home folder after a drive letter, after the POSIX-shell forms of a drive (a one-letter folder, a mount or cygdrive prefix), after a file URL, after a colon, a bracket or a greater-than sign, and under a network host or share (including the WSL host); a web address is still not a path; a hidden character inside the folder or the account name does not hide it; four first names are no longer exempt as placeholders. File scan: a file with a two-byte byte-order mark is decoded and scanned, a file with a stray non-UTF-8 byte is scanned, and a file with a known text extension that holds NUL bytes and no mark fails the scan naming the path only. One scp-style example in `tools/send-snapshot.ps1` now uses a placeholder account folder. Cannot be found, and not claimed: a reserved name written as a hyphenated entry but spelled without any separator (the list holds a digest, so the checker cannot split a word it does not know), look-alike letters of other scripts, text in an image or an archive, an account name in a path shape the patterns do not know (for example the old Documents and Settings folder), and a file larger than 8 MiB. Tests: `internal/hygiene` (each form above with made-up words and a marker so the file holds no path, both byte orders, stray bytes and the failures).
- **Docs: a dated decision record for the audit cadence (L2 and L3 on request, L1 for real designs) and for why changes are not peer reviewed, with the compensating controls, the accepted risk and the revisit triggers** (docs only; `docs/decisions/2026-11-14-audit-cadence-and-no-peer-review.md`, #673)
- **Public-text check over issue, pull request and comment text, a wider file scan and neutral labels in the guard's own files** (refs #653, audit finding F1; CI, tests, docs and comments only, no change to the editor's behaviour). A new workflow, `.github/workflows/public-text.yml`, runs on issues, issue comments, pull requests (opened, edited, synchronized), reviews and review comments, and a pull request's commit messages, and fails with the place and the rule when a title, a body, a comment, a branch name or a commit message holds a reserved word, a path under a personal profile (a drive path under Users or a Unix path under Users or home, with an account name) or, for a pull request, an AI attribution line (the squash message on `main` comes from the title and body, which nothing checked before). The program is `cmd/publictextcheck`, the rules are the new package `internal/hygiene`: reserved words are kept as SHA-256 digests of up to four words in a row, and an optional repository secret, `PUBLIC_TEXT_EXTRA_DIGESTS`, adds digests without putting them in the repository (`publictextcheck -digest WORD...` prints one). The log never repeats the offending text. The tree test now reads every text file whatever its extension, and the file paths too, and its failure message names the file, the line and the rule only. Neutral wording in place of the old labels and comments in `internal/hygiene`, `design/files_test.go`, this file and the 2026-11-13 delta register; comments, a kitchen-sink label set and example labels in `design/` no longer say what the editor is not; the pull request template has one checklist line and the two issue forms one note about the rule. Tests: `internal/hygiene` (fixtures with a digest of a word of the test's own, profile paths and placeholders, attribution lines, the tree walk, the workflow's events) and `cmd/publictextcheck` (events of every kind, a one-off items scan, commit ranges, refusal of a check that read nothing).
- **Landsraad tab: the real house symbols** (closes #645). Each of the 25 houses shows its own symbol at 32 px, with the name beside it, in place of the two-letter placeholder; the symbol and the name are still the one button that opens the house card. The pictures are 64 px (sharp on a high-density screen), about 42 KB for all 25, embedded in the program and served from a fixed list (`/houses/<house>.webp`); a house with no picture keeps the two letters, and the symbol is drawn dark on a light look. They are game art and are covered by the art notice. Tests: `web-tests/landsraad-symbols.test.mjs` (every house of the board resolves to its own file or the fallback, strange names never reach a path) and `internal/web/house_symbols_test.go`.
- **Landsraad tab: review fixes against the mock-up** (refs #599, #609; the reward fix is a save write path). The quick peek on the place (mouse hover) is drawn; the status lines under the bulk bar and the term facts are styled as in the mock-up (the term facts no longer meet the Player tab's card style, and Start and End read as play-clock seconds); headings wrap on a phone so the page does not scroll sideways at 320 px or wider; the bar segments keep a 44 px hit area on a phone; a save with no houses in the term says so. Fill and Fill chosen act only on the character's own faction: with another faction chosen the quick Fill is off, Fill faction goal for another faction completes the house for that faction without a personal contribution and without a reward row, and the server refuses a request that does both for another faction. The place dialog and the card show how far the house is from your character (when it is on the same map and the house has a mapped position) and Open in Saved map centres that map on it; the Smuggler bar is gold and striped, no longer the colour of a house faction; while no house has a mapped position the board says so once instead of a row each, and Group by region and the Location sort are off. Giving the top tier again after a removal, in the term in which the swatch was won, no longer writes the task's own item in place of the swatch: nothing is written, as the swatch was already delivered in that term.
- **Landsraad: the new board, read only (part 1 of the tab rework)** (refs #599, #440, #609; page and read side only, nothing in a save write path changes). The Landsraad tab opens with the board of the mock-up `docs/design/mock/landsraad/a-board.html`: three counts of the houses by your contribution (top tier, in progress, not started) that filter the list, a proportional bar, one alphabetical list of the houses with a sortable heading for every column, Show, House, Group by region and Jump to house, rows that open to the five tiers, both factions' progress and the place, a small card on the house name, and an Earlier terms card. Every tier shows its reward by the game's item name (the internal id only in the tooltip) and where it stands: not reached, waiting to collect (an amount above 0 in the delivery rows), delivered (amount 0, written in this term) or reached with no delivery recorded. The top tier shows the house swatch while the character does not hold it and the house's own item once it is held. The place of a house is a marked placeholder ("not mapped yet") until a table of the house representatives' positions is built; no position is invented. The earlier controls (term, decrees, the first task board with its Fill and Complete buttons) are unchanged and sit in a closed disclosure below the board until the new board writes (the next parts). New read fields of `/api/landsraad`: `reward_state`, `shown_template_id`, `shown_amount`, `swatch` on every tier; `swatch_held`, `swatch_template_id`, `location` on every house; `terms` and `earlier_terms`. Tests: Go (`landsraad_board_test.go`, both save layouts) and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board.test.mjs`).
- **Landsraad: the term and decrees panel (part 4 of the tab rework)** (refs #599, #440, #608, #634; the controls and their writes are the editor's existing ones, drawn as in the merged mock-up). A closed panel above the board shows the term (number, start and end on the play clock, reigning faction, active and elected decree) with the Active decree and Reigning faction drop-downs and Apply, and the decree pool (id, decree, an "In this term" badge, the pool flag as stored, a Set flag button for that one row, and Make active for the chosen faction). Decrees are shown by a readable name with the game's internal id as a small second line (the Refining Contract by the game's own name). Apply and Make active write `active_decree_id` and `reigning_faction_id` of the latest term row in one edit (an empty choice clears the field), Set flag writes the `disabled` column of its own row, and each write goes to the save at once. The old term and decree cards and the "Previous controls" section are gone. Tests: `web-tests/landsraad-decrees.test.mjs` (the panel, the names, the exact requests, the faction drop-down, Make active, a save without a term, a hostile decree name).
- **Landsraad: the board delivers the rewards of the tiers it gives (part 3 of the tab rework)** (refs #609, #599, #440; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). When the board's Write raises the character's contribution to a house (a tier click, Fill, Fill faction goal, Fill chosen), it now also writes what the game leaves in a save for it, as read from real saves (the evidence is in the comments of #609): one `landsraad_task_progress` event (an id above every event and above the cursor, the character's faction, the task, the faction's share, the amount the character added, the play clock as the time) with its `landsraad_task_progress_player` row, and one `landsraad_house_rewards` row for each tier crossed (the template and amount of `landsraad_task_rewards`; an amount above 0 waits at the house representative). The same item from two tiers is one row; a waiting amount from an earlier term is added to, a collected one is set. At the highest tier the house swatch is delivered, with a row in `landsraad_player_one_time_house_rewards` (the current term), while the character holds no swatch row for the house; once it does, the task's own item is delivered and no row is added. A tier is delivered once per term (a row written in this term is not written again after a removal and a second give). Removing a tier, Complete, Reopen and anything done for the other faction write no event and no reward row. Nothing is lowered. The cursor `landsraad_task_progress_processed` is left as it is. The opened row lists what a pending change would add, and the answer and the toast count the reward rows. Tests: `internal/ops/landsraad_rewards_test.go` (each case on a copy, exact rows, with the swatch held and not held, the older layout, the merge, the once-per-term rule and the cursor) and the page.
- **Landsraad: the board writes (part 2 of the tab rework)** (refs #599, #440, #609; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). The tiers, the quick buttons and the bulk bar of the new board now act. A click stages a change on the page: the row shows it as pending with an Undo, the counts, the bar and the sort follow, and nothing is sent. A Write button sends everything that is pending as one request, `POST /api/landsraad/apply`, which is one edit with one backup and is refused as a whole when the term, or any house, is no longer what the page showed. Per house the request carries the contribution wanted (`mine_to`, 0 or a reward threshold of the house, under the character's own faction), the faction the house is to be completed for (`complete_for`, the code of Complete chosen: progress to the goal, never lowered, the winner and the play clock) or a reopen. The contribution is raised by Fill and by a tier click; it goes down only when the highest reached tier is removed (`lower: true`), to the tier below or, for tier 1, by deleting the row; Fill never lowers it, also after a removal. Completing for a faction the character is not in records that faction's win and progress and never writes a personal row. No reward row is written by this part. The old task board (its own Fill, Complete and Choose controls) is gone from the page; the term and decree controls stay in a closed disclosure until part 4. Tests: Go (`landsraad_apply_test.go`: every action on a copy, exact rows, nothing lowered by Fill or Complete, atomic refusal; `landsraad_apply_route_test.go`) and the page (`web-tests/landsraad-board-write.test.mjs`); the tests of the old controls were replaced by these.
- **Base import checks the values of a base file, and Repair buildings and Repair base never copy a health out of range** (refs #613, audit finding D3-1; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). A base file used to pass the preview and the import with a stack of 9e18, two items in one slot, a slot past the end of its inventory and any finite health; a piece with a health of 1e300 then became "the best health seen" for its type, and Repair buildings, Repair base copied it onto every piece of that type, the player's own bases included. The file check (`Verify`, which the preview and the import both run, for the editor's file and the console's) now refuses the whole file, with the row and the reason and nothing written, when: a stack is above the most one slot holds for the item (the game's own item tables; 100,000 for an item the catalog does not know); two items share a slot of one inventory; a slot is above 100,000, or an inventory states a slot count below -1 or above 100,000 (-1, 0 and no value all mean no count is stated, as in the game's own saves); a building piece or a placeable has a health that is not a number from 0 to 1,000,000; an item's current, maximum or decayed maximum durability is outside 0 to 1,000,000 or does not fit a number. The longest text a cell may hold (1,000 characters), numbers that are not finite and the other checks are unchanged. Repair buildings and Repair base take the best health of a type among the healths from 0 to 1,000,000 only, so what they write is always inside the range and an out-of-range piece is neither copied nor lowered. Tests: `baseimport_values_test.go` (each refusal leaves the save as it was; the limits themselves and the game's own export still import; both repairs). Every rule was run over the whole of a real save (30,627 items, 3,457 pieces, 230 placeables) and over the console export of each of its bases, and none refuses what the game wrote; an earlier version of this change held a slot to its inventory's slot count and refused -1 (the game's own value for none, in 3,783 of about 4,200 inventories), which broke the console export of two real bases, so both rules were dropped (`TestExportAndImportOfABaseWhoseInventoriesStateNoSlotCount`). Not changed here: the export still reads its tables with separate queries (D3-2, informational).
- **Public-repository hygiene: a document no longer points at a file outside this repository, the notices cover the embedded game data added since 2026-10-27, and a test keeps reserved words out of every text file** (refs #621, audit findings D11-1, D11-2 and D11-3; docs, comments and tests only, no change to the editor's behaviour). `docs/save-database.md` lost the sentence that named a related document kept elsewhere; comments and provenance lines in `kit/` say "other programs" where they said how many programs share the module; the public-safety test of `design/` keeps its reserved words as digests; `internal/notices/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` has a section for `place-names.json`, `place-arrivals.json`, `deep-desert-taxis.json`, `item_table_fillables.json` and the two save-schema DDL files, with what the repository records of where each came from (and what it does not record); `TestEveryEmbeddedGameDataFileHasANotice` pins every embedded game-data file, and `internal/hygiene` fails when any tracked text file holds a reserved word.
- **A delta audit of the changes since 2026-10-27** (refs #606; docs only, no change to the editor): `docs/audit/2026-11-13-delta-register.md` lists the findings (none critical or high, eight medium, 28 low, 17 info, in 13 groups: #611 to #623), what was checked and found sound, and which earlier fixes were kept.
- **Landsraad: Fill faction goal now completes the house for the chosen faction at the highest reward** (#601; the first piece of the Landsraad rework, the tab redesign stays in #599). It used to set the faction's progress on the house to the house goal and nothing else, so the house still read as open. It now writes what Complete chosen and Fill chosen at the highest tier write together, for the faction in the faction list: the faction's progress goes to the house goal (never lowered), the house is marked complete with that faction as winner and a completion time on the game's play clock (#465, the same code as Complete), and your own contribution under that faction goes to the house's highest reward tier (6,000 in the term read so far; never lowered). A reward row is never written: handing a tier out is the game's own act on the contribution (#380). It is one edit with the usual backup and write-log line, queued when the game is running like every other write, and it needs a faction in the list and a Landsraad term (otherwise the page says so and sends nothing). A house that is already complete for that faction at the goal with the contribution at the top tier says "Nothing to change"; otherwise the result line names what changed (the progress, the completion, the contribution before and after). The button sends `POST /api/landsraad/faction-goal` with `task_id`, `faction_id` and `term_id` (a term that is not the current one, a house of an earlier term or the None faction is refused and nothing is written); `POST /api/landsraad/progress` is unchanged and no longer used by the page. Complete, Reopen, Complete chosen, Reopen chosen and Fill chosen are unchanged. Tests: `landsraad_goal_test.go` (including that the state equals Complete chosen plus Fill chosen at the top tier), `landsraad_goal_route_test.go`, `web-tests/landsraad-complete.test.mjs`. **Not in game yet.**
- **The editor's fill, refill, refuel and repair actions play an animation on the pressed button and on the result toast, with Motion = Full** (refs #571; slice 1 of the fills, the page scripts and one stylesheet, nothing in a save read or write path changes). The icon comes from the action that was pressed, never from the words of the result: a drop for Fill hydration, Fill blood sacks, Refill Literjons and Refill base water, a ring for Fill all liquids, a can for Refuel all my vehicles and Refill power, a shield for Repair all worn items, Repair (one vehicle), Repair all my vehicles, Repair this base to max and Repair every base to max; the heart and the bolt are drawn too, for the fills of health and stamina that the editor does not have yet. On the button the icon sits in a round chip in the middle for about two seconds (nothing moves, and the words, title and aria of the button are untouched); on the toast it replaces the check. A result where something was written is green with the icon, also when the rest was already full; a result where nothing was written is now an amber notice with no icon that leaves by itself (it was a green check before); an error is red with no icon. Calm, Off and the system's reduced-motion setting show the plain check and nothing on the button. A new test (`web-tests/fillfx.test.mjs`) walks the whole list of actions and fails for a fill or repair action that has no animation. The Motion setting itself (Full, Calm, Off, in the Theme menu) is unchanged.
- **The Theme menu gets a Colours group with the 18 palettes of the design module** (#587, refs #570; page scripts, one stylesheet rule set and tests, nothing in a save read or write path changes). Under Dark / Dayside the menu has Colours: None (the look's own colours, the default), then the palettes under their group headings (Planet, House & faction, Character, Guild & commerce, Mystical & mental), Arrakis Incandescent first. A palette recolours whichever look is chosen, and only in its own theme: 16 are dark palettes and two, Fremen Sietch and Mentat, are Dayside palettes. One that is not in effect in the theme shown now stays pickable, carries the words "Dayside only" or "Dark only" and a tooltip, and the Theme button's tooltip says it is not applied; the choice is kept. The choice is one stored word (`tauPalette`, a fixed list of 18 ids; anything else is ignored and never used as a file name or a selector), read by `look-boot.js` before the first paint into `html[data-palette]`, and another window follows by the storage event; the `taulook` event now carries the palette too. Both pages (the editor and the locked page) link `/design/palettes.css` after the look's files and `motion.css`; the content security policy is unchanged. The table of palettes in `look-boot.js` is the module's own list, and a Go test compares it with `design.Palettes()` line by line. Tests: `web-tests/colours.test.mjs`, `internal/web/colours_test.go`; the contrast tests of the editor's colour names and of the Theme menu now also run for every palette laid over every look in its own theme.
- **The editor gets a Theme menu: the look (Fremen, Awakening or Sietch), Dark or Dayside, the heading font and the Motion setting, kept in the browser** (refs #570; slice 3 of 3, stacked on the Motion setting #571). A new head script, `look-boot.js`, reads three keys (`tauLook`, `tauTheme`, `tauFont`) before the first paint, writes them on the page (`data-look`, `data-theme`, `data-heading-font`) and swaps the look's two stylesheets for the chosen look's. A stored value is only ever compared with a fixed list and never names a file; the file table is the design module's own, and a test compares it with the module. Fremen and Dark stay the defaults, Dayside follows the system until a choice is made, and the locked page wears the same look. In the header, a bulb switches between Dark and Dayside, and a **Theme** button opens a menu of four groups (Look, Dark / Dayside, Font, Motion) that works from the keyboard (arrows, Home, End, Enter, Escape). The three Motion buttons of the previous slice moved into it. Nothing touches a save read or write path. Tests: `web-tests/theme.test.mjs`, `internal/web/theme_test.go`.
- **The editor gets the Motion setting (Full, Calm or Off) and a little motion that shows cause and effect: a count that bumps, a check that is drawn, a row that lights up, toasts with fill icons** (refs #571; slice 2 of 3, stacked on the Look #570). A new head script, `motion-boot.js`, reads one choice before the first paint and writes it on the page for the design module's `motion.css` (`data-motion` full, calm or off; `data-motion-src` when the person chose; `data-doc-hidden` while the window is hidden, which pauses animations). Until a choice is made the system decides: "reduce motion" gives Calm, anything else Full. The choice is kept in the browser under one key, `tauMotion`, and is set with three buttons in the status bar (they move into the Theme menu in slice 3). In Full: the "N queued" count bumps once when a change joins the queue, the edited row lights up once when an edit is accepted, and toasts leave with a fade; in Calm only the short fades remain; in Off nothing moves and every toast is gone at once. When a write goes through, a drawn check and the word Written show for three seconds in the status bar (Discard does not trigger it). Results of a fill, a refuel and a repair show the module's drop, can or shield icon instead of the plain check; every toast has a shape as well as a colour (check, triangle, cross) and an error still stays until it is dismissed. Nothing loops, nothing changes the layout, and no save read or write path is touched. Tests: `web-tests/motion.test.mjs`, `internal/web/motion_test.go`.
- **The editor wears the shared look: the Fremen look with Dune Rise headings, drawn by the design module** (refs #570; slice 1 of 3, the Look: the Motion setting #571 and the Theme menu follow in their own pull requests). The editor's `go.mod` now requires the in-tree `design` module with a `replace`, as it does for `kit`, and serves the module's files read-only from its own address under `/design/`. The page (and the locked page) links the four files of the default look (`tokens-fremen.css`, `components-fremen.css`, `components.css`, `motion.css`) before `app.css`. `app.css` keeps its own colour names (`--bg`, `--panel`, `--fg`, `--mut`, `--acc` and the rest), which are now aliases of the module's tokens, so every look and Dayside colour the page; the two hard-coded palettes are gone. Beside the old classes the markup gets the module's: `d-bar` on the header, `d-nav` on the tabs, `d-panel` on every card and on the question box, `d-btn` (with `primary`, `danger` and `sm`) on every button, `d-pill` on the queued count and `d-toggle` on the Auto-refresh switch. The fixed headings of a card are in Dune Rise; a heading that holds a name or a number from the save, the game or a person stays a plain heading, and the wordmark stays in the system face. The content security policy is unchanged (it already allowed fonts from the editor's own address). Nothing in a save read or write path changed. Tests: the contrast checks now read the module's tokens and run for every look in the dark theme and in Dayside (text 4.5:1, a control's edge and the focus ring 3:1), the module's files are served with their own types under the loopback and Host rules, and every card and button carries the module's class.
- **The design module gets version 0.3.0 content: eighteen colour palettes that recolour a look** (`design/assets/palettes.css`, generated from a table by a test). A palette is chosen with `html[data-palette="ID"]`, changes colour tokens only, and applies in its own theme (sixteen in the dark theme, Fremen Sietch and Mentat in Dayside); Arrakis Incandescent is first. The colours are those of the MIT-licensed fan editor themes the notice already names, moved toward white or black in steps of 4 % only where a contrast check needs it (each palette's comment names what moved). New Go API: `design.Palettes`, `design.Palette`, `design.PaletteFiles` (like `LookFiles`: a refusal for any string that is not an id), `design.NoPalette`, `design.PalettesFile`, `design.ThemeDark`, `design.ThemeLight`; the kitchen sink gets a Colours list. The contrast tests now run for every palette on every look. `design.Version` is v0.3.0, and no tag is made by this change.
- **The design module gets its content for version 0.2.0: three looks (Fremen, the default, Awakening and Sietch) in a dark and a light theme, their components, the icons, the Motion setting, the heading typeface Dune Rise and the checks of the design review** (refs #572, #573, #574). `design/assets/tokens.css` is unchanged, so a program written against 0.1.0 keeps its look; the looks have their own `tokens-LOOK.css` and `components-LOOK.css`, with `components.css` (touch sizes, reachable tooltips, tile, rail and drawer, a forced-colours block) and `motion.css` (Motion setting `html[data-motion]` full, calm, off; Calm by default under the system's reduced-motion setting) shared. The grain is a tiled SVG file, never a `data:` URL; the focus ring of a cut part is never clipped; `design.Handler` sends an explicit type for every extension. Dune Rise (SIL Open Font License 1.1) and the MIT-licensed palette values have their notices in `design/NOTICE` and in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`. New tests: contrast of every look and theme (including the derived tints and hover mixes), no outside reference or inline script, focus and clip-path, motion, sizes, forced colours, the kitchen sink uses every class, the font checksum, public safety. **The editor does not use the module yet; nothing in the editor changes.** The font file and the tag `design/v0.2.0` are added by the repository owner (see `design/README.md`).
- **The kit module gets `kit/ddgrid`, the lettered grid of the Deep Desert (A1 to I9) as one function and one set of constants, and the taxi generator and its tests count the cell letters on it** (refs #568). `Cell(x, y)` names the square of a world position (empty outside the grid or for a number that is not finite) and the frame is exported (`N`, `MinX`, `MaxX`, `MaxY`, `MinY`, `CellW`, `CellH`): nine columns 269,650 wide and nine rows 269,217 high. The game's rows are not the equal parts of the picture's rectangle that `internal/ops/livemap.go` draws the map on (rows 269,650 high), so near a row border the cell letter of the generator's old rule could differ by one row; tests were written first against the old rule, and a test now says that the two agree everywhere except between the two sets of row borders. The picture's rectangle and the `grid` record of `deep-desert-taxis.json` stay; the three committed taxi points are well inside row A and the file does not change. `NOTICE` names the five numbers as coming from the console's sector grid (MIT). Release: `kit/v0.9.0`.
- **The kit module gets `kit/gamefolder` and `kit/sessionlog`, the game's folder paths and the session markers of its log as a state machine, and the editor's log tracker, log path and save folders call them** (refs #565). `gamefolder`: the Saved folder, the saves tree, the config folder, the log path (and the log of a save in the standard tree), the saves found and the newest save. `sessionlog`: which line of the log starts or ends a single-player session or is the game's write of its database, the state that gives, and the log time of the end and of the last write; the two readings that differ between programs (a marker only at the start of a line's text; whether the write line counts) are a `Policy`, and the editor's is the zero value. The editor deletes its marker switch, its time parser and `logPathFor`; its waits after a write or an end, and its log follower, stay. A pin written from the old tracker (60000 seeded lines) passes, and the editor's tracker tests pass with only their field names changed. No user-visible change. Release: `kit/v0.8.0`.
- **The kit module gets `kit/schema`, the DDL of a real save with the table and column names and the shared SQL paths, and the editor reads its schema from it** (refs #563). `DDL()` (the 94-table schema, moved from `internal/save/schema.sql` without a change), the names of the tables and columns the SQL touches (each checked against the DDL by a test), the JSON paths of the durability and fillable records and the two actor states of a stored vehicle. Text and numbers only, no SQLite driver and no row of any save. `save.KnownSchema` is `schema.DDL()`, the evidence copy and its byte-for-byte test stay, and `internal/ops` takes the shared paths and states from the package. No change of behaviour. Tag `kit/v0.7.0` after this merges.
- **The kit module gets `kit/webguard`, the loopback, same-origin, content-type, navigation and response-header rules of a page served to this computer only, and the editor's request guard and window call it** (refs #561). `Host` and `Peer`, `AllowedNavigation` and `OriginOf` (moved from `internal/gui`), `SecurityHeaders` with a `Policy`, `SameOrigin` with an `OriginPolicy` and `JSONRequest`. The editor keeps its policy: its Content-Security-Policy text, the exact Host names it accepts, `Sec-Fetch-Site: none` passing, the case-folded `Origin` host, its session cookie and its error answers. A new test (`guard_pin_test.go`) pins the guard input by input (Host, peer address, Origin, Fetch Metadata, content type, response headers) and was written before the change; it and the existing navigation tests pass unchanged. No user-visible change. Release: `kit/v0.6.0`.
- **The kit module gets `kit/gamedata/regions`, the names of the Hagga Basin's 12 areas, and the editor reads its region names from it** (refs #559). `Name`, `Names`, `Maps` and `Parse` over `hagga-regions.json`, which moved from `internal/ops` without a change (the same git blob), with tests that pin the 12 names and their ids, the hash of the table and the refusals of `Parse`. The module's `NOTICE` now carries the licence of the Dune Docker console, whose data file this is. The editor's finder of the region of the nearest marker, the discovered-areas list and every number they give stay as they were; no user-visible change. Release: `kit/v0.5.0`.
- **The kit module gets `kit/gamedata/vehicles`, the game's vehicle catalog with the fuel tank of every chassis, and the editor reads its vehicle tables from it** (refs #555). The seven vehicle classes with their slots, templates and 398 module rows, and the 23 chassis tanks (`ChassisFuel`, `ChassisTanks`), moved from `internal/catalog/vehicles.json` without a change of the data. Refuel, Create a vehicle, the cargo limits and the places lists give the same numbers as before (the tests that list the tanks a second time pass unchanged); `ModuleName` stays in the catalog because it needs the item names; `tools/vehiclegen` writes the file at its new place. Tag `kit/v0.4.0` follows the merge.
- **The kit module gets `kit/gamedata/fillables`, the game's table of fillable items, and the capacity maps of Fill are built from it** (refs #551). 58 rows (the Literjons of every grade, the Decajon, the Dewpack, nine blood sacks, the worn stillsuits and the consumable `WaterPack_Consumable`), each with the most it holds, the liquid it takes and whether it is a container, kept as a snapshot of the game's own table (`DT_ItemTableFillables`) so that other programs can import it. `waterCapacity`, `stillsuitCapacity` and `bloodCapacity` in `internal/ops/fillables.go` are no longer typed: they are filled from the table by the rule they already followed (Water and container; Water and not a container; Blood), and `WaterPack_Consumable` stays out of all three as before. No change to what Fill does: every number is the one the maps held, and the tests that list them a second time are unchanged. `kit/CHANGELOG.md` has the `## v0.3.0` entry.
- **A third Go module in the repository, `design/` (`github.com/Project-Arrakis/tabr-tau/design`), with the shared design tokens as an embedded stylesheet** (refs #553). `assets/tokens.css` holds the colour, type, spacing, shape, elevation and motion values as CSS custom properties, dark by default and light by the system setting (or `data-theme`); the values are the palette the Tabr programs already show, so a program that adopts it looks the same. The Go API is `design.Assets`, `design.Tokens()`, `design.Handler()` and `design.Version`. It imports the standard library only (a test checks it), its contrast and theme tests run on the stylesheet, and it has its own CI jobs, `scripts/check-design.sh`, a `design release` workflow for tags `design/vX.Y.Z` and a `design/CHANGELOG.md`. The editor does not use it yet and its own stylesheet is unchanged.
- **The kit module gets `kit/durable`, one durable file replace that the save, the settings file and the game's config files now share** (refs #549). Temp file in the same folder, fsync, rename with retries, flush of the folder (a no-op on Windows): the steps that `internal/save/durable.go`, `internal/settings/durable.go` and the config writer each had their own copy of. The save and the settings file behave as before (same tries, same pauses, same test hooks). The game's config files (`Game.ini` and the others) were written with a temp file and one rename and no flush; they now get the flush before the rename, the retries while another program holds the file, and the folder flush, so a power cut right after an edit can no longer leave an empty file. The version of the module that carries it is `kit/v0.2.0` (made by the repository owner after this merges).
- **A second Go module in the repository, `kit/` (`github.com/Project-Arrakis/tabr-tau/kit`), holding the save container codec as package `savefile`** (refs #547). `Decode`, `Encode` and `ReadCapped` moved from `internal/save/codec.go` without a change of behaviour, with their tests, so that other programs can import them: a package under `internal/` cannot be imported from another module. The module imports the standard library only (a test fails if it does not), has its own LICENSE, NOTICE, PROVENANCE, README and CHANGELOG, is tagged `kit/vX.Y.Z` by the owner after the checks pass, and is tested by its own CI jobs (`kit`, `kit-windows`) because `go test ./...` at the top skips a nested module. The editor's `go.mod` requires it and replaces it with `./kit`, so the editor always builds the code in the tree. No user-visible change. See `kit/README.md`.
- **Export a base as the Dune console's base backup file** (refs #534, steps 1 and 2 of the issue). Player > Bases has **Export for console** on every base that is still standing, and the Base backups card has it next to **Export base backup** for a base the game's tool picked up. It saves the console's own `dune-base-backup` file (version 1): for a standing base the same entries a pick-up followed by the console's export would give (every actor in the backed-up state, building actors as fresh copies, only the pieces the totem owns, none of the totem's permission rows, a backup record built from the totem with its permission name, the owner as a placeholder; refused for a base with no owner), for a picked-up base the backup as it is. Read only: nothing in the save changes, it works while the game runs, the message repeats the counts of the file (pieces, placeables, stored items) and any notes about it, and the file is named after the base. The editor reads the file it made with its own console-file reader and compares the counts before it hands it out. New: `basefile.WriteConsole` (`internal/basefile/consolewrite.go`), `ExportStandingBaseConsole` and `ExportBaseBackupConsole` (`internal/ops/baseconsole.go`), `GET /api/bases/console-export?base=` and `format=console` on the backup export. Tests: a standing base against its pick-up, a golden round trip from the console fixture, every refusal, the routes and the page. The Blueprint (Solido) file is not part of this step. `docs/export-a-base-for-the-console.md`.
- **Go to a place lists the Deep Desert's three taxis and offers Harko Village and Arrakeen from every map** (refs #521). Under the **Taxis** heading (the headings and their order are unchanged): (1) whenever the chosen map is the Deep Desert, Go to a place lists the Deep Desert's three taxi service points, "Deep Desert Taxi Service (A2)", "(A5)" and "(A8)" (the grid cell each lies in, row A of the Deep Desert grid, which is static and the same in every layout), whether or not the save has found them. Their positions come from the game's own level data (the level DeepDesert_1_Travel: the three Taxi Service markers and the spawn location beside each), kept in the new `internal/ops/deep-desert-taxis.json`, made by the new `tools/deepdeserttaxisgen` and documented in `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md`; each choice fills X, Y and Z with the spot beside the taxi, and on the character's own map they are nearest first. They are marked `observed: false` until someone has stood there. A save's own marker for one of these points is not listed a second time, and the points are listed for no other map. (2) **Arrakeen and Harko Village** ("Arrakeen Taxi Service", "Harko Village Taxi Service") are listed from every map, each at the game's arrival point for it (#515), whether or not the save has found the hub's taxi marker; on another map the entry says "another map" instead of a distance. A hub is another map, so choosing one shows the existing warning about a move between maps even while the Map box says "This map", and Teleport sends the hub's map with it; the editor moves the character into a hub only to its arrival point, and any other position for a hub is refused. The Movement card's About this has a new sentence: whether a save edit moves the character into another map is not proven; do it on a copy first, with the game closed, and expect to need the in-game taxi if it does not work. Trading posts, bases, vehicles, respawn points and the one-drop-down behaviour (#506, #507, #515, #519) are unchanged. Tests only; **none of it is verified in the game**. The operator's check (on a copy, game closed): in the Deep Desert choose a taxi and Teleport; from Hagga Basin choose Arrakeen or Harko Village, Teleport, load the save and say where the character ends up.
- **Go to a place is one drop-down that is filled at once, with no kind of place, no filter box and no Find** (refs #519). In Admin > Movement the kind list (Base, Respawn point, My vehicle, Named places), the filter box and the Find button are gone. What is left is one drop-down labelled "Place to go to", filled by the page itself when the card is drawn and again when another map is chosen in Map and each time the card is drawn from the save (so the distances follow the character), under these headings in this order, a heading with no place not drawn: **Trading posts**, **Personal bases**, **Vehicles**, **Respawn points**, **Taxis**. Within a heading the places are nearest first, each entry as "name - distance". What each heading holds: Trading posts, the ones the character has discovered on the map, by their in-game names, at the game's arrival point (#515); Personal bases, the character's own **deployed** bases only (the totems the character owns, at the claim position, with the name the character gave them or the game's name for the claim structure, and the base's number), not the base backups (the saved base files, or what the game's own backup holds), not a base picked up with the game's tool, not another player's base and not a base the character is only a member of; Vehicles, the character's own vehicles that stand in the world (not stored in the vehicle backup or for recovery), by the name the character gave them or else the editor's own name for that kind of vehicle ("Sandbike", "Medium ornithopter"), never the blueprint name, and the vehicle's number to tell two apart; Respawn points, the character's own respawn points on the map whose beacon or other locator still exists in the world, named by the name given to it or else "Respawn point" and its number (a point the game keeps as bare coordinates is "Respawn point (group)"); Taxis, the hubs and the Deep Desert taxi service points listed since #512 and #516. A drop-down that is open does not close when a refresh finds the same answer; a chosen place stays chosen by its name and heading while it is still in the list, and if it is gone the choice is dropped and the X, Y and Z boxes keep what they hold (#506); an answer that arrives late for an earlier choice of map is dropped; a failed request is shown when the person chose another map, not on a redraw. A map with no place says "No places on <map>." Choosing a place fills X, Y and Z (the arrival point where there is one) and says which place is selected, as before; the warning for a move to another map is unchanged. The answer of `/api/player/destinations` is now the whole list (with its `groups`): the `kind` parameter is no longer read (nothing but the old page used it, so an old caller gets the whole list) and the answer has no `kind`; the `q` filter parameter stays for callers, and the page does not send it. This replaces the Named places drop-down of the first #519 change. Tests: `web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`, `internal/ops/goto_places_test.go`. Not yet checked in the game.
- **Go to a place: Named places is a drop-down that is filled at once** (refs #519). In Admin > Movement > Go to a place, choosing **Named places** no longer shows a filter box and a Find button: the drop-down of places (labelled "Place to go to", under the headings **Trading posts**, **Personal bases**, **Vehicles** and **Taxis**, a heading with no place not drawn, nearest first within a heading, each entry as "name - distance") is filled by the page itself from the map chosen in Map, and it is filled again when another map is chosen and each time the card is drawn from the save (after an action, and when the page reloads the file after the game saved), so the distances follow the character; there is no Refresh button, because the card already redraws itself on those events. A chosen place stays chosen across such a refresh by its name and heading while it is still in the list; if it is gone the choice is dropped and the X, Y and Z boxes keep what they hold (#506). A refresh that finds the same answer draws nothing, so a drop-down that is open does not close, and an answer that arrives late for an earlier choice of map is dropped. Choosing a place fills X, Y and Z (the arrival point where there is one, #515) and says which place is selected, as before; the warning for a move to another map is unchanged.
