Base, Respawn point and My vehicle are as they were, with the filter box and Find; choosing Named places takes them away and choosing another kind brings them back (what was typed in the filter box is kept), and a list of one kind is dropped when the kind is changed to or from Named places. The answer of `/api/player/destinations` and its `q` filter parameter are unchanged; the page just does not send a filter for Named places. Tests: `web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`. Not yet checked in the game.
- **Go to a place teleports to the game's own arrival point for each trading post and hub** (refs #515). The game has an arrival point for Pinnacle Station Tradepost, The Anvil Tradepost, Griffin's Reach Tradepost, Crossroads Tradepost and the Arrakeen and Harko Village hubs: the place where the ornithopter pilot's trip puts a character, taken from the game's own level data. In Admin > Movement, choosing one of those named places now fills X, Y and Z with that point instead of the position of the map marker, and the distance shown next to it is the distance to that point (so the nearest-first order follows it too). Every other place (the character's bases and vehicles, the Deep Desert taxi service points, a trading post whose key the names file does not have) keeps its own position. Several keys of one post all get that post's point. A hub is still offered only while the character is on its map, and the page's warning about moves between maps is unchanged; nothing claims that a save edit can change maps. The points are in the new `internal/ops/place-arrivals.json` (kept by hand, not made by `tools/placenamesgen`, whose output is unchanged), each with an `observed` flag: Pinnacle Station, The Anvil, Arrakeen and Harko Village match trips seen in single-player logs; **Griffin's Reach and Crossroads are only computed from the level data and have not been seen in use.** The Movement card's About this says which position is used. `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md` says where the points come from. Tests: `internal/ops/places_test.go`, `internal/ops/teleport_test.go`, `web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`. Not yet checked in the game.
- **Four Halloween 2026 cosmetics added to the item catalog** (refs #495): "Derelict Recovery Suit" (`Halloween2026_Radsuit_Variant`), "Contagion Cowl" (`Halloween2026_PlagueDoctorMask_Hood`), "Contagion Visor" (`Halloween2026_PlagueDoctorMask_NoHood`) and "Hot Zone" (`Swatch_Halloween2026`). The editor already knew what they unlock; they were missing from the catalog, so Give and Customizations > Unlock could not find them. They are ordinary cosmetics (no store entitlement, no DLC), listed under Other cosmetics and unlocked one at a time by name. The catalog now holds 2,570 items; the names are the customization names from the game's data. The same four ids are also missing from the console's own list.
- **Go to a place lists trading posts, the character's bases and vehicles and taxi hubs by their in-game names** (#500). In Admin > Movement the list that was called "Discovered map marker" is now **Named places**, with four headings: **Trading posts**, **Personal bases**, **Vehicles** and **Taxis**; a heading with no place is not drawn. It no longer lists outposts, camps, caves, resources, hazards, vendors and the other map markers. A trading post is a discovered `TradingPost` marker named by the `LocationKey` its payload starts with, which is a row of the game's own table `DT_StaticLocationData`: the new `internal/ops/place-names.json` says which key is which post ("Pinnacle Station Tradepost", "Crossroads Tradepost", "Griffin's Reach Tradepost", "The Anvil Tradepost", whose map is Mirzabah's Shadow and which the filter also finds as "mirzabah"; the three Deep Desert keys were first named "Deep Desert Tradepost"; they are taxi service points and are named "Deep Desert Taxi Service" under Taxis since #516). A post whose key the file lacks, or whose marker has no payload, is listed with its type and key, not hidden. Personal bases are the character's own totems (the owner rank), by the name given to the base or the game's name for its claim structure, at the claim position; the map's `HomeBase` markers are not used because the base number in them does not say which base a marker is. Vehicles are the character's own that stand in the world, by the name given or the blueprint name; a stored or lost vehicle has no place and is not offered. Taxis are the `TaxiService` markers, which sit on the hub maps (Arrakeen, Harko Village), so they show while the character is on that map; they are not offered from Hagga Basin because the editor does not move the character into a hub. Each place shows its distance, nearest first within its heading; two places with the same name (a save can hold several keys for one post) are told apart by the start of the key; the filter matches the name. The file is made by `tools/placenamesgen` from dumps of the game's tables, and `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md` says where each name comes from. The travel rules, the About text, the position rule, the cross-map warning and the choice that stays in the X, Y and Z boxes (#506) are unchanged. Tests: `internal/ops/places_test.go`, `tools/placenamesgen/generate_test.go`, `web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`. Not yet checked in the game.
- **Import the Dune console's base backup file** (#493). The base import (#475) now also reads the `dune-base-backup` file (version 1) that the self-hosted Dune console exports, as well as the editor's own `tabr-tau-base`. The format name decides which; a console file is converted (`internal/basefile/console.go`) to the same tables an editor file holds and then goes through the same strict checks, size and row caps, preview with its hash, backup first, new ids and one edit. The conversion turns the transform lists of pieces and totems and the location/rotation groups of actors into the save's plain columns, booleans into 1/0, an `fgl` entry into a row of `fgl_entities` and one of `actor_fgl_entities`, the console's transfer ids into the ids of the file's rows, and the `"!!act@N"` strings inside JSON into references to those rows (an `"!!act#N"` string, an id on the console's server, becomes the null reference and is counted; for any other kind it refuses the file); the owner placeholder becomes the save's character, the entities get 64-bit ids under the prefix of the save's latest entity (the console's are small numbers), and the actors are written in the dimension of the save's character (the console's are in a server dimension; the preview says how many were moved). Columns the save lacks (`owner_account_id`, `partition_id`, a null `exchange_id`, `item_id`, `vehicle_module_id`) are left out. The file is refused whole, naming the entry, for a kind a save has no table for (tax invoices, sinkcharts, stored Solido blueprints), an unknown kind, an unknown or missing field, a value of the wrong kind, an inventory of an item, a vehicle module or the market, and every refusal the editor file has. The preview says the file came from the console, with its name, map and counts, and the game build of the server that wrote it. Tests: a made-up console file (every kind, every refusal, the routes, the page) and, when `TAU_REAL_CONSOLE_FILE` and `TAU_REAL_BASE_SAVE_SQLITE` name them, a real console export of a base on a copy of a real save. **Not in game yet**: whether the game lists and redeploys an imported console backup is the operator's check (`docs/import-a-base-file.md`, UAT items 7 and 8). Exporting in the console's format is not decided.
- **Item catalog refreshed to the Dune Docker console's release v1.4.54** (#495): 7 new items, the Halloween 2026 building sets (`MTX_HalloweenEvent_Crucifixion_Patent`, `..._Fleshtube_Patent`, `..._LightPole_Patent`, `..._RadiationCrate_Patent`, `..._RottingCorpse_Patent`, `..._Skeleton_Patent`, `..._VacumBodies_Patent`), so a save that holds them no longer shows them as unknown items and Player > Building Sets lists and can give them (under Special & Promotional). The catalog now holds 2,566 items; nothing was removed or renamed, and the rules of #405 (first entry wins for the seven ids the console lists twice, no stack size or volume from the console) are unchanged. The source and the git blob are in `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md` and the third-party notice.
- **Import a base file as a base backup, phase 2: the page** (#475). Player > Bases > Base backups has an **Import a base file** picker. The file is read in the browser as text (a file over 16 MiB is not read) and sent to the preview route; the page shows the counts it would add (actors, building pieces, placeables, stored items, rows), whose base it becomes and the warnings (references set to none, actors written as picked up, items the catalog does not know, a base on another map, a different set of game patches, merged rank rows), and writes nothing. **Import** sends the same text with the hash the preview gave and asks no question, because it only adds rows; the result says which backup it made and that the game's Base Reconstruction Tool places it (and that it is queued while the game runs). A file the editor refuses shows the reason in the panel with no Import button. The explanation is in the closed About this of the card. New `docs/import-a-base-file.md` (what is rewritten, when a file is refused, the in-game checks); the README rows and the research note's phase table say phase 2 is built and its in-game checks are open. Tests: `web-tests/baseimport.test.mjs`.
- **Import a base file as a base backup, phase 2: the engine and the routes** (#475). `POST /api/bases/backups/import-preview` and `POST /api/bases/backups/import` take the text of a `tabr-tau-base` file and add its base to the save as a NEW base backup (every actor in state 5, the game's picked-up state), which the game's Base Reconstruction Tool should list; the pieces are not placed in the world by the editor. The preview is the real import run inside the edit and rolled back: it answers with the rows per table that would be added, the ranges of the new ids (entity ids as text, they are 64-bit), whose the base would become, how many references inside JSON were rewritten, and warnings, and it writes and queues nothing. The import is that edit kept: one edit, written after the usual backup or queued while a game process exists, no question asked because it only adds rows, and it must be sent the `fileHash` the preview gave (409 if the text is another file). No id of the file is ever used in the save: every actor, inventory, item, entity and the backup get new ids (the editor's own allocators; the item counter moves past the new items), every column that names one is rewritten, and so is every `!!act#N` / `!!itm#N` string inside the JSON (a totem's own id, a crafting station's item ids); the JSON is cut and joined, not parsed and written again, so key order and numbers stay as they were, and it is read back from the save after the write and must hold exactly the references that were written. A reference to an actor that is not in the file (the character in a queued recipe) becomes the null reference and is reported; any other reference outside the file refuses the file. Every player id (the backup's owner, the rank rows, who placed a piece, who edited a permission record) becomes the save's own character, the actors get no account, the buildings' owner stays empty as the game leaves it. The file is untrusted: it is refused whole, with the save unchanged and the reason, when `basefile.Check` fails, a table or column is not on the list, a reference points outside the file, an id is used twice, an entity is linked to no actor, a row has a NULL where the game's schema does not allow one, a class or name is not one of the game's, an actor is a player's or in a state other than 0 or 5, text holds a control character or NUL, JSON nests deeper than 100 levels or holds an id inside other text, a stored item is a Solido, there is not exactly one backup, the entity ids cannot be moved into the save (no room above its counters), or a row or size cap is exceeded (250,000 rows, 16 MiB; the routes accept a body up to 40 MiB, other routes still 4 MiB). Importing the same file twice adds two backups. The page for it follows (Player > Bases > Base backups). Tests: made-up saves (the same save, an empty-base save, a save with high id counters), the round trip (export, import, export again: the tables are equal modulo ids), every refusal leaving the save byte-identical, the preview leaving it byte-identical, the routes (written, queued, hash, body limit, session, hostile save), and a golden test on COPIES of a real save and a real exported base file that runs only when `TAU_REAL_BASE_FILE` and `TAU_REAL_BASE_SAVE_SQLITE` are set. **Not in game yet**: whether the game lists and redeploys an imported backup is the operator's check (`docs/research/2026-10-28-base-export-import-from-console.md`, section 7).
- **Export a picked-up base as a file, phase 1** (#475): Player > Bases has a **Base backups** card that lists the base backups in the save (the bases the game's own base tool picked up) with their actors, building pieces, placeables and stored items, and **Export base backup** saves one as a `tabr-tau-base` file (version 1, built like the care package kit file, `docs/export-a-base-backup.md`). The file holds the rows of 15 tables with their **original ids** (entity ids are 64-bit and stay exact: the page saves the file as text and never reads it as numbers), a manifest (the counts per table, the maps, the actor states, a summary of the applied patches, where the base names an id outside itself, what is not carried, warnings) and a SHA-256 checksum of the tables. Only the listed tables and columns are ever read; the account column, the character's name and any path stay out. It refuses, writes nothing and says why for: no backup, a backup with no actors or with an actor missing from the save, a Solido in a storage, an inventory that belongs to an item, a number that is not finite, invalid JSON, more than 250,000 rows, a file over 16 MiB, and a save edited while the file was made. The same save, time and editor give the same bytes. It reads the save and changes nothing, so it needs no confirmation and no closed game. `basefile.Check` reads a file whole and strictly (format, version, kind, tables, columns, kinds of values, counts, checksum) for the import that comes later; importing and exporting a base that is still standing are not built. Tests: a made-up save of the shape of a real pick-up, every refusal, determinism, the allowlist, the check against changed files, the routes, the page in a browser engine, and a copy of a save with a real pick-up (the counts agree with the card).
- **Create a vehicle from a template, slice 2: the page** (#401). Player > Vehicles has a card **Create a vehicle**: pick a class and a tier (the Mk number of the parts: Mk6 is the tier 6 template), change any part under **Change parts** (a unique or another grade of the same slot; an optional slot can be left empty), set the fuel, add items for the cargo hold (shown while the parts give one), then **Preview** (the rows that would be written; nothing changes) or **Create** (writes at once, asks nothing, backed up first or queued while the game runs). After a create a line says what was written, that the game has not loaded it yet, and where it is (next to you). Tests: a browser-engine test of the whole flow (a Mk6 buggy with three uniques and cargo items, a refusal keeps the draft, queued wording), the page words budget still holds. **Not in game yet**: the in-game checks are in `docs/design/2026-10-27-vehicle-by-template.md`.
- **Create a vehicle from a template, slice 1: the data, the write and the request** (#401). The editor can now write a whole new vehicle into the save from the game's own vehicle tables, as the console's Spawn Vehicle does: pick a class (Sandbike, Buggy, Light, Medium or Carrier ornithopter, Sandcrawler, Container vehicle), a template tier (the number in the template name is the grade of its parts: **Mk6 is tier 6**, the `T6` template, whose chassis is the item "Buggy Chassis Mk6" for the buggy), a module of your choice for any slot (a unique or another grade, in the same slot of the same model), a fuel fraction, a place (default: next to the character as Bring vehicle puts a vehicle, facing the way the character does) and items for the cargo hold. `POST /api/vehicles/spawn-preview` answers with the rows it would write and keeps nothing; `POST /api/vehicles/spawn` writes them in ONE edit, the ordinary one (backup first, or queued while the game is running); `GET /api/vehicles/catalog` lists the classes, tiers and the modules of each slot. The rows are the eight kinds Duplicate vehicle writes (actor, vehicles row, permission record and the character's rank 1, the Actor and VehicleInventory entities, the modules, the cargo hold and the component holds); every number that is not an id or a place follows from the modules: health is the sum over the chassis, body and cockpit modules, critical health is the chassis's, a full tank is the chassis's fuel capacity, the cargo hold is the storage module's slots and volume, and a new module has the table's maximum durability. After writing, the edit checks that the foreign keys of the touched tables hold as they did and works the numbers out again from the modules it wrote; a difference undoes the whole edit. `internal/catalog/vehicles.json` is generated from the game's tables (build 1.5.15.0; 7 classes, 398 modules) by `tools/vehiclegen`, which reads JSON dumps of the tables and is how the file is made again after a game update (`internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md`). Tests: the numbers of real vehicles of several classes are reproduced from their module ids, the template of a medium ornithopter equals the real vehicle's module set, the worked examples (Light T4_Inventory: 11 modules, health 500, fuel 300, a 10-slot 500-volume cargo hold; Buggy T5_Mining and T6_Combat), a Mk6 buggy with uniques in three slots and items in its cargo (35 slots, 3,500 volume), the refusals, the undo when a check fails, and a run on a copy of a real save (when `TAU_REAL_SAVE_SQLITE` names one). **Not in game yet**: no vehicle composed from the tables has been loaded; the page for it follows.
- **A limit that keeps the explanatory text from coming back** (#426; tests and docs only, no change to the editor): `web-tests/uitext-budget.test.mjs` draws every page and fails when one shows more than 100 words to read (tables, choices, buttons, labels, headings and the summary tiles are not counted), or a block of more than 45, before an About this is opened; it also checks that every About this is closed on a fresh load. The design note has the words each page shows, measured the same way before the sweep and now (the worst page went from 287 words to 84; Overview 287 to 56, Bases 258 to 5, Vehicles 221 to 23, Landsraad 205 to 15, Admin 201 to 16).
- **The automatic pass no longer starts before you can stop it** (#460): with the switch on, the pass used to run the moment the editor opened, before the page could be reached to turn the switch off (it wrote the save before: #419, #409). Now a bar at the top of the page counts down 15 seconds ("Automatic refill and repair starts in 12 s.") with **Skip** (no pass in this run) and **Turn it off** (the switch off), both single clicks that act. The countdown starts when the page first shows the bar, so a program that only calls the API never starts the pass. The new flag **`--no-auto`** skips the pass for one run and leaves the setting alone, and the Automatic refill card has **Run the automatic pass now**, which runs the ticked jobs once on request (the master switch is not needed; it never writes while a game process exists and is not queued for later). The write rules are unchanged: with a game process open the pass at launch still waits and writes nothing.
- **Landsraad: Complete chosen and Reopen chosen, and the faction list starts on your own faction** (#451): next to **Fill chosen** there are two buttons that act on the houses chosen in the Choose column. **Complete chosen** marks each chosen house complete for the faction in the faction list and sets that faction's progress on it to the house goal (a progress that is already at or above the goal is kept, never lowered); the winner and the time of the completion are written as Complete on one house does. A house that is already complete for that faction with its progress at the goal is skipped. **Reopen chosen** reopens each chosen house (not complete, no winner, no completion time) and changes no faction progress and no personal contribution; a house that is not complete is skipped. Neither asks a question: the result line says how many houses were completed (or reopened) and how many were skipped, and the usual backup is made before the write. Each is one edit (`POST /api/landsraad/complete-many` with `term_id`, `task_ids`, `faction_id`, `expected`; `POST /api/landsraad/reopen-many` with `term_id`, `task_ids`, `expected`); inside the edit the term is compared and the houses that would change are counted again, and a term, a house of an earlier term or a count that is not the one the page showed is refused with a conflict and nothing is written. The summary line under the buttons says how many houses each would change. The faction list, which Fill faction goal, Complete and Complete chosen use, now starts on your character's own faction (a choice you make afterwards is kept when the page is drawn again); a character in no faction gets the list as before and a line saying that no faction is chosen for them. Tests: `landsraad_chosen_test.go`, `landsraad_chosen_route_test.go`, `web-tests/landsraad-complete.test.mjs`.
- **A code review of the changes since 2026-10-23** (#206; docs only, no change to the editor): `docs/audit/2026-10-27-code-review-delta.md` lists the findings (none critical or high, two medium: #441, #442; nine low: #443) and what was checked and found sound.
- **A design note for the UI text sweep** (#426; docs only): `docs/design/2026-10-26-ui-text-sweep.md` states the rule (a permanent line must earn its place: state, results, errors, queued state and the one thing that can hurt stay; explanations, limits and background move to a tooltip on the control or a closed "About this" disclosure, or are drawn only when they apply), the one mechanism, and an inventory of every permanent explanatory line on every page (168 rows) with a decision for each, plus the plan of small pull requests that carry it out. No change to the editor yet; the pull requests that follow change the pages one at a time and keep every result and error line as it is.
- **The result of the automatic pass names what was switched off** (#352): after the editor opens, the sentence on the page and the card's last result say which jobs of the Automatic refill card are switched off ("Switched off: Repair vehicles, Fill your water."), whether the pass saved something, found nothing to do, or is queued because the game is running. Tests: a switched-off job writes nothing (file, backups, queue and write log unchanged); the pass that waited for the game to close reads the switches as they are when it runs, so a job switched off while it waited does not run; and the game-running gate is unchanged (nothing is written or queued while a game process exists, with any mix of jobs on and off). No change to the gate itself.
- **Care package, slice 3: Apply kit** (#417; W97). **Apply** on a kit gives it to the character in this save as ONE edit: the items by the Give Items path (the free slots, the stack sizes, the volume limit, the bound of 1,000 stacks, the grade and the augments), then the XP by the rule of Give XP (the level follows, one skill point per level gained) and the skill points on top (added to the earned and the unspent points together, never past 100,000 unspent). Like a give it puts in as much as fits and says what did not and why; a save with no level record refuses a kit that has XP and keeps nothing. Before it asks, the page runs a dry run (`POST /api/kits/preview`, the same code on the same rows, rolled back) and lists what would be given and what would not fit; Cancel applies nothing, and a kit applied earlier in the session says so. `POST /api/kits/apply` is an ordinary edit: it is written after a backup with one line in the write log (`Apply kit "Starter": 2 items, 301 units, +215 XP, +3 skill points`), and while a game process exists, or the check cannot say, nothing is written and the edit is queued (tests for each situation, #419). The write gate, the Give code and Give XP are unchanged apart from two helpers split out of them (`giveLinesInTx`, `addXPTx`). The in-game check is listed in the README table and has not been done.
- **Care package, slice 2: the page** (#417; W97). Player > Character has a new sub-tab, **Care package**: the list of kits (name, what is in them, XP, skill points) with Edit, Duplicate, Export and Delete (Delete asks first) on each, New kit, Import a kit file and Export all kits. The editor for one kit takes a name, items picked by their in-game name with a quantity, a grade and the augment slots of the item (the same picker and slots as Give item), XP and skill points; a quantity can be changed in place and an item taken out. What is typed stays when the page is drawn again, and a kit the editor refuses stays open with the reason. Saving, duplicating, deleting and importing change only the editor's settings, so they never wait in the queue and never show its hint; a file over 64 KiB is not read; a kit from a hand-edited settings file that cannot be used is listed with the reason. Applying a kit to a character comes in the next slice.
- **Care package, slice 1: the kit model and where kits are kept** (#417; W97). A kit is a name, up to 50 items (catalog item, quantity 1 to 1,000,000, grade 0 to 5, optional augments and augment grade, the stats the Give Items queue already handles), an XP amount and optional skill points. Kits are kept in the editor's own settings file (not in a save, so a new save starts with them), up to 20, and every kit is checked before it is kept: only catalog items, a name without path separators, markup or control characters, quantities and grades in range, augments that fit the item. New routes `GET /api/kits`, `POST /api/kits/save`, `/delete`, `/duplicate`, `GET /api/kits/export` and `POST /api/kits/import`: a kit file is a small JSON object (format `tabr-tau-kits`, version 1) of at most 64 KiB, read strictly (an unknown key, an unknown item or a bad number refuses the whole file and changes nothing; there is no place in it for a path or a command), and an import never replaces a kit (a name that is taken gets `(imported)` after it). These routes change only the settings file, never the save, so they are not queued and work the same while the game runs. The settings file may now be up to 512 KiB (it was 64 KiB) so the kits cannot make it unreadable, and a file the editor would refuse to read is never written. The page comes in the next slice, applying a kit to a character after that.
- Bugs carry the priority they were reported with: the labels `rp1-critical`, `rp2-high`, `rp3-normal`, `rp4-low` and `rp-unset` (the operator has not said; ask him at the next check), a required dropdown "How bad is it, as you report it?" in the bug issue template, and the rules in the roadmap's "How we work the roadmap": the working priority is never lower than the reported one without the operator's OK, and the roadmap lists bugs first within each working priority, ordered by reported priority and then by the date reported (oldest first). `scripts/roadmap-gen.sh` shows a Reported column; the roadmap sections are tables.
- The roadmap is ordered by priority: labels `p0-critical`, `p1-now`, `p2-next`, `p3-later`, `p4-parked` and `inbox`, a generator (`scripts/roadmap-gen.sh`, status tags in `docs/roadmap-status.tsv`) and the working rules at the top of `docs/roadmap.md` (documentation and labels only, no code; W70).
- The 2026-10-15 implementation audit register is reconciled with the code (#157, 93 findings): 69 fixed, 1 obsolete, 7 accepted risk, 16 open (nine wait for the owner or the organisation's workflow repository, two for the live game, three are the accessibility backlog of #180, one is a decision about per-save track numbers, one is test hygiene). The README calls the map tab "Saved map", as the page does.
- The 2026-10-07 implementation audit register is reconciled with the code (#24, 84 findings): 55 fixed, 12 obsolete (the review pane was removed), 7 accepted risk, 10 open (two need the live game, six need a decision, two are test hygiene). It names the pull request or test that shows each one; #301, #302, #304 and #324 closed the gaps found on the way.
- The 2026-09-30 design audit register is reconciled with the code (#24, F-01 to F-22): each finding was checked again against the current code, tests, CI and repository settings; 13 of 22 are fixed, 2 are accepted risks (the removed review pane, `foreign_keys` left off), 7 are open and low or waiting for the live game or the owner. Three statements of the design plan that were no longer true (authorizer and `trusted_schema`, the refusal of online-server folders, IPv6) are corrected.
- **Three guards written as tests** (#23, #180, register 2026-09-30 F-22 and register 2026-10-15 QA-3): the claim that the editor's own code makes no outbound connection now fails the build if shipped code uses an HTTP client or dials out (the one name lookup, of `localhost`, is listed by name); writing a config entry back with the value it has leaves the file byte for byte as it was (CRLF or LF, byte-order mark, comments); and `TutorialSet` and `TagSet` are tested to touch only the character's own rows (a mutant that dropped the player from either delete is caught). No behaviour changed.

- **The journal of queued edits keeps what the edits ran** (#251, audit finding DBA-10): a nil blob stays NULL (an empty blob stays a blob), text that is not valid UTF-8 comes back byte for byte (it used to be rewritten with U+FFFD), a single-precision float comes back as the value that was stored (it used to come back as the shorter decimal, 0.1 instead of 0.10000000149011612), and a ';' inside a quoted value, a quoted name or a comment no longer counts as a second statement, which turned the journal off and left the queue unable to be put back onto a newer file. A journal that cannot be written (its folder cannot be made, the disk is full, the rename fails) is turned off with the reason, which the page shows, and it is flushed to the disk before it replaces the old one. Before, a failed write was ignored and the page went on saying the queue was kept.
- **An edit's answer says whether it was written or queued, and a refusal has a code** (#262, audit finding ARCH-15): every edit request that leaves edits to write now answers with `"outcome": "written"` (the edit is in the game's file) or `"outcome": "queued"` (accepted, written when the file can be), next to the fields it always had. A refusal keeps its `error` text and now also carries a stable `code` with a status that fits: `changed_on_disk` 409, `queue_held` 409, `queued` 409, `game_running` 409, `read_only` 409 (these two were 400 when this was written; #334 made them 409), `bad_argument` 422, `not_found` 404, `bad_request` 400 (an error with none of these is still a 400 without a code). The page reads the outcome of the answer to decide whether a message says "queued" (it used to read a status that could be seconds old) and keeps the code and status of a refusal; it never reads the words of a message.
- The 2026-10-22 audit register marks the findings fixed by #274, #280, #287, #292 to #299 (E1, E4, E7, E8, E9, E11 and E12; #251, #254, #257 to #259, #261, #262): DBA-16 (table definitions and applied patches at open) and NET-8 (navigation hook of the native window) stay open with the reason written in their rows, DBA-8 is partly fixed. No code changed.
- **Provenance and notice for the data taken from the game's own tables** (#260, audit finding GRC-6): `internal/notices/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` and `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md` say which two embedded files (`stacks.json`, `customization_unlocks.json`) come from the game's item tables, what they hold, how big they are, and what is not recorded about them (the game build, the date, the tool, the licence position). Tests fail when the notice is missing or the sizes in PROVENANCE.md no longer match the data.
- The 2026-10-22 audit register marks the findings fixed by #270, #271, #272, #277 and by the repository settings changed on 2026-10-22 (required checks `windows` and `ui`, private vulnerability reporting), each with the evidence (#256, audit findings CLD-4, CLD-5, CLD-6, CLD-8, CLD-13, QA-18, QA-1, DBA-2, DBA-3, DBA-5; GRC-18 partly). `SECURITY.md` needed no change: the channel it names is now on.
- **The game-running check is made again right before the file is replaced, and the on-disk check is taken before the read** (#257, audit findings DBA-15, DBA-17 and ARCH-8): the check that no single-player session is active was made once, before the edits were encoded, backed up and written to a temp file, which on a large save is seconds in which a session can start and the game rewrite the file. It is now also made right before the rename; if a session began in between, nothing is written and the edits stay queued (the temp file is removed). The size and time of the save file are now taken before its content is read, so a game save that lands between the two is seen as a change (taken after, the new time was recorded against the old content and "changed on disk" could say no for a file newer than the loaded one). The automatic write no longer starts a second process check while it holds the save's lock (Flush has just made one, and the new check right before the rename is the one that counts); the rest of the lock (hash, encode, backup, rename retries) is kept on purpose and the reason is written at the top of `commit`.
- **Tests of the write path** (#262, audit findings QA-2, QA-4, QA-15): the answer of an attempt to write now has a test for each way it can end without a write (the edits cannot be put back, the queue is held with the conflict named, the game saved while the file was being written, the write failed, the save is read-only) and for the warning of a file that was written but could not be read back; a cascading delete (a bag and what it holds) is tested through the journal, at start and after the game saved; the journal's two size limits are tested; the flush route and the write after every edit request have tests of their own. No change in behaviour.
- **Decoded copies of the save are kept in a folder of their own and swept** (#259, audit findings SEC-3, SEC-12 and NET-5): the plain copy of the save the editor works on (it holds the account and platform ids) is made in `tabr-tau-work` in the temp folder, created for the owner alone, and named by the process that made it. It is removed when the editor closes, including on Ctrl+C in a terminal; a copy that a crash or a kill left behind is removed at the next start once its process is gone, and copies older versions left loose in the temp folder are removed when a day old. SECURITY.md now states, under the known limits, that the queue of waiting edits is a plain file in the roaming profile and is replayed without the console's checks, that the decoded copy exists, and what the cookie's reach is (and how to avoid it with `--web`).
- **The Live Map tab is the Saved map** (#268, audit finding UX-34): the tab and its card are called Saved map and say they show the save as of its last write. Marker names are words ("House Representative Argosaz", "Azurite Pickup"), the game's own id is in the tooltip, and the hover labels the coordinates. The picture follows the size of the canvas and the screen's pixel ratio (sharp on a dense screen, not stretched when the window changes). The view (centre and zoom) is kept when the page is drawn again, including by the automatic refresh, and Hide undiscovered markers only redraws instead of fetching the map again. There are Zoom in, Zoom out and Fit buttons; on the focused map + and - zoom, 0 fits and the arrow keys move; the canvas has an accessible name.
- **Review-era leftovers removed** (#261, audit findings ARCH-6 and QA-17; the pristine copy of the save was already gone in #287): the internal write function no longer has a `force` argument that nothing ever set (the game-running check cannot be skipped by any caller), the exported `Save.Commit` that only tests called is now `CommitForTests` in the test-hook file (a guard test fails if shipped code calls it), and the comments that still described a review pane or a review-before-save screen (the journal, the diff package, the batch give, the auto-refill setting) say what the editor does now.
- **Bulk base actions ask first, inline edits say what changed** (#254, audit findings GRC-2 and UX-29): Refill power, Refill base water, Repair every base and Clear sand on every base change every base on one click, since the review step went. The first press now turns the same button into "Yes, ..." for eight seconds (a press elsewhere, Escape or leaving the button cancels; a screen reader is told), the second press runs it. There is no dialog, so the keyboard is never trapped. A value edited in its box (stack, grade, reputation, a skill rank, a config value, a database cell) reports "old -> new" and how to put the old one back (type it again; the backup holds the file as it was). There is no undo of its own.
- **Editor accessibility** (#250, audit findings UX-32 and UX-41): a question is a named dialog (labelled by its title, described by its text); while it is open the page behind it is inert and does not scroll, and focus returns to the button that asked. Enter in a filter box (Inventory, Journey, Research, Crafting, Customizations, Database, the teleport finder) runs the filter and leaves the cursor in the box. Table headers that sort can be reached with Tab, used with Enter or Space, and announce their order. Buttons, inputs and selects are at least 32 px tall. The light theme's muted text and green are darker (the muted text on the beige tiles was 4.08:1; now at least 4.75:1, and a test keeps every light text colour above 4.5:1). The browser tab has an icon and names the open page.
- **Bounded work on the save's lock** (#257, audit findings SEC-11, QA-13 and NET-13): a statement typed into the write console is stopped after 15 seconds (it ran for as long as it liked, with the save locked) and nothing is changed; a give of an item that holds one per slot no longer reads the whole inventory again for every piece (500 pieces took 0.23 s, 2,000 took 5 s; the inventory is now read once per give), and one give makes at most 1,000 separate stacks (a bigger one is refused whole with a sentence that says what to do; no inventory the game builds has room for more); an edit request may take up to ten minutes to be answered, not the two minutes of the server's write timeout, so a very large save that is written late is not reported to the page as a network error.
- **Small screens and a loading line** (#266, audit finding UX-37): under 720 px wide the header and the warning banner scroll away instead of staying fixed (they took about 200 px of an 800 px screen), the header is tighter, cards and tables use less padding, controls and toasts fit the width, and the sub-tab rows scroll sideways instead of growing. Until the first view is drawn the page says "Loading the save...", and says so when the editor does not answer (with Try again). The empty file-name chip is hidden until the name is known.
- **A record of every write** (#254, audit findings GRC-3 and SEC-14): after each write the editor appends one line to `write-log.jsonl` in its own folder (`%APPDATA%\tabr-tau`): the time, how many edits, what they were in words, and the name of the backup taken before. The log holds no values from the save (a statement typed into the SQL console is not copied, a number of 15 digits or more is replaced, each description is cut at 200 characters), is created with owner-only permissions, and at 256 KB becomes `write-log.1.jsonl` with a new file started, so there are never more than two. If a line cannot be written the write stands and the banner says the record is missing. `SECURITY.md` now states the accepted risk of a write on every accepted request and the controls that stand in its place, and lists the log among the guards.
- **Specialization on a new save, and empty tables** (#264, audit findings UX-36 and UX-38): while no track has a number in the save, the Specialization page says so, says what to do (earn a little XP in a track in the game, then come back and assign it) and that keystones work now, and each disabled button says why. "Advanced: set a raw row" is folded and asks before it writes. Tables that can be empty say what is empty (no placeables, no building pieces, no decrees, no backups of this file, no skills in this school) instead of "Nothing to show here.".
- Documents and page text describe the write-as-you-go model only (#255, audit findings GRC-1, GRC-12, GRC-13, GRC-14, UX-28, UX-40, QA-16): SECURITY.md lists the guards that stand in place of the removed review pane; the SQL, delete and backup texts say that an edit is written to the game's file as it is made (after a backup) or queued while the file cannot be written; the older changelog entries below that describe the review pane carry a note that #238 replaced it; the audit register records what replaced the control, and `docs/control-removal.md` says how a change that removes a register control is handled.
- **Edits are written as they are made** (#238): there is no Review & save step. After every edit the editor writes the game's file (a backup first, taken once for a burst of edits and kept for the newest 60; the same checks as before: no single-player session, the file unchanged, an integrity check, a read-back). While the file cannot be written (the game is in a single-player session, the save's location is not confirmed) the edits are queued in the journal and written by themselves, a few seconds after the file can be, onto the game's newer file when it saved meanwhile. The header shows how many are queued, the banner says why, **Write now** tries again and **Discard queue** drops them.
- CI is steadier against runner flakes (#269): the PSScriptAnalyzer install is tried three times, and the Windows tests have 20 minutes (the job 30) after a runner with a slow disk took over twelve and failed a main run.
- The folder of agent worktrees (`.claude/`) is ignored, so a `git add -A` cannot commit copies of the repository (#263, audit finding E13).
- **A cell edited in the tables view is checked against what the table showed** (#259, audit finding SEC-7): the Database tables view addressed a row by its row number or key and sent only the new value, so a view that was out of date (the game saved, or queued edits were put back onto a newer file) could overwrite a different cell without a word. The page now sends the text the cell showed with the edit; if the cell holds something else, nothing is changed, the answer is a 409 with the code `conflict`, the page puts the old text back and loads the table again. The comparison reads the text the way the column does (a number column reads "5" as 5, so 5.0 matches; an empty cell is NULL or empty text). A caller that sends no expected text is served as before.
- **Give honors stack sizes** (#239): the game's own item tables list how much one slot holds (Spice-infused Fuel Cell 500, most resources 500, Spice Sand 2,500). A Give of more makes several stacks, fills a partial stack of the same item and grade first, and is refused whole when the inventory has no room for them; a stack edited past the stack size is refused.
- **UI pass, round 2** (#236): the editor gets one look: a header with a mark and the save's file name as a chip, tabs that read as levels (the active one underlined), rounder cards with a 11 px caps title, value tiles with a border, warnings and notes as banners with a coloured edge, and a consistent button set. The Player Summary now shows the character, status, level, XP, skill points, Intel, vitals and balances, and keeps the account and database ids (platform, Funcom and FLS ids, database player, account, controller, state) in a folded **Account and database ids** section: still hidden behind Show for the identifiers, as before.
- **Journal review fixes** (#230): an edit the journal cannot keep (a script) turned it off for the rest of the session and made Reload now refuse even with nothing pending; the journal is on again once the edits are saved or discarded, and Reload now only refuses when unkept edits are pending. Edits no longer build journal records at all while the journal is off or not enabled.
- **Unsaved edits survive a restart, and Reload now keeps them** (#230): every edit is also kept in a private journal in the editor's own folder (`%APPDATA%\tabr-tau\pending-<id>.json`, mode 0600, deleted when the edits are saved or discarded). It holds what each edit ran (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and REPLACE statements with their typed arguments, its description and its row count), nothing else of the save. When the editor starts, or when **Reload now** reads a newer file the game saved, the edits are run again one by one, each in its own transaction through the same write path as a new edit, so the same checks apply; an edit whose statements fail or change a different number of rows than before is not applied, it is listed (what it was and why) so it can be made again. Put-back edits appear in Review & save like any others and saving still needs the review, the game-running checks and an unchanged file. An edit that ran anything the journal does not keep (a script, DDL) turns the journal off for the session instead of keeping part of the edits, and the banner says so. The Discard button still throws the edits away, journal included. (Superseded by #238: there is no Review & save step. The journal now holds only the edits that are waiting for the file to become writable; edits that can be written are written as they are made, and the journal is removed once they are.)
- **The status poll no longer re-reads a changed save every few seconds** (#230): once the editor has found the file on disk to differ, it remembers that file's size and time, so the next polls answer at once instead of reading and hashing the whole save again. A slow status read is what let the automatic refresh decide from a state that an edit had already overtaken.
- **The Give queue survives a refresh** (#230): the items queued under Player > Character > Inventory are kept in the browser's local storage and read back after a reload, so a refresh of the window no longer empties them. What is read back is checked field by field (types, counts, lengths) before it is used. The automatic refresh also asks the server to discard only if nothing is unsaved by the time the request arrives (`ifClean`), so an edit made while the status was being read on a large save is not thrown away with it.
- **The Land Claim Editor removes cells** (#225): click an existing cell to mark it (red), then **Remove marked cells**. Guard rails, enforced by the server as well as shown in the page: a cell that holds a building piece or a placeable can never be removed (the same count Shrink uses; a piece with no owner link counts for every claim, so it keeps the cell), the totem's own cell stays, every cell that stays must remain connected edge to edge to the totem's cell (marking a cell that would cut another off disables the button and says so; mark them together to remove them together), and the request is refused whole when any part fails. It goes through the review pane and the usual backup. Checked by tests on a copy of a claim (empty cells go, a cell with a piece or a placeable blocks the whole request, a cut-off cell is refused); not yet checked in game. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- **The Land Claim Editor shows its compass and axes** (#224): the grid has N, E, S and W at its edges, the totem's two axes drawn as arrows marked +x and +y, a line above it that says which way they run for this base ("Column +x runs South, row +y runs West (the base's yaw is 90°)"), and a cell says its coordinates when you hover it. A base placed at yaw 90 has its grid turned a quarter, so the way to tell which side a cell is on is now on the picture, not an apply-and-look in game. Checked in game: with a base placed facing east (yaw 90), the cell added to the right of the totem in the editor appeared on the right in game, so the editor's direction of the cells is right.
- **Augment items are given with perfect rolls** (#219), as the console does: giving a standalone augment item (`T6_Augment_*`) to the player now writes its roll data with every stat roll at 1 (as many rolls as the augment has) and the best grade, 5, when no grade is chosen (#222; it was grade 1 at first, which showed weak stats in game; a grade you choose is kept). Before, the new item copied the stats of another copy in the save, so it could carry that copy's weaker rolls, or none. Effect indices on the copied roll are kept; gear is unchanged. The data shape is the one the console writes and the editor already writes for pre-applied augments; not yet checked in game.
- **Unlock customizations directly** (#215): the save does record unlocked cosmetics, in the character actor's customization library (a list of ids), and the game's item table says which ids each cosmetic item unlocks. The Customizations tab has **Unlock set**, **Unlock all sets** and a per-row **Unlock** that add those ids as one edit in the review pane: no backpack item and no 66-item limit. **Give as items** stays as the fallback. Neither gives a DLC or an account entitlement. Checked on a copy of a real save (the unlocked ids grew by the set, the actor's JSON stayed valid, items, vehicles and bases unchanged), then in game (#217): a plain dye pack (`MWpnDyepackAtre`) stayed and was usable, but a store cosmetic (`MTX_Atreides_Battlerifle`) was removed by the game when it loaded the save (the library lost it). So **Unlock set** and **Unlock all sets** now skip entitlement-controlled cosmetics (MTX_ items and ones that need a DLC) and say how many were skipped and why; the per-row **Unlock** still works for them, with a warning, in case the account owns it. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- **UI review, round 1** (#212): the header shows only the save file name (the folder holds your account id; the full path is the tooltip); the warning banner now sticks below the header instead of under it; the Reset all vendors confirmation says it resets every vendor; a SQL write confirmation shows the whole statement in a scrollable block and a read result says when it was cut at 1,000 rows; backups list a readable size and date; toasts are announced to screen readers; primary buttons and field borders meet contrast guidance in both themes; keyboard focus, hover and press states; native controls follow the dark theme; inline badges no longer render as heavy blocks; "the console" now says Dune Docker; the "(#96)" note and "row(s)" are gone.
- **Auto-refresh** (#210): when the game saves and the editor has no unsaved changes, it reads the new file by itself and redraws the page where it was (at most every 15 seconds; not while a question or the review is open or a field is being typed in), so a building placed in the game shows up without clicking Reload. Unsaved edits keep the banner and the Reload button as before. A checkbox in the header turns it off. (Since #238 "unsaved changes" means edits waiting in the queue, and there is no review to keep open.)
- Player > Inventory has **Fill hydration** (#208): sets the character's own hydration to the maximum (the base and current value in the character's attributes, kept as text like the game's). **Give water** still adds ten Cups of Water items, as the console's Give Water does; its tooltip says so.
- README has a verification matrix of which write features were checked in game, on a copy of a real save, or by tests only (#163).
- Test hardening after the mutation run (#181): session-tracker split reads and grace boundary, served-asset and start-up wiring tests, boundary and golden values, hostile fixtures that now feed skills, research, crafting, power, water and inventory, hermetic settings path, CI job timeouts, and runs on main are no longer cancelled.
- Documentation currency pass (#162): `README.md` has a "Restoring a backup" section and no longer says the safety checks are still open; the continuation prompt, the docs index and the status table of the plan describe the current state; this changelog had repeated headings and contradicting entries, which are merged.
- Tests that pin the save-write safety gates (#158, #159): Commit and Restore must refuse whenever the game may be writing (an active single-player session, an unreadable log or process list, a session that just ended) and leave the file, the pending edits and the backups alone; the open-time refill writes nothing during a session and never folds in someone else's unsaved edits; repairing one vehicle leaves the player's other vehicles alone and the repair route passes its vehicle and threshold on; Repair all to max, Clear sand and Reset vendors are checked with rows they must change and rows they must not. Each was shown to fail when its gate was removed. A guard test fails if shipped code calls a test hook.
- The Layer 2 implementation audit of 2026-10-15 (docs/audit/2026-10-15-L2-findings-register.md, tracking issue #157): eight hat reviews of everything built since 2026-10-07, 93 findings merged into 25 issues (#158-#182) with a STRIDE report.
- Player > Inventory has the console's **Give water** (#49): ten Cups of Water into the backpack in one edit. The console's other Refill Container behaviour is the existing **Refill containers** button.
- Teleport matches the console more closely (#67). Player > Admin > Movement has **Go to a place**: pick Base, Respawn point, My vehicle or a Discovered map marker, filter by name, and choose from the list (nearest first, with the distance); choosing one fills X, Y and Z. A new optional **Facing** (degrees, 0 looks along +X) turns the character; left empty the facing is kept. Places are the ones on the map the character is on. The page says the move shows when single-player is next entered, because the game keeps its own copy of the save in memory while it runs. Changing maps and teleporting from the Live Map are not done yet.
- Player > Specialization is the console's tab (#108): all five tracks (Combat, Crafting, Exploration, Gathering, Sabotage) are listed even with no XP, with XP, level, keystone state (Granted or owned/total), **Add XP**, **Grant Max** and **Reset** per track, and **Grant All Keystones** / **Reset All Keystones**. The console's database names its tracks; the save keeps only a bare number per track and nothing says which is which, so XP actions need each track's number. It is learned from the character's own rows: earn a little XP in a track, save, and assign the new row to the track on the same tab (kept in the editor's settings). Keystones are named by track in the save and work without any number. Levels use the console's XP table (level 100 at 44,182 XP).
- Character > Overview no longer repeats what the summary header above it shows (map, account, platform, database ids); it keeps the Solari stack, position and Journey progress (#96).
- Player > Building Sets and Player > Customizations are the console's tabs (#96): the item catalog's building sets (patents and sets) and customization items with a filter, the state of each, and **Give**, which puts the item in the backpack (the game teaches the set or unlocks the cosmetic when it is used). Building sets show **Learned** from the save's learned-sets table (including store-pack sets the catalog does not list, shown but not givable) or **In inventory**; the New buildable pieces list stays below. The save keeps no list of owned customizations that tabr-tau can read, so those only show whether the item is in an inventory, as in the console. Blueprints are tracked in a separate issue until a save has one.
- Player > Research and Player > Crafting are the console's tabs (#96). **Research** lists the character's research tree in the console's categories with product groups, a filter, and the state of each entry; **Unlock** marks it purchased and adds what it unlocks, a crafting recipe in the known-recipes list or a building set (and its placeable) in the building tables, and a purchased entry whose recipe or set is missing shows **Repair unlock**. Group markers (DA_GRP_) cannot be unlocked as one entry, as in the console. **Crafting** lists the recipes you know with their source and limited-use state, plus the ones your research tree can still give, with **Unlock** on those. The console finds its recipe list by guessing recipe names from the item catalog's schematics; against a real save that guess matched only a small part of the known recipes, while the research tree's RCP_ keys matched all of them, so the research tree is the list used here. Only the one entry is written (the rest of the research and recipe lists is untouched), new recipes have the game's own entry shape, and every change goes through the review pane. Checked on a copy of the real save (unlocking a recipe, its research and two buildings each changed exactly the expected rows); not checked in game yet. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Player > Skills is the console's Skill Browser (#96): **Skill Point Controls** (earned in total, spent in skills and unspent as stored, and *Set unspent points*, 0 to 100,000 as in the console) and the five schools with every skill's type, a rank bar, the points it holds and a rank control. A rank change writes the points that rank costs (the cumulative ladder in the console's catalog; checked against your skills: rank 2 of a 1-3-6 skill stores 3) and nothing else in the record. Like the console's control it does not touch the unspent points unless you tick *Pay for changes from the unspent points*, which takes the difference from them or gives it back and refuses when they are short. *Restore starter skills* raises a school's first Key skill and first ability to rank 1 and never lowers anything. A skill the catalog does not know (one ships in the game that the catalog omits) is listed with its points but cannot be given a rank. The save does not keep spent plus unspent equal to the total, so the three numbers are shown as stored. Not checked in game yet. The catalog is the console's `admin-skill-modules.json` (MIT, RedBlink), credited in the third-party notices.
- Player > Journey is the console's Journey Browser (#96): **Story, Contract, Codex and Tutorial** lists with readable names (from the console's catalog), the tree shown by indentation with each node's id on hover, status **Complete / Revealed / Incomplete** (Tutorials: Complete / Started / Not Started), a pending-reward marker, the number of gameplay tags each node sets, and a filter over names and ids. Nodes you have no row for yet are listed from the catalog as Incomplete. Complete and Reset work on Story, Codex and Tutorial rows as before (a node takes its children with it); they change the node states only, not the gameplay tags the console also sets, so a completed quest may not give its follow-on effects. Contracts are read-only for now: one is shown complete when every tag it sets is among the character's tags. The old Tutorials card is now the Tutorial list. Names and tags come from the console's `journey-tags.json` (MIT, RedBlink), credited in the third-party notices.
- Player > Admin gets two more of the console's controls (#96). **Faction Assignment** shows the character's faction and changes it to Neutral, Atreides or Harkonnen (the console's three; the save's Smuggler faction is not offered). It writes only the character's one faction row, stamped in microseconds as the game stamps the player's own change; reputation is not touched. **Repair Below %** on Repair Vehicle Durability (1 to 100, default 50 as in the console) raises only the modules of your vehicles that are below that share of their current maximum; modules with no recorded maximum are still skipped. Faction change checked by tests and not yet in game.
- Augments (#96), as in the console: **Character > Inventory** shows an **Augments** button on clothing and weapons that take them, opening an editor with one drop-down per slot (2 on clothing, 3 on a weapon) listing only the augments that fit the item with their best-grade effects, a grade for newly added ones (an augment already on the item keeps its stored grade) and a choice to also unlock the augment slots (the matching specialization keystones, found by name in your save; nothing else about specialization is touched). Choosing none in every slot removes them. **Give item** and the Give Items queue take augments and their grade too, and a plain Give no longer copies another item's augments. The written augment data has the exact shape of the augments in a real save (checked byte for byte against an item from one); it has not been checked in game yet. The compatibility data is the console's `augment-compatibility.json` (MIT, RedBlink; compiled there from method.gg), credited in the third-party notices.
- Give Items queue (#96), on Character > Inventory next to Give item: **Add to queue** collects items (name or template id, quantity, grade), the queue is listed with a remove button on each line, and **Give queued items** adds them all to the backpack in one edit (all or nothing: if one does not fit or is invalid, none are added), up to 50 at a time. The single Give button works as before.
- Player > Vehicles matches the console's Vehicles tab (#96): your vehicles with **Type**, **Lowest condition**, **Fuel** and location, and for the open vehicle (Details) its components with condition and durability and its **Cargo hold** (slots used and the items in it, read-only for now). Condition is each module's current durability against its current maximum, the lowest across modules; a module with no stored maximum shows its value and no percent rather than a guess. The save has no fuel tank size, so fuel is the current amount. Checked read-only on a copy of a real save.
- Player > Bases matches the console's Bases panel (#94, #96): a table of your bases (ID, Base Name, Base Type, Owner, Map, Generators, Building Pieces, Placeables, Coordinates) and, for the open base, the console's tabs **Power** (generators, windtraps and wind turbines with what they hold, how many are empty and the full amount; Refill generators; the automatic-refill setting), **Water** (each kind of water device with the water stored, capacity and fill; Refill base water), **Inventory** (the base's containers in the console's four groups, Storage, Refining, Crafting and Other, with Open) and **Land Claim Editor** (expand and shrink the claim, as before). Holograms (placement previews) are not counted, as in the console. The permission tabs are not mirrored. The structure tools tabr-tau has and the console's base row does not (structure health, repair all, clear sand, the placeables list and the piece types) moved to the base's **Structures** tab (the Extras tab was removed again, see the Changed section).
- Character > Inventory matches the console (#96): the character's carried inventories in the console's four groups, **Backpack, Character (worn gear), Loadout (weapons and tools) and Unique schematics**, each with its item count, a filter over name, item id, template and slot, the slots used out of the group's capacity, and a new **Augments** column showing the augments applied to each item by their in-game names (the id and quality are in the tooltip). The view used to list every inventory the character has under its own label (and called type 15 "Weapons / tools" and type 30 "Storage"); it now shows only the console's four, so emotes, contract items and machine slots are no longer listed here (the Database tab still shows them). Editing a stack or grade and deleting an item work as before.
- Give XP and Give currency (#96), on the same Quick rewards card. **Give XP** raises the character's XP and keeps the level data consistent the way the game does: the level follows the XP and a level-n character has n-1 skill points in total (checked against a real save at three levels), so each level gained adds one skill point to both the total and the unspent count; nothing else in the record changes; XP stops at level 200 (344,440). **Give currency** adds to the virtual wallets (Solari Credit, House Credit; a missing wallet is created), which are not the Solari Coin stack in the backpack. Both refuse a save without the record and go through the review pane. Checked on a copy of a real save: +5,000 XP raised the level and changed exactly the three level fields; not yet checked in game. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Give Intel (#96): a **Quick rewards** card on Character > Overview adds Intel for the research tree, never past 2,779 (the console's cap); the result says what was applied when the cap cut it short. It edits the plain `m_TechKnowledgePoints` number in the character's record and leaves the rest of that record alone; a save without the record is refused. Goes through the review pane like every edit. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Player Summary (#96): the header above the Player tabs, as in the console, read from the save: character, status, map, faction alignment, level and XP (levels from the console's XP table, up to 200), skill points (unspent / total), Intel (of 2,779), health, hydration and spice addiction, platform, Funcom and FLS ids, the database ids, and the currency balances. Solari Credit (the virtual currency) and Solari Coin (the item stacks in your inventory) are shown separately, as in the console. Health shows the current value and "max at least", because the save does not store the maximum. Read-only.
- Live Map tab (#101, slice 1): the console's Live Map from your save. Hagga Basin (and the Deep Desert picture) with your character, your own vehicles, your base, its storage containers and the game's own map markers from the save (grouped as ore and minerals, salvage, plants, enemy camps, places, trainers and House representatives, hazards), discovered ones solid and undiscovered ones faded; drag to pan, scroll to zoom, hover for details, layer toggles, and a table of the marker types on the map. It shows the save as of its last write, not the running game. The two map pictures and the coordinate conversion come from the Dune Docker console (see the notices; the pictures are Funcom's and not covered by the MIT licence). (Marker icons, spice fields and flour sand came in the next slices, below; the Deep Desert terrain and teleport from the map are still open, #67.)
- Live Map spice fields and flour sand (#101): the fields that are up in the save are drawn with the console's pictures, spice sized Large, Medium or Small by what is left in it (over 150,000, over 5,000, else small) and flour sand where exactly 60,000 is left, with their own layers in the legend. The save has no coordinates for them, so the position is decoded from the field id (three 21-bit numbers packed into it, as the console does; the console checked this against 350 known positions, 84% exact); a decoded position outside the map is dropped. The note under the map says the positions have not been checked against the game for this map: compare a field with the one you see in game and tell me if it is off.
- Live Map marker icons (#101, slice 2): the map now draws the console's own pictures for your character, base, storage and vehicles (by vehicle type) and for most marker types in a real save (ores and pickups, wreckage, camps, caves, sietches, trading posts, trainers, House representatives, fortresses and more); a type with no picture keeps its coloured dot. Map markers shrink when the whole map is in view so they do not hide it; your own things stay full size. The legend and the marker-type table show the pictures too. The pictures are Funcom's, copied as the console has them (see the notices). Two bugs found on the way and fixed: a marker type named like an inherited property (`__proto__`, `constructor`) was lost from the type table or could break the icon lookup.
- Optional refill when the editor opens (#63 step 2): the Bases tab has an **Automatic refill** switch (off by default, remembered in `%APPDATA%\tabr-tau\settings.json`; turning it on asks first). When on, opening the editor refills base water and generators and **saves straight away**, without the Review & save step, so the refill applies the next time the game loads. This is a deliberate exception to "Save only from the review pane", for this opt-in setting only (operator decision, 2026-10-08). The write still goes through the whole pipeline (single-player check, changed-on-disk check, integrity check, backup first, read-back); it is skipped while a single-player session is running or when there are unsaved edits, and if the write is refused the edits stay pending under Review & save with the reason shown. The refills no longer leave an empty pending edit behind when there is nothing to fill. (Superseded by #238: every edit is written as it is made, so the refill is no longer an exception to a rule; a refused or waiting write is queued like any other edit.)
- Shrink a land claim (#57 follow-up; verified in game 2026-10-08): the Bases tab claim card has **Shrink claim**, which removes every stored cell outside a chosen square (down to the totem's own cell). A cell that holds building pieces or placeables is never removed; the request is refused and says how many pieces block it. The piece-to-cell match uses the claim's own frame (offset from the claim origin, turned by minus the claim yaw, divided by the 5,120-unit cell, rounded; no axis flips), and the card shows how many pieces are inside and outside the claim. Verified: removing the extra cells of an expanded claim worked, and the game then added its own cells when Staking Units were placed (and raised the vertical level when Vertical Staking Units were placed), using the same two tables, with all pieces still in the totem's own cell.
- Land claim size (#57; verified in game 2026-10-08): the Bases tab has an **Expand claim** control per totem. It adds every missing cell up to a 3x3 .. 11x11 square around the totem (one cell is 10x10 foundations) in `landclaim_segments`, and can raise `totems.landclaim_vertical_level` (0 to 5). Expansion only: cells are never removed and the level never lowered; the square is always connected edge to edge. Same tables and rules as the Dune Docker console's Land Claim Editor. Verified: a one-ring (3x3) expansion of a claim loaded, allowed building outside the old claim, and was still stored after the game rewrote its save.
- Item names everywhere (QA, F-20 / #21): one embedded catalog (`internal/catalog`, 2,558 items (2,551 when it was added): template id, in-game name, category) feeds a single item picker used by Give item on the Player tab and by the storage Add item box (which had an empty list), and the item tables (inventory, storage, vendors, Landsraad rewards) show the in-game name with the template id on hover. A name shared by several items is told apart by its id. An id in your save that the catalog does not know stays selectable by its id, and a typed template id is still accepted. The names come from the Dune Awakening Docker item catalog (MIT, RedBlink; its licence is reproduced in the notices); only id, name and category are kept.
- Base upkeep and vehicle repair (#63, #56; verified in game 2026-10-08): Bases tab buttons **Refill base water** (cisterns, windtraps and blood purifiers to capacity, via `FWaterStorageComponent.m_WaterStored`; capacities from the Dune Docker console's table) and **Refill generators** (fuel and spice generators topped up to a full stack of 500, an empty generator gets a stack); Vehicles tab **Repair all vehicles** (every module's `CurrentDurability` raised to its `DecayedMaxDurability`). This first version raised each module only to its recorded maximum; it was replaced by the full-durability rule of #151 (see Fixed), which also restores the worn-down maximum and covers modules with no recorded maximum. Wind turbines and windtraps were added later (see Refill generators under Changed). Base "Repair all" is unchanged. The game loaded the edited save, showed the values, and kept them after rewriting its own save (water and fuel at the written levels, one Fuel Cell burned by a running generator, all repairable vehicle modules at their maximum).
- Refill containers (#55): a Player-tab button fills every water container the player carries to its capacity. The fill level is the item's own `FFillableItemStats.CurrentAmount` (an empty container has no such key). Verified live on 2026-10-08: the game loaded the edited values and kept them after rewriting the save. Capacities (Literjon 1000, Mk6 20000, Decaliterjon 10000, Hajra Mk1-Mk6 1500-3000) are read from the game; a water container with a fill record that is not in the table is skipped and listed, never guessed (an empty unlisted one has no record to recognise it by and is left alone). Blood sacks are left alone (#60).
- Tiered confirmations (S2 slice 2, F-10 / #11): the bare browser `confirm()` dialogs are replaced by an in-app question that states the consequence. Reversible edits ask nothing (they are pending, listed in the review, and Discard undoes them). Deleting an item, discarding, reloading, deleting a config line, restoring a config backup, resetting vendor limits and completing a Landsraad task ask first. Far-reaching actions (repair every base piece, clear sand, a raw SQL write, restoring a backup over the save) also need a word typed. Escape or a click outside cancels. Success messages now say "(not saved yet)" while edits are pending, so a green toast is not mistaken for "in the game". (Superseded by #238: edits are not "pending" or "not saved yet" any more; a reversible edit asks nothing, is written as it is made, and is undone by restoring the backup taken before it.)
- Native Windows window: on Windows the editor opens in its own window (Microsoft Edge WebView2, via `github.com/jchv/go-webview2`, pure Go, no C compiler) instead of a browser tab. A native Open dialog picks the save when none is found, startup errors appear in message boxes, WebView2's profile lives under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\tabr-tau\webview2` (never next to the exe), developer tools and the context menu are off, and a missing WebView2 runtime shows a message box with instructions (no silent background server). `--web` forces the browser. Closing with unsaved edits asks first: the window host destroys the window directly, so the native window intercepts the close itself (a Yes/No box, default No) and the page asks via beforeunload in browser mode. The build is a GUI-subsystem exe that reattaches to the parent console (only for streams that were not redirected) so `diff` and the other subcommands still print; a crash in window mode is reported in a message box; an injected guard refuses `window.open` and links that leave the editor's origin. The Microsoft WebView2 loader notice is included. CI cross-compiles and vets the Windows build and runs govulncheck against it; Third-party licence texts for every module in the binary are embedded in the exe (`tabr-tau licenses`, source `internal/notices/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, with a test that fails when a dependency has no notice). First checked on a real Windows machine in the informal operator run recorded in `docs/evidence/` (#44), and in daily use since.
- Review pane (S2 slice 1, F-10 / #11): the header button is now "Review & save". It opens a pane listing every edit and the row/value changes inside game.db; "Save to game" exists only there. The server enforces it: `/api/save/commit` needs the `reviewed` token returned by `/api/save/review` (a hash of the pending edits), and refuses a save with no token or with edits that changed after the review. A persistent banner (polled every 5 s) says when the game is running and disables Save with the reason. Errors stay on screen until dismissed. The UI still never sends `force`; the HTTP API no longer accepts it. A "changed on disk" failure keeps the edits listed and offers "Reload from disk". (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Pristine baseline and review data (F-06 / #7): the editor keeps an immutable decoded copy of the save as loaded, records every edit as a structured op (description, rows changed, time), and can compare baseline against working copy. New package `internal/diff` (Go port of `tools/snapdiff.py`, which stays for the live-test scripts): row-level add/remove/modify per table (rows matched and compared on raw values, typed keys), JSON-path diffs inside JSONB columns (integers compared exactly, fractions with a tolerance), hex diffs for opaque blobs, multiset handling for tables without a primary key, noise-column filter, row caps with true totals (clamped to 1000). Identifiers are masked in the output only, by default: personal columns (platform/Funcom ids, names, guild identifier, anything named like `*steam*`, `*identifier*`, `*email*`), any 15+ digit number whatever its type (text, integer, float, JSON number, JSON key), and long digit runs in edit descriptions; masking never hides a change (an edited `funcom_id` is listed as `<redacted>` -> `<redacted>`), and a test fails if a new column that looks personal is not classified. `GET /api/save/review` returns the edits and the diff; `tabr-tau diff <before> <after>` does the same for two files, read-only, with the Python tool's flags. A failed reload no longer leaks temp files or half-closes the save, and a failed reload after Commit or Restore disables further edits. The review screen that uses all this is the S2 slice above. (Superseded in part by #238: the review screen is gone; `tabr-tau diff` and the baseline copy remain.)
- Live-test protocol Phase E (T7-T9): can tabr-tau give armor, place it in the worn container, and keep item augments, using a reference armor set taken from a multiplayer character (template ids only, no identifiers). First run recorded: worn set, augments and loadout all survived a game load (direct SQL route).
- Documentation: single-player runtime analysis, dune-docker parity table, architecture and implementation plan (v0.2),
