  Layer 1 audit findings register, single-player file reference, live-test protocol, schema-only evidence, redacted
  client-log evidence (`docs/`).
- Tools: `tools/snapshot.ps1`, `tools/send-snapshot.ps1` (hash-verified scp), `tools/snapdiff.py` (redacted diff),
  `tools/snapsummary.py` (redacted fingerprint).
- Governance: CI (gofmt, go vet, staticcheck, race tests, govulncheck, PSScriptAnalyzer, shellcheck, commit-message
  policy, shared security scan), `.gitleaks.toml` with identifier rules, PR and issue templates, `CODEOWNERS`, Dependabot.

- Tests: `internal/testsave` builds test saves from the REAL game schema (94 tables, STRICT, CHECKs, foreign keys, the game's trigger): empty, filled, hostile (every TEXT column holds markup/quote/CSV/SQL breakout text) and a consistent one-player world that mirrors the game's controller/pawn/player-state linkage. A drift test keeps the embedded DDL identical to `docs/evidence/single-player-schema.ddl.sql` (F-02 / #3).
- Tests: a contract test: every read path in `ops` runs against a populated real-schema save and any SQL error, even one the code swallows, fails the test (new `save.SetSQLErrorHook`, test-only). A self-test proves the hook sees errors.
- Tests: Give-item, full-backpack and Solari tests on the real schema, each ending with `integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check` and the sequencer invariant.
- **Clear this container** (#338, part of #112): a button under the item list of the backpack, the worn gear and the loadout (Player > Character > Inventory) and of every base container (Player > Bases > Inventory > Open) removes every item in that one container, with everything attached to each item, through the same removal as the single-item delete (a bag takes its inventory and what is in it). It asks twice: the button turns into "Yes, clear this container" for a few seconds (and ignores a second press that comes too soon), then a question names the container and the number of items and wants that number typed. The Solari Coin stack is kept unless "Also remove currency" is ticked. The unique schematics cannot be cleared. It is one edit: one backup, one line in the write log ("Cleared container 12 (7 items)"), one entry in the queue while the game runs, and the same refusals as the other edits. The container's items are read inside the edit and the typed count is sent with the request: if the container holds another number by then (or the game changed it while the clear was queued) the clear is refused (409, code `conflict`, or held) and nothing changes. A container with nothing to remove is not an edit and says so. The route is `POST /api/inventories/clear` with `inventory_id`, `count`, `nested`, `ids_hash` and optional `include_currency` (the last three fields came with review R6, see Fixed). Tests run on a real-schema save: only that container changes, nothing item-linked is left behind, the other containers are untouched, the integrity check passes, and the queued edit is put back whole.
- **Reset all skills** (#339, part of #112, the Skills part of Reset Progression): Player > Skills has a "Reset all skills to rank 0 (N)" button. The console's single Reset Progression is not one button here: each area that holds the data gets its own reset, with its own confirmation, and pressing each of them is the whole reset. This one puts every skill of the five schools that has points at rank 0, by the same edit as the per-skill Set rank (so the points stay consistent in the same way; the "pay for changes" box also gives the points back to the unspent points). Hidden skills, skills the catalog does not know, the level and the XP are not touched. It is one edit (one transaction, one backup, one write-log line "reset skills (N changed)"); the question names the area and the count and asks for a typed sentence ("reset 12 skills"), ignores an OK that comes within 400 ms of it opening, and says that Database > Save backups restores the save as it was. The request carries the count the page showed; the editor counts again inside the edit and refuses with a 409 `conflict` if it changed. With nothing to reset the button is off and the request is a no-op that writes nothing.
- **Reset level and XP, and reset the XP of the specialization tracks** (#339, part of #112): Character > Overview has "Reset level and XP (N XP)" and Player > Specialization has "Reset XP of all known tracks (N)", each with its own question, typed sentence ("reset N xp", "reset N tracks", with the number the page showed), count check and no-op, as the Skills reset. The level follows the XP, so the character's XP goes to 0; Give XP adds one skill point to the total and to the unspent points for each level gained, so the reset takes the same number back (only as far as the points are unspent, see the entry of review R6 under Fixed) and leaves skills already bought, points that did not come from levels and the rest of the record as they are. The tracks reset is the per-track Reset applied to every track whose number the editor knows (one edit, "reset specialization XP (N changed)"); rows of tracks whose number is not known yet are left alone and the question says how many, and keystones are not touched. Give XP and the level reset share their read and write, and the per-track Reset and the reset of all tracks share their delete.
- **Reset journey, and a Reset research button that says why it is off** (#339, part of #112): Player > Journey has "Reset journey (N steps, M tutorials)", with the same question, typed sentence ("reset 6 journey items"), count check and no-op as the other resets. It is the Reset button of each row applied to every finished Story and Codex step (the step and its children are marked not finished and revealed) and every started or finished tutorial (back to not started), in one edit ("reset journey (N changed: ...)"). Waiting rewards, tags and contracts (which are derived from tags) are not touched, so it neither grants nor takes away a reward; the question says that whether the game gives a reward again when a step is finished again is not known. The per-row Reset and the reset of the whole journey share their writes. Player > Research has a disabled "Reset research" button whose tooltip and note give the reason: the save does not say which research, recipes and building sets a new character already has, so there is no state to reset to, and the editor has no safe way to take a recipe or building set back out. This completes the Reset Progression part of #112: one reset on each tab that holds the data (Skills, Character > Overview, Specialization, Journey), none for Research, no single button for everything.
- **Give Blood Marks** (#344): the currency of the Scourge event (Halloween 2026), a stack of the item `HalloweenCoin` in the backpack that the game names Blood Marks. Player > Character > Overview, in the Money card, shows how many the backpack holds (the sum of its `HalloweenCoin` stacks, 0 when there is none), an amount box (1 to 50,000, default 100) and a "Give Blood Marks" button. It is the general give (the same code as Give item, #345): the Blood Marks go onto the stack that is there first, up to the 50,000 a slot holds, then onto a new stack in a free slot, and as much as fits is given; the page says "Gave 100 of 500 Blood Marks: the backpack is full." when less went in, and a give where not one fits is refused (409, `no_room`) and writes nothing. It is one edit: one backup, one line in the write log ("gave 500 Blood Marks", or "gave 100 of 500 Blood Marks"), queued while the game runs. The stacks and the free slots are read inside the edit, so a queued copy is held if the game changed them meanwhile. The control is always shown (there is no date check) and has no "set amount" box, as the Solari control has none. The route is `POST /api/player/blood-marks` with `amount`; `GET /api/player` carries `bloodMarks` and `bloodMarksMax`. The embedded item catalog has no name for `HalloweenCoin`, so the page names it and shows the item id in the tooltip. Tests run on a real-schema save: 420 + 500 = 920, a new stack when there is none, the limit and a full backpack, nothing else in the save changes, the integrity check passes, the write-log line, and the queue held / written / put back.
- **Fill blood sacks** (#346, part of #60): Player > Character > Inventory has a "Fill blood sacks" button under Give item, with a count ("3 of 4 blood sacks are not full"). It sets every blood sack the character carries (backpack, worn gear and loadout, the same scope as Refill Literjons) to its capacity with the fill type Blood, in one edit: one backup, one line in the write log ("Filled 5 blood sacks"), one entry in the queue while the game runs. A blood sack keeps its fill in the item's own stats, `FFillableItemStats.CurrentAmount`, like a Literjon keeps its water; only those two values are written, so the sack's other stats (durability, customization) stay as they were, and a sack at or above its capacity is not rewritten. The capacities come from the largest amounts seen in a save, not from the game's data: `Bloodsack_Unique_Durable_06` 30000 and `Bloodsack_01` 3000. A blood sack of any other template (its capacity is not known), and one that holds something other than blood, is left alone and counted: "N blood sacks of an unknown kind were left alone". The answer lists how many of each kind were filled. Refill Literjons is unchanged and still leaves blood sacks alone. The routes are `POST /api/player/fill-blood-sacks` and the read-only `GET /api/player/blood-sacks` (the count). Tests run on a real-schema save: empty, partly filled, full, absent and odd fill records, both known templates, an unknown template, a sack holding water, a sack in a base chest, the other stats keys byte for byte, the other tables unchanged, the integrity check, and the queued edit held or written. The fill has been tested only; whether the game loads a sack filled this way has not been checked in the game.
- **Refuel all my vehicles** (#349): Player > Vehicles has a "Refuel all my vehicles" button with a count ("3 of 5 vehicles are not full"). It sets the fuel of every vehicle the character owns (the owner rank, the same rule as Bring to me and Repair; another owner's vehicles and the world's vehicles are never touched) to a full tank, in one edit: one backup, one line in the write log ("refuelled 3 vehicles"), one entry in the queue while the game runs. A vehicle keeps its fuel in `FVehicleComponent.CurrentFuel` of its entity record, in the game's own units, and the save stores no tank size, so the full tanks are a table in the editor, measured by filling a vehicle to a known value and reading the fuel gauge in the game: Sandbike 100, Buggy 240, Light Ornithopter 300, Medium Ornithopter 750, Sand Crawler 750, Transport Ornithopter 2500. The Container Vehicle's tank has not been measured, so a vehicle of that kind, or of any other class that is not in the table, is left alone and counted ("N vehicles of an unknown kind were left alone"); so is a vehicle with no fuel value. Only that one number is written (the other keys of the record stay as the game wrote them, and so does the actor's own data), a fuel amount already at or above the tank is never lowered, and the answer lists how many of each kind were filled. The routes are `POST /api/vehicles/refuel` and the read-only `GET /api/vehicles/fuel` (the count). Tests run on a real-schema save: each kind filled to its tank, a full vehicle, a value above the tank, an unknown kind, a vehicle with no entity and one with no fuel value, another owner's and a world vehicle, the other keys of the record byte for byte, the other tables unchanged, the integrity check, and the edit written, queued while the game runs, and held with the queue. The fill has been tested only; whether the game accepts a tank filled this way has not been checked in the game. The Vehicles page no longer says that fuel cannot be edited.
- **Fill all liquids** (#349): Player > Character > Inventory has a "Fill all liquids" button under the Blood sacks card, with the count of what is not full ("Not full: 2 water containers, 1 blood sack, 3 vehicles") and a box "Also refuel my vehicles" (on by default). One press does the Refill Literjons fill (Literjons, stillsuits and the other water containers), the Fill blood sacks fill and, with the box ticked, Refuel all my vehicles, as ONE edit: one backup, one line in the write log ("filled all liquids: 2 water containers, 1 blood sack, 3 vehicles", without the vehicles part when the box is off), one entry in the queue while the game runs. The three buttons and this one share the same functions (each fill is written once and takes the open edit), so the capacities, what is left alone (containers, sacks and vehicles of an unknown kind, a sack that holds something other than blood) and the rule that a value is never lowered are the same everywhere; the three single buttons behave as before. A failure in any part puts all of them back. The routes are `POST /api/player/fill-all-liquids` (body `{"vehicles": true|false}`, anything else is refused with 422 `bad_argument`) and the read-only `GET /api/player/liquids` (the counts). Tests on a real-schema save: all three parts in one edit with one log line, the combined fill leaving the same rows as the three buttons one after the other, the box off, a forced failure in the last part putting back the first two, a box that is not true or false, nothing to fill, and the routes written, queued and refused. The fill has been tested only; the Fill blood sacks and Refuel parts have not been checked in the game yet.
- **Automatic repairs and fills when the editor opens** (#352): the Automatic refill card has a checkbox per job besides the master switch: base water, base power (both as before), repair vehicles, repair worn equipment and loadout, fill water containers, fill the character's water, fill blood sacks, refuel vehicles. Every job is on by default, the repairs too, and the master switch is still off by default; a settings file from before gives each job its default. The jobs run as one edit (one transaction, one backup, one write-log line, "automatic pass: ..."); nothing to do writes nothing; if a job fails everything is undone and the card names the job and the error; it is still skipped while a single-player session is running. It acts only on what the character owns or carries; the items in the backpack are not repaired (only what is worn and the loadout). The card says per job what the last automatic save did.
- **The free space of the drive is checked before a write** (#39, finding D8c): a write (an edit, or a restore) needs room for a backup of the file on disk and the new file, and 16 MB more. When the drive has less, the write is refused before anything is written, nothing is changed, the edit stays queued and the message says how much room is needed and how much is free. Where the free space cannot be found out the write is tried, as before; a disk that fills up in between still fails the backup or the temp file before the save is replaced.
- **A save whose tables are not defined as the editor expects is told at open** (#257, audit finding DBA-16, and #20): the definitions of the tables of the save are compared with the game's schema the editor was built against. A difference (a game update that added a column, a table the editor does not know, a table that is missing) shows as a notice under the header naming the tables; it does not make the save read-only and editing continues. The set of applied patches is not compared: the editor has no list of patches to compare it with.
- **The Landsraad tab knows which recorded contribution is the character's, and Fill to this tier writes it** (#380, follows #363): the player id of `landsraad_task_player_contributions` is the id of the character's `player_state` row (the same number as the controller and as `player_faction.actor_id`); the other ids are the simulated guilds. In the save this was settled on, the character's row is the only one whose id is not a guild, it equals the sum of the three `landsraad_task_progress_player` rows tied to that id, and `landsraad_house_rewards` holds the five tiers of that house under the same id. The tab now shows **Your contribution** (whole, above the goal too, with how many tiers are reached) and keeps the simulated players' rows apart as "other players". Each tier says "not reached", "reached, no delivery recorded in this term" or "reached, recorded as delivered" (a delivery is the save's reward row for that house and item, updated since the term began and of at least the reached tiers' amounts; the rows add the amounts of every term, so two equal items of one house cannot be told apart). **Fill to this tier** raises the character's contribution to that tier (up to 6,000), never lowers it, and leaves the faction's progress and the reward rows alone, unless the box "Also set my faction's progress on that house to its goal" is ticked (it raises the faction's progress to the goal, never lowers it). It is one edit, refused for a character in no faction and for a house of an earlier term. The task board lists the current term's houses only (the save keeps the boards of earlier terms, and a fill of those changes nothing the game uses). Whether the game hands out a tier when it loads the save after a fill is not known from a save and is not claimed.
- **The fill level of liquid containers is shown in the Inventory** (#367): the Player > Character > Inventory table has a Liquid column for every Literjon, stillsuit, blood sack and other container that holds a liquid: "Water 11,000 / 20,000 (55%)", "Blood empty", or the amount alone with "capacity unknown" when the editor has no verified capacity for that template (it never guesses one); an amount above the known capacity is marked. The card header counts the liquid containers and how many are not full. The same column is in the item list of an opened base container. Read-only: nothing is written.
- **Landsraad: choose several houses and single tiers, and fill them in one edit** (#364, builds on #380): every house on the task board has a box, and choosing a house chooses all five of its tiers (they are shown ticked and locked); a single tier of a house can be chosen on its own. **Choose all houses** and **Clear the choice** act on the whole board, and a line says how many houses are chosen and on how many a fill would change anything. **Fill chosen** raises the character's own contribution on each chosen house to the highest tier chosen for it (a house chosen whole goes to its last tier, 6,000 in the term read so far), never lowers a contribution that is already at or above it, and leaves the faction's progress and the reward rows alone unless the box "Also set my faction's progress on the house to its goal" is ticked. It asks first and wants the number typed ("fill 4 houses"); the page reads the board again when the button is pressed, and the request carries the term and the number confirmed. Inside the edit the term is compared and the houses that would change are counted again with the rules of the single fill: a term that is no longer current, a house of an earlier term, or a count that is not the confirmed one is refused with a conflict and nothing is written, and any refusal rolls the whole edit back (it is all the houses or none). One backup, one line in the write log, one entry in the queue; at most 100 houses per request. Reward rows are never written, and whether the game hands out a tier after it loads the save is not known from a save.
- **Config: put one setting, or all settings, back to the game's default** (#348): on the Config tab, `ServerCustomSettings.ini` shows the default of each setting (with its allowed range or words) and a Reset button on every row that is not at it (off, with the reason, when it already is or when no default is known). "Reset all to defaults" asks with a typed sentence that names the file and the number of values that change, and is one edit: one backup of the file, one line in the write log (no values in it), nothing written when nothing differs, and a refusal when the page's count is out of date. The defaults are the Dune Docker console's table (45 keys, MIT, RedBlink; notice added): `DifficultyLevel`, `ColdBuildupRate` and keys added by hand have no verified default and are never reset. The default is written as the game writes it (`1.000000`), not by removing the key. Other config files have no Reset.
- **Give item stops at the inventory's volume limit** (#357): Give item, the give queue, Blood Marks and Give into a base container now add up the volume of what the inventory holds (the unit volume from the game's own item tables, new file `internal/catalog/volumes.json` with 4,210 items, 103 of 106 values equal to the Dune Docker console's measured ones; a row's own `volume_override` counts per unit, as the game reads it) and give as much as fits under `max_item_volume`, saying how many and why ("Gave 340 of 500 Aluminum Ingot: the inventory is full (volume)"). A give only calculates: the rows it writes keep `volume_override` NULL. An item with no known volume (17 ids that two game tables list with different sizes are left out rather than guessed, and ids that are not in the tables), or an inventory with no volume limit recorded, is limited by slots and stacks only and the answer says that the volume was not checked. Items already in the inventory with an unknown volume count as none and are said. Blood Marks and Solari take no volume. Provenance and notice updated.
- **Reset research** (#112, the Research part of Reset Progression): Player > Research has "Reset research (N entries)", with the same two-step question, typed sentence ("reset 5 research entries"), count check and no-op as the other resets. It gives the research back to what a new character has, which was unknown until a save of a brand-new character was read: its research list has 50 entries of which exactly four are bought (the mining tool, the power pack, the health pack and the scrap metal knife recipes), it knows exactly those four recipes, and nothing else. Every other bought entry goes back to not bought and marked new, the recipes those entries gave (RCP_x gives x) are taken out of the known recipes and the building sets they gave (BLD_x) out of the learned sets, with the "new piece" row the unlock adds; Intel is not given back (the save does not say what an entry cost). A recipe known from a schematic whose entry is not bought, a recipe or a set the research tree does not list (store packs), the items, the placed buildings and the skills are not touched. The question counts the entries, recipes and sets. UAT: reset a copy of your save, load it, and check that the research tree shows only the starting entries, that the removed recipes and sets are not offered, and that Unlock all research brings them back.
- **Teleport can choose the map** (#67): Player > Admin > Movement has a **Map** choice (this map, Hagga Basin or The Deep Desert, the two maps the console offers for an offline player). **Find** lists the bases, respawn points, your vehicles and discovered markers of the chosen map (on another map there is no distance, since it means nothing across maps), and a place on another map fills X, Y and Z for that map. Teleporting with another map chosen moves the character's three actors (controller, body and player state) to that map and position together, because that is how every save the game wrote holds them (checked in saves the game wrote, including one with the character in the Deep Desert), and sets the facing on the body when one is given. On the same map only the body moves, as before (that is what was checked in the game). The position must lie inside the destination map's bounds (Hagga Basin's are smaller, so a Deep Desert position is refused for it), and a save missing one of the three actors is refused rather than half moved. The social hubs and the dungeons are not offered. UAT: on a copy of your save, move to a Deep Desert base from Hagga Basin and back, load the game and check the position and the map, and that it still holds after the game's own autosave. Not checked in game.
- A status note for the Player tab (#96), `docs/design/2026-10-24-player-tabs-status.md`: the console's 12 tabs against what the save holds and what is built. Eleven tabs are built (Admin's items are on the tabs that hold the data); Blueprints waits for a save that has a blueprint (#111); contracts stay read-only in the Journey because completing one has game-side effects that were not checked.
- **Duplicate this vehicle** (#112, the console's Spawn Vehicle): Player > Vehicles has a **Duplicate** button on each of your vehicles. It asks first, then makes a copy next to you (800 along x and 150 up, where Bring puts a vehicle): the same kind, turn, properties and modules with the reference's condition, an empty cargo hold, and yours (a permission record with your rank). The editor cannot ask the game to spawn a vehicle, so the copy is made of the rows a vehicle is in a save the game wrote, read from a real one: the actor, the vehicles row, the permission record and rank, two entities (Actor and VehicleInventory) under new ids that keep the reference's prefix and the distance between them and sit above every counter that prefix already has, the modules, and the inventories with their component hashes. The abandoned time of the copy is now. A vehicle with an inventory that hangs from an item or a module is refused rather than copied blindly. Ids are chosen inside the edit and named in the insert, so an edit put back onto a newer file is the same edit. UAT: duplicate your Sandbike on a copy of your save, load it, find the copy next to your character, ride it, save and reload; check that the game did not remove it and that the original is unchanged. Not checked in game.
- A status note for the Player tab (#96), `docs/design/2026-10-24-player-tabs-status.md`: the console's 12 tabs against what the save holds and what is built. Eleven tabs are built (Admin's items are on the tabs that hold the data); Blueprints waits for a save that has a blueprint (#111); contracts stay read-only in the Journey because completing one has game-side effects that were not checked; Spawn Vehicle is open under #112.
- **Reset journey leaves what a new character has finished** (#112): a save made right after a character was created showed what the "initial state" of the journey is, which the first version of Reset journey had to guess: a new character has 95 steps finished (the achievement markers, the first Dunipedia fragments and the introduction that completes by itself) and four tutorials complete (Movement, TechTree, StatusUITutorial, SprintSimple). Reset journey now leaves those as they are and the count in the question does not include them, so a reset no longer takes a character below a new one. The same save answers the other open questions of #339: a new character's level record differs from the reset's only in its 72 XP (TotalSkillPoints, UnspentSkillPoints and KeystoneBonusSkillPoints are 0); its skills are all at 0 points except the starter ability (Voice Compel in the Bene Gesserit start) and its start key, which is why Reset all skills to rank 0 is followed by Restore starter skills on the same tab; its specialization tracks are empty. Nothing else changed. UAT: reset the journey of a copy of your save, load it, and check that the introduction does not start again.
- **Vehicles: the cargo hold can be changed, and the fuel says how full the tank is** (#48): the cargo hold card of the open vehicle (Player > Vehicles) is the console's "View Contents": the quantity of each stack is an input, every stack has a Delete, **Add item** puts an item in (as much as fits, the same rule as a base container) and **Clear this container** removes everything after the typed count. It works only on the cargo hold of a vehicle you own; the cargo hold of a vehicle that is not yours, a world vehicle and a vehicle's per-component holds stay refused by the editor, as before. The Fuel column reads "90.5 of 100 (91%)" for the kinds whose tank was measured in the game (the same table Refuel uses) and the bare number for the others. UAT: put an item in the Sandbike's cargo hold, change a quantity, delete one, load the game and check the hold; clear a hold that has a few stacks.
- **Item catalog refreshed from the console** (#405): `internal/catalog/items.json` has the Ixian Icon (`MTX_IxianTotem_Patent`, a building set; the id is also in the game's own item tables), the Steam id of the DLC of the eight Filmic Archive items (`requiredSteamDlc`) and the console's group of 99 resources (`group`: component, refined resource, raw resource); the catalog list sent to the page carries both fields, the picker does not use them yet. The seven ids the console lists twice keep the placeable entry (rule: the first entry wins, written down in `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md`); which name the game shows for `UniqueThumper_02` and `_03` is still open. The notice names the console commit (`82548d72`). Tests: `internal/catalog/refresh_test.go`. UAT: open Player > Inventory > Give item, type "Ixian Icon" and see it listed under its name.
- **Region names on the Saved map, the vehicle list and the base list** (#406): the Hagga Basin is cut into 12 named regions (Hagga Rift, Sheol, The O'odham ...). The Saved map has a card that lists the regions the character has discovered (from `map_areas`) and says which region the character, each vehicle and the base are near; the Location of a vehicle and the Coordinates of a base say "near <region>". A place is given the region of the nearest map marker that has a named area (the save stores the region of a marker, not of a vehicle or base), so near the edge of two regions it can be the neighbour. Only the Hagga Basin has names; on the Deep Desert nothing is added. Read-only. The names are `internal/ops/hagga-regions.json`, the console's table, copied unchanged with its notice. Tests: `internal/ops/regions_test.go`, `web-tests/regions.test.mjs`. UAT: open Saved map and see the card; open Player > Vehicles and read the Location.
- **Stored vehicles and picked-up bases are labelled** (#403): the game gives every actor a lifecycle state (`actors.state`). In the saves at hand a vehicle in the character's vehicle backup has state 2 and one stored for recovery has state 4 (the vehicle lists in `backup_vehicles` and `recovered_vehicles` agree in every save); a vehicle in the backup keeps its owner record and its last position, so it used to look like a vehicle standing in the world. Player > Vehicles now labels such a vehicle (In the vehicle backup, Stored for recovery) and turns off Bring to me and Duplicate for it, which are also refused with the reason; the Saved map does not plot it and says how many are not shown; the teleport list of vehicles leaves it out. Player > Bases labels a base picked up with the game's base tool (no claim, its actors linked in `base_backup_linked_actors`) as Picked up; no save at hand has one, so that rule follows the console's description of the tool and is tested on a built save. Nothing is written for the labels. Other state numbers (an older save has a vehicle with state 1) are not interpreted. Tests: `internal/ops/lifecycle_test.go`, `web-tests/lifecycle.test.mjs`. UAT: put a vehicle into the vehicle backup in the game, reload the save in the editor and look at Player > Vehicles and the Saved map.

### Changed
- **Look and feel: the Building sets and Cosmetics lists say how many are owned, the save file name in the header reads as a label, and the Database tab draws Previous and Next only when there is more than one page** (closes #681, #682, #684; wording and styling only, no change to any action or API). The count line of the Building sets list now ends with "X of Y learned" and the Cosmetics line with "X of Y unlocked or in inventory" ("in inventory" where the save has no customization library to read), as the Crafting line ends with "N of M known". The save file name in the header is plain text after a "Save:" caption, with no box or border, and its full path stays in the tooltip. The Database tab draws the pager only for a table of more than 100 rows; the "N rows, showing a-b" line stays.
- **A full audit of the changes since 2026-10-27** (refs #606; docs only, no change to the editor): `docs/audit/2026-11-14-full-audit-register.md` merges eight role reviews and a high-effort code review of the 82 commits to `c71a85d` into one register (68 findings: 0 critical, 3 high, 11 medium, 30 low, 24 info), with a regression sweep of the 54 closed fixes (none reverted; #594 was closed without its fix), and `docs/audit/2026-11-14-remediation-plan.md` orders the work in waves, decisions first. The findings are filed as #653 to #662 and as comments on the open issues that already cover them.
- **Roadmap page: lists every open issue, with its category, and ranks the top of the queue** (`scripts/roadmap-gen.sh` no longer skips issues without the roadmap, bug or inbox label: an issue with no priority label goes to a final Reference (no priority) section; `docs/rank.tsv` holds the operator's rank and a one-line reason for the top of the queue, ranked issues sort first within their priority and an unranked one shows `-`; the generator warns on stderr about an open issue with no priority label and no `cat:audit` and about a row of either tsv file whose issue is closed; stale status rows of closed issues removed)
- **The "N queued" chip in the header is a button: a click opens the list of queued changes in the banner** (#597). The list was already there (What is queued, one line per change) and in the chip's tooltip, but nothing opened it on a click. The click opens the list, scrolls to it and puts the focus on it; the tooltip ends with "(click to open the list)". With nothing queued the chip stays hidden, as before.
- **Repair gear also repairs the items in your backpack** (refs #524). The button on Character > Inventory (now **Repair all worn items, loadouts and backpack**) and the automatic pass job (now **Repair worn equipment, loadout and backpack**; same switch, same setting, so no saved setting changes) repair what you wear (inventory type 1), your loadout (type 15) and your own backpack (type 0), with the rules they had: durability up to the item's maximum (100 when it records none), a worn-down maximum restored, an item already full or above its maximum left as it is. Only your own character's backpack: a chest, another character's items and the storage of a vehicle are not touched. The result counts the backpack apart: the button says "Repaired 2 worn or loadout items and 3 backpack items", the automatic pass says "2 worn or loadout items repaired, 3 backpack items repaired", and the text is unchanged when no backpack item needed repair. The write-log line reads "repair gear: 2 items, 3 backpack items". The reply of the route also carries "backpackScanned" and "backpackRepaired". Tests only, not checked in the game: open the editor on a save with a worn item in the backpack, run the repair and read the count line.
- **The automatic refill at launch uses the same check as an edit made by hand, so it no longer waits for the game to close when the game is only at its menu; this changes the stricter rule of #419 and #449 for the pass, and nothing else** (#499). Until now the pass ran only when no Dune process existed at all, so with the game open at its main menu a minute after leaving the world the page said "Automatic refill queued: the game is open; applied when it closes", while every edit made by hand was written at once. The pass now waits only while the editor's own check (`GameStateFor`, the rule of #449 and #470) says the game may still hold the save's rows: a single-player session is active, the game wrote its database a moment ago (within 3 minutes; within 23 seconds when the log shows the session's end and the last write after it), the session ended a moment ago (within 30 seconds; 6 minutes when that last write is not in the log), another program has the save file open, or the process list or the game's log cannot be read. Then it runs by itself, on the freshly read file after any queued edits have been dealt with, as soon as that clears, with the game still open; with no Dune process it runs as before. The banner and the card say what the pass waits for in the words of the edits made by hand ("Automatic refill queued: a single-player session is active, ...", "... the game wrote its database 20 seconds ago, ...", "... the single-player session ended 12 seconds ago ..."), not "the game is open; applied when it closes"; **Run the automatic pass now** answers with the same reason instead of "the game is open". The write gate itself (`installBlob` and the test that lists every route to the save file) is unchanged except that the pass no longer has a gate of its own: `FlushAutoPass` and the extra process rule are gone, the pass writes through `Flush`. The switches (the master switch, each job, `--no-auto`, the countdown, Skip), the "no confirmations" rule and the skip when edits from before are still queued are unchanged. Evidence that this is safe is the evidence of #449 and #470: the write of #419 came shortly after leaving the world, which is inside the 30-second wait, and the exits from a world in the logs showed one more write within a few seconds and then silence at the menu. README ("How saving works", "Automatic refill and repair", the verification table), SECURITY.md and the design notes say the same. UAT: with the game at its main menu a minute or more after leaving the world, open the editor with Automatic refill on and let the countdown end: the refill is saved (a new `-auto` backup, a write-log line) with the game still open; open the editor during a single-player session, or within 30 seconds of leaving the world: the page says the refill is queued and why, the file is untouched, and the refill runs by itself when the reason clears.
- **Shorter note under Teleport when another map is chosen** (#426): the note was 66 words (what is written, the one move that was checked, the position rule). It is now two sentences (only the move to the Deep Desert was checked, one character, a throwaway save; try any other on a copy first); the whole note is unchanged in the Movement card's About this. The budget test now also draws the notes that appear only while they apply (Admin with another map chosen, the Land claim editor with a cell chosen, the Care package list full) and holds them to the same limits. Text and tests only.
- **Edits wait about 30 seconds, not 3 minutes, after you leave the world** (#470; changes the wait of #449/#458, and no other rule). The log of the game was measured over the exits from a world in several logs: after the exit the game writes its database exactly once more, a few seconds later, and then writes nothing at the menu until the next session starts; while a world is loaded it writes about every 20 seconds (the log line says 60), not every 60 as the README and the waiting message said. When the log shows the session ended AND the game's last write after it, an edit is now written 30 seconds after the exit (the largest gap seen plus the largest save interval seen, rounded up) and not before 23 seconds after that write (it only binds if the game writes later than anything measured). Unchanged: a world in play is never written; with the log unreadable, or no write seen after the end (6 minutes after the end), or no end seen (3 minutes since the last write), the waits are as before; the game closed writes at once; the file-open check; the automatic pass at launch. The waiting text counts down ("about 25 seconds more"; "1 second" no longer reads "1 seconds"). The #419 case (a write shortly after leaving the world) is still refused. Table of the exits and the rule in `docs/design/2026-10-27-menu-write-grace.md`; README ("How saving works"), SECURITY.md, the continuation notes and row H4 of the UI text sweep say the same; tests with fake logs and file times for the short wait before and after it passes, no write seen, session active, log unreadable, game closed and the countdown text. UAT: leave the world to the menu and press Create or Apply at once: the page counts down at most 30 seconds, the edit is written by itself, and the game then loads the character with no repair prompt and the edit there; one edit after more than a minute at the menu is written at once.
- **Four in-game checks done on 2026-10-27 are recorded, and the texts that said "not checked in the game" now say what was checked** (docs and wording only; no button does anything different). On the operator's own game: (1) Care package, Apply kit (#417, issue #459): a kit of 12 items (stillsuit pieces, a weapon, gear, 10 consumables, 50,000 Solari, no XP) applied to a new single-player character at the game's menu; the game loaded the character and the items were there and stayed after the game's next saves. Not covered: the level and the unspent skill points (the kit had none), more than one character. (2) Duplicate this vehicle (#112): the game loaded a copy of a medium ornithopter and it flew, and an earlier copy of a light ornithopter also worked; other kinds of vehicle were not tried; a vehicle in the vehicle backup (stored) cannot be duplicated, and the refusal is right. (3) Edits written while the game is open at its menu (#449, PR #458): an edit made 3 minutes after the player left the world (the game had saved at the end and then stayed quiet) was written by the editor's rule and the game loaded the result; the 6-minute fallback and the other waits were not covered. (4) Teleport to another map (#67): the Teleport tab's move to the Deep Desert wrote the character's three actors and the game loaded the character there, on a throwaway save; one character and that one move. Changed: the README table "Which write features were checked in the game" (rows for Duplicate vehicle, Care package, Teleport to another map, and a new row for the write at the menu), the question of Duplicate this vehicle and the warning under Teleport on another map (and its About this) in the page, the code comments in `internal/ops/vehicle_duplicate.go` and `internal/ops/teleport.go`, `docs/design/2026-10-24-player-tabs-status.md` and row AD8 of `docs/design/2026-10-26-ui-text-sweep.md`, and the web tests that pin those sentences. The evidence is the operator's report, in issue comments; no snapshot or screenshot is stored. The blood-sack fill is still not checked in the game.
- **Top-ups no longer ask first** (#450, #453): **Refill power (every base)**, **Refill base water (every base)** and **Repair every base to max** act on the first press, as the other refill and repair buttons already did. The result line after each one still says what was refilled or repaired and how many buildings were already full, and the backup taken before the write is the undo. The two-press ask stays on what destroys or resets: **Clear this container**, **Clear sand on every base** and the config **Reset**. The screen reader no longer hears "Press the button again" for the three top-ups. **Fill chosen** on the Landsraad board is a top-up too (it only raises the character's own contribution, never lowers it) and no longer asks for the typed "fill N houses": it runs on one press, and its result line says what changed (your contribution on N houses, the faction goal on M houses; a house whose faction goal alone was raised is not called a contribution change) and that the rewards themselves are not written. "Nothing is chosen" and "Nothing to change" are still said without writing anything.
- **Edits are written again while the game is open at its menu, by the editor's own judgement; this changes the rule of #419** (#449). #419 refused every write while a Dune process existed, because the automatic pass had written the save at the character-selection screen shortly after the player left the world and some items could then not be moved. That also queued edits made at the main menu long after the world was left, until the game was closed. The editor now decides by itself, and never asks the player. With the game open, an edit made by hand (and restoring a backup) is written only when all of these hold: the process list and the game's log could be read; the log shows no single-player session running; the game has not written its database for 3 minutes (the log shows the game writing every 60 seconds while a world is loaded, one more write within a few seconds of leaving it in each leave event checked in real logs, and then silence until the next session starts); if a session ended and that last write is not in the log, 6 minutes have passed since it ended (a fallback, NOT measured: nothing observed says how long the game may still hold the save's rows, so it is long); and no other program has the save file open in a way that stops it being replaced (Windows: an open with delete access that allows all sharing, so it cannot make the other program's own open fail). Otherwise the edit stays queued, and the banner, the "Still waiting" toast and Check again say what is actually waited for (a session is active, the game wrote N seconds ago, the session ended N ago, another program has the file open, the log or the file check cannot be made), not "the game is running". The case of #419 (a write soon after the game's last write) stays queued. The automatic pass at launch is unchanged and stricter: it writes only when no Dune process exists at all, even at the menu, and its final write refuses while one exists. All routes still go through `installBlob` with the check right before the rename (the test that lists every route passes, with the pass added to it). Not established: whether the game keeps game.db open at its menu in a way the file check cannot see (a handle that shares delete is not seen), and what the game does with rows it read earlier; the logs show it opens the database again when a session starts, so the cause of the items that could not be moved after #419 is not settled by them. README ("How saving works"), SECURITY.md and the UI text sweep (rows H4, H8) say the same. UAT: with the game open at its main menu for more than 3 minutes after leaving the world, make an edit by hand: it is written at once (a new backup and a write-log line); leave a world and edit within a minute: the banner says the game wrote its database a moment ago and the edit is written by itself a few minutes later; open the editor with Automatic refill on and the game at its menu: the page says the refill is queued until the game closes.
- **The UI text sweep is finished** (#426, #212, #46): the design note `docs/design/2026-10-26-ui-text-sweep.md` now says which pull request carried out which page, the choices made on the way, the numbers (41 tooltip sites, 24 closed **About this** disclosures, 33 web tests added) and the by-eye check for the operator. Documentation only.
- **Less explanatory text on Player > Character > Inventory, Reputation and Care package** (#426, step 2): on Inventory the sentences about Repair, Clear this container (and the Augments card's Apply) are tooltips on those buttons, the note about Give item sits in the Give item card's **About this**, and the pointer line "The fill actions are on the Character tab" with its button is gone from the card (the way back to Character > Overview is in the Inventory card's About this). The reason Clear this container is off ("X is empty: nothing to clear") stays, and so do the counts. Reputation: the range is on each box (hover or focus: "0 to 12474"; the boxes still have min and max) instead of a line, and "Edit and press Tab/Enter" is gone (the result line says what was written and how to undo it). Care package: the five-sentence description is the list card's About this; a line "The most kits are kept (N): delete one first." is drawn only while the list is full; the name rule and the XP/skill-point hint in the kit editor are tooltips. Errors, results and the empty state ("No kits yet") are unchanged.
- **Less explanatory text on Player > Character > Overview, and the mechanism for the rest** (#426, step 1; design note `docs/design/2026-10-26-ui-text-sweep.md`): the sentences that explained a button (what Add or remove, Give a wallet amount, Give Blood Marks, Give XP, Give Intel, Fill hydration, Fill blood sacks, Refill Literjons, Fill all liquids and Reset level and XP do, and what they leave alone) are now a **tooltip** on that button, shown on hover and on keyboard focus (Escape puts it away) and read by a screen reader as the button's description; the same words, moved. The Fill card has a closed **About this** (kept per card in the browser's storage) that says the blood-sack fill has not been checked in the game and that a fill is queued while the game runs. Drawn only when they apply: the hydration line (only when the summary has no hydration) and the reason Reset level and XP is off. Deleted because the page already says it: the pointer to the summary above, the hydration number the summary tile shows, and the sentence that every Give applies when the game next loads the save (the banner and the result line say it). Every result, error and queued line reads as before. New web test helper `web-tests/uitext.mjs` (the default visible text of a page) and `uitext.test.mjs` (the checks every page must pass).
- Roadmap rule: bugs come before features (operator, 2026-10-25): within the same priority open bugs are worked first and listed first, and a new bug the operator reports gets `p1-now` unless it is trivial or needs the operator or the game, in which case it is labelled so but not left in the inbox for long. The rule is in `scripts/roadmap-gen.sh` (the "How we work the roadmap" section) and in the regenerated `docs/roadmap.md`. Documentation and a generator only.
- **Less explanatory text on Crafting, Research, Building sets, Customizations, Skills, Specialization and Journey** (#426, step 3): the sentence under each "Unlock all" button and each reset button is a tooltip on that button (the reset explanations keep their ids, so the buttons are still described by them), and the reason a reset is off ("Nothing to reset: ...") stays drawn. The card-level background is in a closed **About this**: the recipes listed (Crafting), Give adds the item to your backpack (Building sets), the library and DLC note (Customizations), why spent plus unspent need not equal the total (Skills), the 90-word explanation of track numbers (Specialization) and what completing or resetting a node does (Journey). Drawn only when they apply: "N rows of the save whose track is not known yet are left alone", the contract line on the Contract list, and the no-track-has-a-number note on Specialization, which is now two sentences. Deleted because the page already says it: "group markers cannot be unlocked as one entry" (the Group button says it), and "Changes are written to the game file as you make them ..." on Specialization (the banner and the results say it); "Max XP is N" is on each Grant max button. Customizations keeps "N unlocked now." as a state line. Fixed while doing this (#429): on Journey the lists (Story, Contract, Codex, Tutorial) could not be switched until the Filter button had been pressed once, because the filter text was kept as `window.jq` and the filter box has the id `jq`.
- **The fill actions moved from Player > Inventory to Player > Character** (#415): one **Fill** card on the Character tab (Overview) holds Fill hydration, Fill blood sacks (with the count of sacks that are not full), Refill Literjons and Fill all liquids (with the "Also refuel my vehicles" box). The wording, the confirmations, the queued-edit message of Fill hydration (#371), the counts and the disabled states are those the buttons had, and the action keys and routes are unchanged. The counts are read when the Character tab is drawn, and the tab still draws for a save with no hydration record or no blood sacks, or when a count cannot be read. Player > Inventory keeps the item table with its Liquid column (#367), Give item and the other cards, and shows a note under its title, "The fill actions are on the Character tab", with a button that switches there. Tests: the Fill card in every state (sacks full and not full, hydration queued while the game is running or written at once, no hydration record, no sacks, counts that fail), the Inventory tab without the fill actions. UAT: open the editor on a copy of a save, go to Player > Character and check that the Fill card shows the four actions and their counts, press Fill blood sacks and Fill all liquids and compare the toasts with the ones the Inventory tab gave; open Player > Inventory and check that the note is there and its button returns to the Character tab.
- Go 1.26.10 (#228, #229): the toolchain was raised to fix GO-2026-6809 in `crypto/x509`, which the earlier entry for Go 1.26.9 did not yet mention (audit finding GRC-6, 2026-10-15).
- **No second copy of the save is made when it is loaded** (#261, audit findings ARCH-6, QA-17): the editor used to decode and write the save twice on every load, rebase and reload, for a pristine copy that only tests read; that copy is now built from the file on disk when a test or diagnostic asks for it (`WithBaseline`, which says so when the file has changed since). `Save.Commit` no longer takes a `force` argument: nothing in the editor passed it, and it was a way to skip the game-running check that no caller should have. A stale comment about the removed review is corrected.
- **Less explanatory text on Player > Bases** (#426, step 4): on Power and Water the sentence about what Refill power and Refill base water fill and leave alone is a tooltip on the button (the button still says "every base" and still asks with a second press); on Structures the sentence about the highest health seen is a tooltip on Repair every base to max, and the sentence that the two buttons act on all bases is gone because the labels say it; on Inventory the sentence about Slots used is gone (the column reads "5 of 20") and the other is in About this. The Automatic refill and repair card: the one-line description of each job is a tooltip on its box, the reason a job is not available, the last automatic save and the problems stay as they were, the nine-sentence paragraph is the card's About this, and a new line "Waiting: the save cannot be written yet ..." is drawn only while the master switch is on and the save cannot be written, so the pass that waits for the game to close is stated where it matters. The Land claim editor: the how-to, the orientation of the grid and the notes about removing cells are in its About this; the level cap is a tooltip on the level choice; "This editor bypasses the game's normal staking checks ..." is drawn next to Apply while cells are chosen to be added, and is also in About this; the legend and the pending counts stay. Resize by square: "Cells that hold building pieces are never removed" is a tooltip on Shrink claim and the cell-size note is in About this.
- **Repair in one place, no stubs, one Money card** (#248, UX-35; items 16 and 17 of the 2026-10-18 review): gear repair is on Character > Inventory and vehicle repair is on Vehicles, which now has the "only modules below N %" box that was on Admin; Admin keeps a short Repair card with buttons that open those two pages. The five disabled "Not in tabr-tau yet" buttons (Repair faction, Repair Landsraad quests, Wipe inventory, Reset progression, Spawn vehicle), the Danger zone card and the Blueprints page are gone; the README lists what is not built yet. Character > Overview has one **Money** card for the Solari in the backpack and the two wallets (it was a Solari card and a wallet row under Quick rewards).
- **Wording and counts** (#246, UX-33, UX-35, UX-38, UX-39; items 13, 14, 18, 19, 31, 34 of the 2026-10-18 review): every count says what it counts ("N items, N of M slots used", "N nodes, the first 400 shown", "N unspent, N earned", "N of M slots used") and "N of N" shows only while a filter narrows the list ("3 of 12 nodes match"). Money has one set of names everywhere, in the Summary, the Overview, Give to a wallet, the toasts and the queue: **Solari in backpack** (the item stacks), **Solari in wallet** and **House credit in wallet** (the two balances); it was Solari Credit, Solari Coin and House Credit. The asides about "the console" and "Dune Docker" are gone from visible text (the customization group "Other (not in the console's sets)" is "Other cosmetics"). Every table column has a name in words (Stored at, Reason code, Vendor ID, ID, Slots used), Landsraad Start and End and the vendor and recovered-vehicle times are dates, Landsraad rewards and the Admin vendor table show item names from the catalog, card titles and buttons are sentence case, toasts use one arrow ("Hydration 70.1 → 100"), an empty table says what is empty ("No bases yet: build one in the game, then reload."), and the Database says "No rows in this table" instead of "showing 1-0". The README has a short glossary.
- **One banner, a plain reason, and the queue in view** (#245, UX-30, UX-31): the banner under the header says the state once, in a sentence ("Could not tell whether Dune: Awakening is running."), with the tool's own text under a **Details** toggle; it no longer repeats the reason. **What is queued** lists the queued changes in sentence case (the short internal ones such as "solari +10000" are spelled out: "Add 10,000 Solari to the backpack"; item, reputation and currency changes already show before and after), and the **Discard queue** question lists the same changes it would drop. The banner is redrawn only when its content changes, so a focused button keeps focus and an open list stays open.
- **Less explanatory text on Player > Vehicles** (#426, step 5): the long paragraph under Repair all my vehicles and the sentence under Refuel all my vehicles are tooltips on those buttons (the count "3 of 5 vehicles are not full." and the lines about vehicles of an unknown kind or without a fuel record stay beside the button). The tank sizes measured in the game are the Fuel card's **About this**; the note about condition, maximum and the fuel tank is the Components card's. "N other vehicles in the world (not yours) are not shown" is in the Your vehicles card's About this and exists only when there are some. The second "None of your vehicles are in this save yet" line is gone: the table already says "You have no vehicles in this save yet". Results and errors are unchanged.
- Licence statement for the game art (#164, owner decision 2026-10-15): the README now says the MIT licence covers the code, not the Funcom map pictures and icons in the Live Map, which are kept under a not-MIT notice with a removal-on-request contact (an issue on this repository); `docs/art-notice.md` repeats it, and the third-party notices record where the ported tables and the game-data numbers came from.
- Dependencies and toolchain: the Go version is 1.26.9 (#100, GO-2026-6617 reported by govulncheck, #99); `actions/checkout` is 7.0.1 (#28) and the `jsdom` test dependency 30.1.2 (#53).
- CI: the Windows job's limits are 12 minutes for the tests and 20 for the job (were 40 and 60), now that the suite takes about three minutes there (#89).
- **Less explanatory text on Player > Admin** (#426, step 6): the "Admin actions" card, whose only content was a sentence saying where other things are, is gone. On Movement, the 170-word paragraph under Teleport is split by what it is about: "Choosing a place fills X, Y and Z" is the tooltip of Find, the sentence about the map is the tooltip of the Map choice, the Facing sentence is the tooltip of Facing, and "stay above the terrain or you may fall through" is the tooltip of Z (shown on focus); the background (the game keeps its own copy of the save in memory while single-player runs) is the card's **About this**. The warning that a move to another map has not been checked in the game yet ("try it on a copy first") is now drawn under the Teleport row only while another map is chosen, and is always in About this. The sentences under Change faction and Reset all vendors are tooltips on those buttons. The current faction, the Find result and the vendor tables with their empty states stay.
- Land Claim Editor: clicking inside a dotted (available) cell now works (#145). The cells are unfilled, and an unfilled SVG shape only receives clicks on its thin outline, so a click in the middle of a square did nothing. They now take clicks anywhere inside.
- The Bases, refill and Inventory screens use the in-game names instead of "devices" and "containers" (#143): Water Cistern, Medium and Large Water Cistern, Windtrap, Large Windtrap, Blood Purifier and Improved Blood Purifier (the editor said Blood Water Extractor), Fuel-Powered and Spice-Powered Generator, Omnidirectional and Directional Wind Turbine, and Literjons. **Refill generators** is now **Refill power** and **Refill containers** is **Refill Literjons**. Refill results, the pending-edit descriptions in the review pane and the open-time messages say what was changed by name (for example "refill power: N Omnidirectional Wind Turbines, N Windtraps"). (The review pane named here was removed in #238.)
- Refill generators now covers wind turbines and windtraps, and follows the Dune Docker console's numbers. Fuel and spice generators fill to 499 (was 500). An omnidirectional wind turbine takes Low-grade lubricant and a directional one Industrial-grade, in up to five stacks of 100, 499 in all; a partial stack is filled before a new one is added. A windtrap gets 5 filters of the best kind for the machine (Standard Filter, or Advanced Particulate Filter in a Large Windtrap); a lower-grade filter already inside is changed to it. Placeholders (items you have laid out but not built) are no longer filled, for generators, turbines and water alike. The Power tab shows the same capacities.
- **Less explanatory text on the Saved map and the Landsraad** (#426, step 7): Landsraad: the nine-sentence explanation of the two scales (house goal, reward tiers, your contribution, "recorded as delivered", what Fill to this tier does, what is not known from a save) is the Task board's **About this**, word for word; "Your character is in no faction (Neutral), so there is nothing to fill ..." stays in view and is drawn only when that is the case; the empty-choice line reads "Nothing is chosen." (the counts after a choice are unchanged); the sentence about the faction choice is now on the buttons it concerns (Fill faction goal has it as its tooltip as before; Complete has a tooltip), and the choice is labelled "Faction for Fill faction goal and Complete". Saved map: "The save as of its last write, not the running game." stays; "Markers you have not discovered yet are shown faded" is a tooltip on Show undiscovered markers; the idle hint ("Hover a marker for details. Drag to move, scroll or press + and - to zoom, 0 to fit.") and the reason a stored vehicle is not shown are the card's **About this**, and the line under the map is empty until a marker is hovered; the caveat about spice and flour positions is a tooltip on those two layer buttons; the explanation of regions is the Regions card's About this. The tooltip and About this helpers are now plain functions so the map's script can use them.
- Bases > Structures: **Repair all to max** and **Clear sand buildup** no longer ask for a typed word (#70). Like Refill water and Refill generators they run at once; nothing is written until Review & save, which is the confirmation. Restoring a backup and running a SQL write still ask for a typed word. (Superseded by #238: there is no Review & save step, so nothing waits for a confirmation; the edit is written as it is made, after a backup, and the backup is the way back.)
- Database > Tables no longer offers boxes for things that must not be edited by hand (#71). The `accounts`, `applied_patches` and `items_id_sequencer` tables are read-only, and in every other table the primary key, link (foreign key) and account identity columns are plain text with the reason on hover. The server refuses those edits too, and the SQL write console refuses statements that name `accounts`.
- Tests: building a test save takes 0.04 s instead of 0.8 s (#89). The throwaway working database no longer waits for the disk after every statement of the 96-table schema. The Go suite runs in about a minute here instead of about twelve, and the Windows job should follow; nothing a test checks changed.
- **Less explanatory text on Config and Database** (#426, step 8, the last page): Config: the success line reads "All expected config files are present." (the list of names is the choice below it), the closing sentence of the problem box ("Missing files are normally created by the game ...") is gone, the warning that the game rewrites its config files when it exits stays, now pointing at the Backups list below it, and the source of the defaults (the Dune Docker console's table, MIT, RedBlink) is the file card's **About this** (the credit is also in the third-party notices). Database: Write mode's explanation is a tooltip on Run as write; "Plain text columns are read-only ..." is the Tables page's About this and exists only when some column is read-only, while a read-only table still says "Read-only: reason."; the Save backups line is one sentence ("A backup is taken before changes are written and before every restore. Restore needs the game to be closed.") with the two-minute detail in About this. The counts, results and errors are unchanged.
- CI: the Windows test job no longer fails on the schema evidence test (#131). The evidence copy of the game schema is now pinned to LF like the embedded one, and the test compares them with line endings normalised; on a Windows checkout the two differed only by CRLF, which turned every run on `main` red.
- Land Claim Editor mirrors the console (#133): the claim is drawn as a grid turned by the base's original yaw (north at the top) with the totem's cell, the cells it has (with their building-piece counts) and dotted available cells next to it. Click an available cell to add it and a chosen cell to take it back (chosen cells must stay connected edge to edge, and cells that hung from a cell you take back go with it), pick a vertical level (up only, to 5) and **Apply changes**, which asks first and goes through the review pane. The server checks every cell again: connected to the claim, not already in it, within 128 cells of the totem, at most 500 in one edit. Summary tiles show the totem id, the yaw, the horizontal segments and the vertical level. The earlier grow/shrink-by-square controls are kept below as **Resize by square**. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Operator test fixes (#115, #116, #117, #118): the Player summary no longer shows a Guild field (single player has no guilds) and Character > Overview no longer has the Respawn points card; the Live Map layer buttons use the page's own colours so their text can be read (they were dark on dark); on the base list the open base is labelled "Open below" and the other bases get a working Open button (the old button did nothing for the only base); the automatic refill switch for water and power is now on both the Power and the Water tab.
- Building Sets and Customizations match the console, and every list tab has an unlock-all button (#122, #123, #124). **Building Sets** are filed in the console's groups (Structures & Building Sets, Crafting & Utilities, Furniture & Decorations, Faction & House Sets, Special & Promotional, Experimental) with a group drop-down and a *Show experimental* switch, a Requires column, and the three missing Filmic Archive sets. **Customizations** are the console's five cosmetic sets (Atreides 14, Harkonnen 14, Smuggler 25, Dune Man 10, Filmic Archive 3) as cards with what each requires (Lost Harvest, Filmic Archive, account entitlement) and **Grant set** / **Grant all sets**, which put one of each cosmetic in the backpack all or nothing (they say how many fit if there is not room) and do not grant a DLC or entitlement; the other 270-odd cosmetics stay listed under Other. **Unlock all** buttons: all building sets (all but the experimental ones, learned directly), all research, all crafting recipes, and **Max all skills** for a school or all five schools (the unspent points are not touched). Each asks first and is one edit in the review pane. The item catalog was brought up to the console's current one (it was missing 7 items: three Filmic Archive building sets, two Filmic Archive cosmetics and two emotes) and now keeps each item's source and required DLC. Not checked in game. (Superseded by #238: there is no review pane or Review & save step; every edit is written to the game's file as it is made, or queued while the file cannot be written.)
- Every table sorts by any column header (#126): click a header to sort ascending, again for descending, a third time to go back to the original order; an arrow shows the direction. Numbers sort as numbers (9 before 10), editable cells by their value, text naturally, empty cells last. The sort is kept when the table is drawn again after an edit. The Database viewer shows a page at a time, so it sorts the page on screen.
- Operator test fixes, second batch (#119, #120, #121, #127): **Repair all worn items and loadouts** on Character > Inventory (and in Admin): it now repairs exactly what you wear (inventory type 1) and your loadout (type 15), no longer the backpack. (Superseded by #524: it repairs the character's own backpack too.) The Vehicles tab has **Repair** on each vehicle and **Repair all my vehicles**. The Land Claim Editor draws the claim as a **grid of cells** (the totem, the claim cells with their building-piece counts, and cells outside the claim that hold pieces). The **Extras** tab is gone: structure health, repair all, clear sand, placeables and piece types are on the base's new **Structures** tab, and the vendor purchase limits are on Admin.
- The Player tab now mirrors the Dune Docker console's Players > Player Name view and its tabs (#96): Character (Overview, Inventory, Reputation), Crafting, Research, Building Sets, Customizations, Skills, Specialization, Journey (with tutorials and player tags), Blueprints, Bases, Vehicles and Admin. Bases and Vehicles are no longer top-level tabs; the top-level tabs are Player, Landsraad, Config, Database and Extras (Extras was removed again, see #127 under Changed). Each Player tab is meant to hold what the console's tab holds: Admin now has Repair (gear, vehicles), Danger Zone, Movement (Teleport) and Faction Assignment as in the console, with the actions tabr-tau lacks shown disabled and the server-only ones (kick, ban, login queue) left out. Features the console's player view does not have go to the new **Extras** tab (vendor purchase limits for now). The tabs the console has and tabr-tau does not yet (Crafting, Research, Customizations, Skills, Blueprints) say so instead of being left out. Nothing that worked before was removed; features moved: Faction reputation to Character > Reputation, specialization tracks to Specialization, tutorials and tags to Journey, learned building sets to Building Sets, Teleport, Repair gear and Repair vehicles to Admin, vendor limits to Extras, then to Admin. A saved browser tab of Bases or Vehicles opens the matching Player tab.
- `tools/snapshot.ps1` lists accounts and accepts `-SteamId` when several exist.
- **A give puts in as much as fits** (#345, part of the Blood Marks work, #344): Give item, Give water, a give into a base container and the Give Items queue no longer refuse the whole give when the inventory has too few free slots. They fill the stacks that are there first (up to the item's stack size), then make new stacks in the free slots, and stop when the slots run out; an item that does not stack is limited by the free slots. The answer carries `requested`, `given`, `reason` (empty when everything went in) and `limit` (`slots`, or `bound`), and the page shows "Gave 340 of 500 Iron Bar: the backpack is full." as a warning that stays until it is dismissed. Only a give that cannot place a single unit is refused (409, code `no_room`, nothing written, no backup). A partial give is one edit with one backup and one line in the write log that states the real amount ("Give 340 of 500 x Iron Bar (grade 0)"; a whole give keeps its wording). The free slots are read inside the edit, so a queued copy (the game running) is put back only onto a file where the inventory still has the room it had, and is held otherwise. One give makes at most 1,000 new stacks; a request past that now gives the 1,000 and says so (it was refused whole). Solari still add all or nothing. The editor does not limit a give by volume: the item catalog it embeds has no volume for any item, so there is nothing to add up against an inventory's `max_item_volume` (it never did). Tests changed with it: `TestAGiveOfMoreStacksThanTheBound...` (now gives the bound), `TestGiveHonorsTheStackSize` (the 4,000 give now puts in what fits) and `TestGiveThatNeedsMoreSlotsThanAreFreeSaysSo` (the all-or-nothing refusal it asserted now belongs to the strict give that Solari use, `TestTheStrictGiveThatNeedsMoreSlotsThanAreFreeSaysSo`).
- The automatic refill when the editor opens (#352) is now one edit for everything that is switched on: base water and power used to be two edits (two entries in the write log if they were written separately). The card is titled "Automatic refill and repair when the editor opens" and lists the jobs. The refills and repairs the buttons do are unchanged (the same code runs inside the pass).
- The audit registers match the code and the repository settings as of 2026-10-24 (#157, #24; the tracking issues #24, #40, #41, #257 and #368 carry the evidence): DBA-16, S7 and QA-7 are fixed (#384, #382, #386), the cells and fields of the accessibility backlog are fixed (#385), the unchecked assertions of F-17 are fixed (#383), and the settings that were found changed are written down (SHA pinning is required, `windows` and `ui` are required checks, private vulnerability reporting is on). The README says what to do when the game was updated after an edit (#20).
- **Tests that bound the work do not use the clock** (#41, audit finding QA-7): the read console, the write console, the give at its bound and the repair of buildings were checked by how long they took (3 s to 20 s). The read console now sets its time limit through a test hook (no bare global) and checks the error that says the query was stopped, the other bounds check what was done: the plan of the repair statement has no correlated subquery, and the give reads the inventory once. A slow machine cannot fail them and a regression no longer shows as a slow test.
- **A new resource the save has never held gets the game's plain durability record** (#258, #18; audit finding DBA-8, the part that is verifiable): when no item of the resource's template exists to copy, the new item's durability record is `{"DecayedMaxDurability": 0.0}`, as in almost every resource row the game wrote in a played save, instead of an empty record. Items of other kinds keep the old record: their shape differs item by item and one weapon and a few garments are not enough to settle it (see the comment on #258). Tests: `internal/ops/resource_stats_test.go`. UAT: give a resource the character has never held, e.g. Azurite Ore, and load the game.

### Removed
- **Review & save** (#238): the review pane, its token and the `/api/save/review` and `/api/save/commit` routes. Every edit is written as it is made, with a backup, or queued (see Added).
- The edit boxes on single pieces and vehicles (#155): the health box on every row of Player > Bases > Structures > Placeables (the health is shown as a number now) and the Set durability button on each Recovered / stored vehicle. Repair all to max, Repair and Repair all my vehicles are unchanged.
- The read-only Permissions card on the Bases tab (#58) and the `permissions` field of `GET /api/bases`: it was multiplayer scaffolding; in single-player every row has the same access level and the only ranks are the owner.
- Support for non-Windows systems is now stated plainly (#87): the game runs only on Windows and the save lives on the same PC, so the README says Windows 10/11 (64-bit) only, and the macOS/Linux branches that opened the browser are gone. `--web` still opens the browser on Windows (for example when WebView2 is missing).
- The Exchange tab (#58): it only held a second copy of the Solari box (still on Player > Overview) and the vendor purchase limits. The vendor limits and restock cycles moved to **Player > Admin**; the API paths are now `/api/vendors` and `/api/vendors/reset`.
- The `--allow-remote` flag (#58): the editor has no login and can rewrite your save, and nothing needs it to be reachable from another machine, so it now only ever listens on this computer and only accepts connections from it. A non-loopback `--addr` is refused outright.
- **Give water is gone** (#369): the button gave a fixed ten Cups of Water items. Fill hydration fills the character's own water and Refill Literjons fills the water containers, so nothing needs the item; the console's Give water was only kept for parity.

### Fixed
- **The public-text check for pull requests can no longer be replaced by a review run** (fixes #701, audit finding L3-06; CI only, no change to the editor's behaviour; a CI-control change, so reviewed before it is merged). The job named `public-text` ran for pull requests, reviews and review comments alike, and a review run reports on the same head commit as the pull request run but skips the commit-message step; with one check name on one commit the newest result decides, so a clean review run could replace a failed pull request run (and a stranger's comment could turn the check red). `.github/workflows/public-text.yml` now runs on the `pull_request` event only and keeps the name `public-text` (title, body, branch name and commit messages); issues, issue comments, reviews and review comments moved to the new `.github/workflows/public-text-discussion.yml` with the check name `public-text-discussion`, which is not a required check. A test (`internal/hygiene`) pins the events, the job names and that no other workflow uses the name `public-text`. The repository setting that makes `public-text` required stays with the owner.
- **The automatic refresh no longer scrolls the page back to the last pressed control** (#696, audit finding L3-01, a regression of #687). The refresh from the file (about every twenty seconds while the game runs) gave the focus back to the last pressed button and scrolled that button into view, so the page jumped away from what was being read at every refresh. The focus still goes back after a redraw, and the page is moved to the control only for a redraw that follows the person's own action. Test: `web-tests/redrawplace.test.mjs`.
- **Other in-page changes no longer throw the page back to the top: opening a base, a base sub-tab or group, an inventory group, the inventory filter, adding to the give queue, opening the augments, the care package editor, the table browser's search and paging** (#686, rest of audit finding G-010; page only, nothing in a save write path changes).
