Each drew the page as if it were another page, so the scroll offset went to 0 and the focus was lost. They now keep the offset and the focused control, and a control that the kept offset leaves off the screen or under the header is scrolled into view when it gets the focus back. A new search or another page of table rows starts at the top of the table's own box. A refused database edit or a refused reset keeps the offset too. Changing the top-level tab, the sub-tab rows, a link to another page, and another table, config file or map still start at the top on purpose.
- **Vehicles: changing a part, the tier or the class of a vehicle to create, adding or removing an item, Preview and Details no longer throw the page back to the top** (#650, audit finding G-010 in part; page only, nothing in a save write path changes). Each of these drew the page as if it were another page, so the offset went to 0 and the box that was changed lost the focus. They now keep the offset, the focused box and the unfolded parts list, and the boxes are drawn from the draft. The other redraws of G-010 (a base, a base tab or group, an inventory group) stay open on #650.
- **Landsraad: the house card lists each reward waiting to collect on its own line** (#666; page only, nothing in a save write path changes). "Waiting to collect" used to print every waiting reward as one comma-separated run that wrapped badly; each reward now has its own line, like the factions under "Faction race", and a house with nothing waiting still says "nothing waiting". The rewards, their order and the card itself are unchanged.
- **Bring vehicle, Create vehicle, Duplicate vehicle and Teleport work from where the player really is, and refuse when the character's body is somewhere else** (#654, audit findings G-001, G-008 and G-068; a save write path, so reviewed before it is merged). A real save can hold the character's body (and the vehicle it sits in) on the travel map with a state that is not 0, while the controller and the player state are on the map the player is on; the four actions read the place from the body alone, so a vehicle was written onto the travel map, where the player cannot walk to it, and a same-map Teleport moved only the parked body. One helper now reads the controller, the body and the player state inside the edit: the map and the dimension are the controller's, the position is the body's. When the body is on another map than the controller or the player state, or its state is not 0, "next to the character" is refused with a plain sentence and nothing is changed (a place given by hand in Create vehicle is still written, in the player's dimension). A Teleport of such a body is refused unless a map is named, and then it is a move between maps that writes all three actors together; a body whose state is not 0 is not moved. The whole Teleport is now one edit that reads the character's map and state through the edit, so a queued move is held (not applied with the old map's numbers) when the game's newer file has the character on another map. Bring vehicle now writes the dimension of the character too, as Create and Duplicate do. The destination list says `parked` when the body is not where the player is and measures distances from the player. The Landsraad distance to a house is not shown while the body is parked. Tests: `internal/ops/character_place_test.go` (the parked-body fixture for each action, the refusal changes nothing, the helper's cases, the queued edits; they fail on the code before this change).
- **Landsraad: Fill faction goal for another faction records the faction's win and skips your own contribution row; a failed read of the Landsraad page shows its error** (D1-1, D1-2 and D1-9 of #611; D1-3 to D1-8 stay open on that issue; single-player only). Fill faction goal wrote your own contribution row under the faction in the list, so a fill for a faction your character is not in left a second row of yours beside the one under your own faction (the audit reproduced it: 6,000 under one faction and 1,300 under the other). It now splits by faction. For your character's own faction nothing changes: the faction's progress to the house goal, the house complete with that faction as winner and the play clock as the time, and your own contribution at the highest reward tier. For another faction, or a character in no faction, the faction's progress and the win are written and your own contribution row is skipped: no row of yours is ever written under a faction your character is not in. The result line says so (for example that Atreides won the house and that your own contribution was not recorded because your character is not in Atreides), and the queue line says no personal contribution. This lets a rival faction win houses; how the game reacts to a win with no contribution of yours is not verified in game. The faction list already starts on your character's own faction (when it has one); a line under it now says what happens for another faction, and the tooltip and the About note agree. The Landsraad page read eight of its tables with the error thrown away (and the character's faction too), so a failed or schema-mismatched read looked like an empty board, and a failed term read showed the tasks of every term with Fill and Complete offered against it; each read now returns its error, which the page shows in place of the board with Try again. Fill faction goal also tells a failed read of the player from a save with no character: only the second still means "no character", the first stops the edit. The reward progress event is not part of this (#609). Tests: `landsraad_d1_test.go`, `landsraad_goal_test.go`, `landsraad_goal_route_test.go`, `web-tests/landsraad-errors.test.mjs`.
- **Create a vehicle, Bring vehicle and Duplicate vehicle put a ground vehicle 10 m ahead of you, along the way you face** (refs #480). Outdoors the first spot used to be 8 m east of the character whichever way it faced; it is now 10 m ahead along the saved facing, the distance the Dune console uses for a ground vehicle, at the character's feet and 150 up (the console leaves the settling to the game server; the editor cannot see the terrain, so the 150 stays). The clear-spot search, the rule in a base and the aircraft rule (#540) are unchanged; with no facing in the save the old point (8 m east) is used. The answer says "10 m south of you" and so on. Tests on fixtures: every ground class at several facings, no usable facing, a wall at the spot ahead, Bring vehicle.
- **The "Automatic refill queued" notice is a plain notice, not a red error** (#595). While the game holds the save (a single-player session is active, it wrote moments ago, the check cannot say) the automatic refill waits and runs once the file can be written; that is the normal state, so the toast no longer carries the warning colour and cross. A refill that could not finish, was skipped or was not run is still red. The Discard queue button stays hidden when nothing is queued (the #593 fix); the tests that pinned the warning flag for the wait note now pin its absence.
- **The header no longer offers "Discard queue" for a queue that has nothing in it, and the question never says "0 changes"** (fixes #592). With the game closed and the status Offline, the page could show Discard queue, and asking it said "These 0 changes are waiting for the game to close". Two causes, both closed. The status the page polls read "has unwritten changes" and "the list of changes" in two separate steps, so a write that finished between them gave a queue that was waiting with nothing listed; they are now read together (`Save.QueueState`). And an edit that changed rows but carried no description was marked as waiting without being added to the list; every edit the editor makes names itself, so none was found that does this, but one that did not is now listed as "Unnamed change (N rows)" and kept in the journal and written like any other. The Discard queue button is now shown only when something is listed (or a queue is held because the game saved conflicting changes); if the save is somehow waiting with nothing listed, the banner says "Unlisted changes are waiting to be written" with a Write now button instead, so nothing is dropped without a word. The question's wording for a count of nothing is "The queued changes ...". Tests: `internal/save/queue_listed_test.go`, `internal/save/queue_unnamed_flush_test.go`, `internal/ops/state_queue_test.go`, `web-tests/banner.test.mjs`, `web-tests/ui.hostile.test.mjs`.
- **A save written by the current game opens without the schema banner, and the Saved map, Go to a place and the region names read its markers** (fixes #588). The game changed the layout of its save in an update of early November 2026: `markers` and `player_markers` have `map_name_id` in place of the text column `map_name`, a new `map_names` table holds the names, and two Landsraad reward tables are new. The editor showed "This save is not quite the one this editor knows" and its three reads of the markers (the Saved map's markers and their discovery, the discovered trading posts and taxis of Go to a place, and the region of a position) found no `map_name` column and showed nothing. Both layouts are read now, told apart by the columns of `markers` (never by a game version): the reads take their table from `Save.MarkersTable` and `Save.PlayerMarkersTable`, which give `markers` itself for the older layout and, for the newer, the same rows with `map_name` joined from `map_names`. The schema note compares a save with both layouts and says nothing when it matches either; a table that matches neither (a truly new table, a changed definition, a missing table) is still named. The Saved map also now matches a character's discovery to the marker's own map (it matched on the marker's number alone). The kit's `schema` package gets `DDLMapNames()` beside the unchanged `DDL()` (see `kit/CHANGELOG.md`, v0.10.0 unreleased; no tag yet). Tests: both layouts are built from the kit's real DDLs with synthetic rows only (`testsave.Layouts`; an older-layout fixture is converted the way the game would), the markers reads and the schema note are checked in both, a save with an unknown table still gets the banner, and CI runs the editor's save-building packages a second time with `TABR_TEST_LAYOUT=map_names`.
- **Create a vehicle, Bring vehicle and Duplicate vehicle put an ornithopter in the air, ahead of you, never inside the ground** (refs #540). A Light ornithopter made with Create a vehicle was found under the ground below a standing character, inside rock: the editor reads base pieces, things you built and other vehicles from the save, but not the terrain or the level's rocks, and the height it chose outside a base (your feet and 1.5 m) is only a guess about the ground. An ornithopter (Light, Medium or Carrier) now follows the rule of the Dune console for a flying vehicle: it is put at least 30 m ahead of the character, along the direction the character faces, and at least 7 m above the character's height (the console's published `DUNE_ADMIN_FLYING_VEHICLE_MIN_OFFSET` 3000 and `DUNE_ADMIN_FLYING_VEHICLE_Z_OFFSET` 700, in centimetres), so it starts in the air and the game settles it. The facing is read from the saved rotation with the console's maths; with no usable facing the search starts to the east. The clear-spot search is as before (base pieces, things you built, other vehicles, 80 m, the same refusal) and is made at the height the aircraft will have. A ground vehicle is not changed: it is never put below the character, except in a base the editor knows, where it still goes to the ground outside. The answer of Create, Bring and Duplicate now always says where the vehicle is ("8 m east of you", and for an aircraft "30 m south of you and 7 m above you; it starts in the air and the game settles it"; it used to say "next to you" when nothing was in the way). Tests use fixtures only. The game check (an aircraft made in the open and next to a rock formation) waits for the operator; the issue stays open until then.
- **The console base backup file no longer holds an id the console refuses** (refs #538, continuing #534). The Dune console's import stopped at `Non-transfer id !!itm#0` in an actor's weapon component, and the file also held two `!!act#N` ids of the character who queued a crafting job. Every id inside the JSON of an entry is now `!!kind@N` (a row of the file) or `!!kind@0` (none, or a row that is not in the file, such as the character who queued a job); no `#` form is left anywhere. Every file is also checked with the console's rule for ids before it is handed out, in addition to the editor's own reader, and a file that fails is refused with the entry and the place. Compared with a console-made file, the pieces and the totem also carry the console's note that the game's arrays are 0-based (`__lb`). `docs/export-a-base-for-the-console.md` lists what else differs and why.
- **A tooltip no longer clips, moves the table or adds a scrollbar** (refs #536). Hovering **Export for console** in the Bases list made the row grow, a scrollbar appear in the table box and the rows under it be covered or cut off: the tooltip was drawn inside the table's scroll box, which clipped it and counted it in its scroll size. Every tooltip made by the shared helper (Player > Bases, the Base backups card and the other tooltips on a page) is now drawn on top of the page, outside every scroll box, from the place of its button: below it, above it when there is no room below (the last row, the bottom of the window), and moved in from the window's edges on a narrow window. It opens on hover and on keyboard focus and goes away on blur, on Escape and when the page or the table scrolls. The words, colours and the Escape behaviour are as before, and a tooltip on a button that is switched off still shows.
- **A long game session no longer queues every edit** (refs #530). With the game open for hours its log (`DuneSandbox.log`) grows to hundreds of MB, and the editor refused to read a log over 256 MiB, so it could not tell whether a world was loaded and every edit stayed queued with the banner "its log cannot be read (the game log is larger than 256 MiB)". The log is now followed whatever its size: each check reads only what the game added since the last one, and a log that is already long on the first read (or was replaced) is read in 4 MiB pieces, line by line, so it is never held in memory as a whole. A line longer than 1 MiB is read as its first 1 MiB. A log that cannot be read at all (removed, no permission) is still an error and edits stay queued, as before. Tests: a log followed past three times a small cap equals a parse of the whole file at every step; a long first read in chunks of several sizes, one smaller than a line, with an unfinished last line kept until complete; a replaced or shorter file restarts from the top; a very long line is bounded; a removed log queues the edit.
- **Bases > Structures is limited to the open base** (refs #526). The Structures sub-tab used to ask for the whole save: its health card, its Placeables table and its Building piece types table counted every base, and **Repair every base to max** and **Clear sand on every base** changed every base, under the one base that was open. Now `GET /api/bases/structures?base=<id>` (the `/api/bases` route is gone) lists only the building pieces and placeables whose owner is that base's totem (the link the Inventory, Power and Water tabs use): the card (building pieces, lowest and average health, total sand), the piece types (count, min, max, average health) and the placeables (built ones only, not holograms; they match the placeables count of the bases table). A base with no pieces says so. Pieces and placeables with no owner that is a base are listed apart under **Not part of any base** and are never counted into a base. **Repair this base to max** (`POST /api/bases/repair` with `base_id`) and **Clear sand on this base** (`POST /api/bases/clear-sand` with `base_id`) change only that base's rows; the highest health of a type, which is the target, is still the highest seen anywhere in the save, so a base whose every wall is worn is still repaired (nothing is ever lowered). An id that is missing or is not a totem is refused with 422 before anything is read or written. The two whole-save actions stay, as separate routes (`POST /api/bases/repair-all`, `POST /api/bases/clear-sand-all`) and as separate buttons in a **Whole save** card that also says how many pieces and placeables the save has at this base, at other bases and at no base; they act on every row, including those of no base. Tests: a save with two bases of different health and unowned pieces (the open base shows its own card, types and placeables; repair and sand change only its rows; B, the unowned rows and a hologram are untouched; A + B + unowned equal the save-wide count; 422 for a missing id, 0, a negative id, a non-totem actor and an unknown id), the same over the routes, and the page tests (the request names the base, the buttons send its id, the Whole save card).
- **The Recovered and stored vehicles card lists every stored vehicle** (refs #528). The card on Player > Vehicles drew only the rows of the recovered-vehicles table, so a vehicle in the vehicle backup, or one the game marks as stored without a row, was labelled "In the vehicle backup" in Your vehicles while the card said "No vehicle has been recovered or stored.". The card now lists the recovered vehicles, the vehicle in the vehicle backup and the vehicles whose state says stored, with a Kind column ("Stored for recovery" or "In the vehicle backup"), the vehicle type, and the chassis durability, time stored and reason code where the save has them (a dash where it has not). The number in the title is the number of rows, and the empty text shows only when none of the three has a vehicle. The label on a vehicle in Your vehicles and the card come from one function, so they cannot differ. The answer of `/api/vehicles` keeps `recovered` and `backups` and adds `stored`.
- **Go to a place no longer lists a base the game's tool picked up as one of the character's bases** (refs #519). A picked-up base keeps its totem and its owner record, so a list of the character's own totems included it; its actors have the state 5, and the list of Personal bases now leaves out every totem in that state. A base that was picked up and claimed again keeps its rows in the base backup tables but stands in the world, and is listed. The earlier list called Base listed every totem on the map, other players' too, and is gone with the kind list. Tests: `internal/ops/goto_places_test.go`. Not yet checked in the game.
- **The three Deep Desert keys are taxi service points, not trading posts** (refs #516). Go to a place (#500) named three keys "Deep Desert Tradepost" and listed them under Trading posts. The rows of the game's table `DT_StaticLocationData` that carry the trading-post tag and belong to the map object `DeepDesert_1_Travel` are the Deep Desert's Taxi Service points, and the Deep Desert has no trading post. `internal/ops/place-names.json` now gives those three keys the kind `taxi` and the name "Deep Desert Taxi Service" (the editor adds the first six digits of the key when several appear under one heading, as it does for any group), and a `TradingPost` marker with one of those keys is listed under **Taxis**, never under Trading posts. The name is still written by the editor (the game's text has none) and is flagged `"derived": true`. Taxis are listed only on the map their markers are on, so a Hagga Basin save has no Taxis heading from these, and its Trading posts are unchanged (Pinnacle Station Tradepost, Crossroads Tradepost, Griffin's Reach Tradepost, The Anvil Tradepost). No Deep Desert trading post is invented. `tools/placenamesgen` maps the object `DeepDesert_1_Travel` to the taxi kind and the file was regenerated with it; `internal/catalog/PROVENANCE.md` says so. Tests: `internal/ops/places_test.go`, `internal/ops/teleport_map_test.go`, `tools/placenamesgen/generate_test.go`. Not yet checked in a Deep Desert save.
- **The automatic refill and repair no longer runs on a copy** (#508). A save that is not the game's own file (shown as "NOT LIVE: a copy") was refilled by the pass at launch, which then said it saved "to the game". Now there is no countdown on a copy and the page says "Automatic refill not run: this is a copy, not the game's save."; the Automatic refill card says "Not run: this is a copy, not the game's save. The automatic pass only runs on the game's own save."; **Run the automatic pass now** answers "This is a copy, not the game's save: the automatic pass only runs on the game's own save." and writes nothing. The Fill and Refill buttons are edits by hand and still work on a copy. The texts about a write on a copy (Write now, Run as write, the question before a reset, the backup note) say "this file" and not "the game file". In Database > Save backups the backups of a copy carry "made from a copy", and Restore asks with that wording. Tests: with no game, a running game and a game at its menu, a copy gets no pass at launch and the game's own save does (`copy_pass_test.go`, `web-tests/copypass.test.mjs`); not yet checked in game (the operator opens the editor on a copy).
- **Teleport says what happened and what to do** (#501). The message after a teleport used to be "Teleport set" whatever the state, so it read like "you have been moved" while a running game kept the old place. The editor now words it by the state of the game (the same check an edit by hand uses) and the page shows it as sent. Game closed: "Position saved. You will be there when you load this save." Game open at its menu: "Position saved. Press Continue to load it." A world is running (the move waits in the queue): "Position queued, but the running game keeps its own copy of the save: it will not move you now, and the game may overwrite it when it saves. Leave the world, wait about 30 seconds, then teleport and press Continue." (the same with "saved in the file" when it was written). Queued for another reason: "Position queued: <why>. It is written by itself as soon as that clears; press Continue to load it then." A copy: "Position saved to this file. This is a copy, so the game will not see it unless this file is installed as its game.db." A message that asks you to wait or act stays on the screen until dismissed. The Movement card's About text is unchanged. Tests: `teleport_message_test.go` (one per state) and `web-tests/teleportmsg.test.mjs`; the operator checks it in game (teleport while in the world, then at the menu and press Continue).
- **A save that is not the game's own file is written at once, and shown as a copy** (#498): the editor was started on a throwaway copy of a save (`--save` a file in no game folder) while the game ran on its own save, and two base imports into the copy sat in the queue ("edits stay queued until this clears, or the game closes"), because the game-running check asked about this computer and not about the file. Now the check protects only a file the game could have loaded: everything under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DuneSandbox\Saved\Cloud\PlayerClientStorage\` (`game.db`, `game_prepatch.db`, `game-1.db` and the `autosave` ring) keeps the rule exactly as it was, in whatever spelling the path has (letter case, `\\?\`, `..`, links and junctions, 8.3 names, a hard link; when the editor cannot tell, the file counts as the game's own). Any other file takes an edit at once, with no wait for the game, no "single-player session is active" and no quiet period. Every other check stays for it: the integrity check, "the file changed on disk since it was loaded", the backup before the write, the atomic write and read-back, the confirmation for a network share or removable drive, and the queue when the file really cannot be written. The page says so wherever the open file is shown: a **NOT LIVE: a copy** mark in the header between TABR TAU and the file name, the same words at the front of the tab title and the native window title (with the path), and a banner "This is a copy, not the game's save: edits are written at once and do not reach the game unless this file is installed as its game.db" with the path. The game's own save shows nothing of the kind. The status has a new field `live`. The automatic pass at launch does not wait for the game on such a file either; its switches and countdown are unchanged. Tests: `internal/save/ownsave_test.go` (path spellings, links, a sibling folder `PlayerClientStorage2`, a copy takes the edit at once with a game session active and the game's own save in the same situation still queues), `internal/web/notlive_test.go`, `cmd/tabr-tau/main_test.go` (window title), `web-tests/notlive.test.mjs` (header, title and banner in both cases).
- **Go to a place: the place you choose stays in the Teleport X, Y and Z boxes** (#506): choosing a place in the list filled the boxes, but the next time the page was drawn again (after any action, or by itself when the game saved and the editor read the file again) the boxes were rebuilt from the character's saved position, which the game had moved, and the choice and the list of places were dropped, so Teleport sent the character to where it already was. Now what is chosen or typed in the Movement card (X, Y, Z, Facing, Map, and the kind and filter of the places) is remembered by the page and the card is drawn from it until a Teleport succeeds, and the list is drawn again with the chosen place still selected. After a choice the page says which place is selected next to the Teleport button ("Selected: NearPost, N m away"); typing over a coordinate takes that sentence away, because Teleport then goes where you typed. If the game has moved the character to another map in the save, boxes meant for "this map" go back to the saved position. Choosing the heading of the list no longer fills the boxes with the nearest place. Page tests in the browser engine (`web-tests/gotoplace.test.mjs`) choose the nearest and the farthest place in turn and redraw the page after an action, after a refused change and by the automatic reload of the file.
- **Vehicles: Add item no longer puts items into a vehicle that has no storage part** (#483): the cargo hold card of a Sandbike with no storage part offered Add item, and the editor wrote items into it ("N of 0 slots used"). A hold row of 0 slots was read as "no limit", as it is for the other inventories; for a vehicle it means the vehicle has no room at all. Now the card says "This vehicle has no cargo hold (no storage part)." and has no Add item row, and the editor refuses a give into such a hold (409, `no_room`). Rows already in a hold with no slots stay listed ("N items in a hold with no slots") with Delete and **Clear this container**, which work as before, so the surplus can be removed. A hold with room reads the real "N of M slots used" (the slots in use, not the rows) and takes items up to its free slots, the stack size and its volume, and says how much did not fit; this was already so for any hold with a slot count and is now covered by tests with the rows of real vehicles. A base container was checked too: its slots and volume were already respected, and a test now shows it at its limit. The numbers found in the saves are in `docs/design/2026-10-28-cargo-limits.md`. Not checked in game yet.
- **Landsraad: Complete writes the game's own clock into the completion time, not Unix time** (#465): Complete on one house and Complete chosen set `completion_time` to the Unix time of the moment of the click (about 1.79 billion), while every completed row the game wrote holds a number of a few hundred thousand at most, inside the window of its own term: in the copies of saves checked, every game-written row was inside its term's window, and none of the rows the editor wrote was. The column is whole seconds of the game's play clock, the same clock as the term's start and end; the save keeps its value at the time of the save in `farm_variables` (`(universe_lastactive_timestamp - universe_time_timestamp - down_time_accumulation) / 1,000,000`). A completion now writes that value, held inside the term's window (the start of the term when the save has no such row, NULL when it has no term). Reopen is unchanged: completed 0, no winner and a NULL time, the shape of every open row the game wrote (never 0). Whether the game reads the column is not known, so the rows an older editor wrote are corrected as well, with no question: when Complete, Complete chosen, Reopen or Reopen chosen writes, the completed houses of the same term whose time is outside the term's window get the same value; a time inside the window and the houses of earlier terms are left alone, and the count the page showed is not changed (the two `-many` answers carry `repaired`). The evidence is in `docs/design/2026-10-28-landsraad-completion-clock.md`. Tests: `landsraad_clock_test.go`, built from the shapes of a game-written completed row, an open row and an editor-written row. **Not in game yet.**
- **Fill water containers knows every water container of the game, and a stillsuit no longer reads "capacity unknown"** (#482): the Inventory showed "Menol's Stillsuit Garment: Liquid N (capacity unknown)" because only nine Literjon kinds had a capacity. The editor now reads the whole table of fillable items in the game's item data (58 rows: 9 blood sacks, 17 water containers, 31 worn stillsuits and a Cup of Water). The nine Literjon capacities it had agree with the table exactly (no disagreement); new are `Literjon_03` to `Literjon_09` (1100, 1200, 1300, 1400, 1500, 1600, 1700) and the `Dewpack` (250), which Fill water containers, Refill Literjons and Fill all liquids now fill. A worn stillsuit is not a container (the table says so, the save keeps no liquid type for it, and the game changes its amount all the time while it recovers the wearer's water), so the editor does not fill it: its line reads "Water N / 500 (N%), the suit fills itself" (1500 for the high capacity suit), the card header does not count it, and the fill buttons no longer list it as a container of unknown capacity. A template the data does not list is still left alone and reported. The base water devices (`waterDeviceCapacity`) were checked against the game's placeable data too: all seven agree. The whole table, its source and the decision are in `docs/design/2026-10-28-fillable-capacities.md`. Tests: each of the 17 water containers fills to its capacity from an empty record, no record or a partly full one, a full one, one holding blood and one above its capacity are not rewritten, unlisted templates are skipped and reported, each of the 31 suits is left alone by both buttons and shown with its capacity, and a page test for the suit line. **Not in game yet** for the new templates and the suit line.
- **Create a vehicle, Bring vehicle and Duplicate vehicle no longer set a vehicle down inside a base** (#480): the spot was always 8 m east and 1.5 m up from the character, which in a base is inside it (a save showed it inside a room of the base, under the floors above; the vehicle had to be driven out through walls). The spot is now searched for: the old point first, then rings of growing radius (to 80 m), and the first point with room for the vehicle is taken, judged from the base pieces, buildings and other vehicles in the save, with a footprint per class (a Sandbike 2.5 m, a Buggy 4 m, up to 22 m for a Sandcrawler, plus a 1 m margin). A character standing on a base piece gets the vehicle on the ground outside the base, at the level of the nearest foundation (not the character's floor), outside the land claim when that is within 30 m. Outdoors with nothing near, the result is the old point as before. When no spot within 80 m is clear the request is refused and says so. The preview, the result line and the toasts say where the vehicle goes and what it moved away from (for example "outside your base, 14 m south of you, outside the land claim (base pieces at the first spot)"). Not known to a save, so not checked: the terrain and the level's props (a point can still be inside a rock; Bring vehicle moves it). Footprints are chosen, not measured. Not yet checked in the game.
- **The Fill blood sacks tooltip no longer names two capacities** (#474, #426): it said the fill goes to the most blood seen in a save, with 30000 and 3000 as examples; every blood sack kind now takes its capacity from the game's item data (#472), so the tooltip says that. Text only.
- **Refuel and the Fuel column use the tank of the vehicle's chassis, not one tank per class** (#469): a tank follows the grade of the chassis, but Refuel filled each class to the one number measured for it (Sandbike 100, Buggy 240, Light Ornithopter 300, Medium Ornithopter 750, Sand Crawler 750, Transport Ornithopter 2500). A Mk6 buggy holds 300, so it read as full at 240 and was never topped up, and the Vehicles tab showed a tank of 240; a Mk5 Sandbike holds 140, not 100. The tank is now the fuel capacity of the vehicle's own chassis module in the game's module table (`CDT_BaseVehicleModules`, field `m_FuelCapacity`, the same table as the vehicle catalog): Sandbike Mk0 to Mk6 90, 100, 110, 120, 130, 140, 150; Buggy Mk0, Mk3 to Mk6 220, 240, 260, 280, 300; Light Ornithopter Mk0, Mk4 to Mk6 225, 300, 350, 400; Medium Ornithopter Mk0, Mk5, Mk6 450, 750, 850; Carrier Ornithopter Mk0, Mk6 1350, 2500; Sand Crawler Mk0, Mk6 450, 750. A vehicle with no chassis the catalog knows falls back to the old per-class number, and a vehicle neither knows (the Container Vehicle, which has no fuel) is left alone and reported, as before. The Fuel column shows the same tank and a percent that never exceeds 100, and the count beside the button uses it. A fuel amount already above the tank is never lowered. The full class-by-grade table, its source and the cross-checks (the catalog, the vehicle rows of played saves, none above its tank) are in `docs/design/2026-10-28-vehicle-fuel-tanks.md`. The text of the Fuel card's About this changed to say the tank follows the chassis. Tests: each of the 23 chassis rows filled from empty, the real vehicles of the #401 tests, a created Mk6 buggy (300), a Mk5 Sandbike (140), a Mk1 Sandbike (100), a chassis the catalog does not know, a vehicle nobody knows, and a second press writing nothing. Not checked in the game yet: refuel a Mk6 buggy from a low tank and read the gauge (100 %, value 300).
- **Fill blood sacks knows the capacity of every blood sack, not two** (#472): the sack `Bloodsack_02` (and every kind but two) was left alone and reported as "of an unknown kind". The capacities now come from the game's item data (the table of fillable items, field MaxAmount, one row per template), not from the largest amount seen in a save: `Bloodsack_01` 3000, `Bloodsack_02` 9000, `Bloodsack_03` 15000, `Bloodsack_T6` 24000, `Bloodsack_Unique_Durable_02` 13000, `_03` 16000, `_04` 19000, `_05` 22000 and `_06` 30000 (the first and the last were already known and are unchanged; all nine equal the capacities listed on the wiki). A blood sack template that the data does not list is still left alone and reported. The table, its source and the cross-check are in `docs/design/2026-10-28-blood-sack-capacity.md`. Tests: each of the nine fills to its capacity from an empty record, no record or a partly full sack, a full sack and a sack holding water are not rewritten, the `Bloodsack_02` sacks of the report fill to 9000, unlisted templates are skipped and reported. **Not in game yet** for the new templates.
- **Landsraad: the decree pool no longer calls SpecialVendorActive "enabled" while SpecialVendorActive_Vehicles is the decree in effect** (#448): the save holds five special vendor rows (SpecialVendorActive, _Vehicles, _Weapons, _Armor, _Utilities) and the editor listed them correctly, but its State column printed the save's `disabled` flag as enabled / disabled. That flag does not say what is in effect: in the saves read, the term's active decree (SpecialVendorActive_Vehicles) carries `disabled` = 1 and the base row carries 0. The pool now has an **In this term** column (Active, Elected, In rotation, read from the term and the rotation by decree id) and a **Pool flag (disabled)** column that shows the stored 0 or 1; the buttons are **Set flag to 0 / 1** and change that one row only, and a note under the table says what the flag is. Tests: the 11 real decree names, each special vendor variant on its own, and an edit of one row leaving the others unchanged.
- **Land claim editor: the two axis arrows are drawn at their size** (#457): the +x and +y labels and their arrowheads drew as two large solid blocks that hid cells (shown as blocks that hid cells). Cause: an SVG stroke with no stroke width is one unit, and the grid is drawn in cell units, so the 0.55 high labels and the 0.28 wide arrowheads were stroked a whole cell wide, the heads with long mitered spikes. The labels are now filled text with no stroke and the arrowheads have a thin stroke (0.03) and round joins; colours and positions are unchanged. Tests: `web-tests/claim-axes.test.mjs` (the stylesheet rules, and the labels inside the drawing, turned upright, at yaws 0, 90, 165 and 270).
- **Landsraad: the per-house Complete and Reopen no longer ask first** (#455): the two buttons at the end of each house row opened a question ("Complete this task?" / "Reset this task?"); like Complete chosen, Reopen chosen and Fill chosen they now act on the first press. The result line names the house and the faction, for example "Alexin completed for Harkonnen" or "Alexin reopened" (the house name is shown without the `DA_House` prefix), and the automatic backup taken before the write is the way back. The faction list behaves as before, and Fill faction goal on each row, which never asked, is unchanged. Tests: `web-tests/landsraad-complete.test.mjs`.
- **The low findings of the code review of 2026-10-27** (CR-3 to CR-11, #443). *Apply kit* is refused with a conflict (409) when the kit was changed, deleted and made again, or replaced by an import between the dry run and the apply: the dry run answers with `kitHash`, the apply sends it back, and the page says "The kit has changed since you were asked. Nothing was applied." (CR-3). The result of a blood-sack fill (Fill blood sacks, and Fill all liquids when it filled a sack) says again that the fill has not been checked in the game yet, as the action does not ask first (CR-4). The reason a control is off is now text that the control is described by, not only a tooltip, because a disabled button cannot take the keyboard focus: New kit, Import, Edit and Apply on the Care package tab, the specialization rows whose track number is not known, Bring to me and Duplicate on a stored vehicle, the Group marker in Research and the Reset buttons of the config page (CR-5). Set task progress refuses an amount above 1,000,000,000 (CR-6). A kit of the wrong kind sent to the kit routes by hand is answered in words (`the value of "items.quantity" is of the wrong kind`), not with Go type names (CR-7). Fill chosen on a Landsraad board with no term says there is no term, before it asks (CR-8). The two bounded-work tests fail after a minute of their own instead of hanging to the 12-minute package limit; the comment in `ci.yml` names that limit as the number to raise first (CR-9). Duplicate this vehicle copies only the character's own permission rank, not the ranks of other players, and its question says so, and that the serial number and the module records are copied as they are (CR-10). `scripts/roadmap-gen.sh` writes issue titles and reasons as plain text into `docs/roadmap.md` (markdown, HTML and web addresses are escaped) (CR-11). Tests for each: `kit_apply_route_test.go`, `landsraad_scales_test.go`, `bounded_work_test.go`, `vehicle_duplicate_test.go`, `roadmap_gen_test.go`, `web-tests/reviewlows.test.mjs` and `carepackage.test.mjs`.
- **`settings.json` (with the care package kits) is replaced durably and has a way back** (#442, code review 2026-10-27 CR-2): the file was written to a temp file and renamed over, but never flushed, so a crash or power loss shortly after could leave it empty or cut off; an empty file was read as "nothing to lose", and the next change overwrote it, taking the hand-built kits, the learned track numbers and the automatic-refill switches with it. Now the temp file is flushed to the disk before the rename (and the folder after it, where the system allows), the file as it was before each change is kept as `settings.json.bak` (one generation), and an empty, cut-off or otherwise unreadable file is **damaged**: the settings come from the backup copy when that is good, the Automatic refill card and the Care packages list say so (and the log does), and the next change keeps the damaged file as `settings.json.damaged-<time>` instead of writing over it. With no good backup the damage is reported, nothing is written over the file and a change is refused, as for any damaged file. A file that was removed on purpose does not come back from its old backup; temp files a crash left behind are swept by the next write. Tests: a flush before the rename and a folder sync after it; a simulated crash between the temp file and the rename (the old file whole, kits intact, the stale temp swept); an empty file and a truncated file with and without a backup; the damaged copy kept and never made the backup; one generation only; a failed flush writes nothing; the route answers with the notice and the kit survives.
- **The waiting automatic pass stops waiting when its switches are turned off** (#441, code review 2026-10-27 CR-1): the pass that waits for the game to close is retried every 3 seconds. If the master switch, or every job, was switched off while it waited, the retry still decoded the whole save and built a new working copy (with the save's lock held) at every tick, because `RunStartupTasks` returned at the switch check without clearing the waiting flag. The retry now looks at the switches first and, when nothing is switched on, ends the wait and drops the "queued" note without reading the file; `RunStartupTasks` clears the wait at both of its early returns. A pass with a job still switched on keeps waiting and runs once on the fresh file, as before. Tests: switches turned off while it waits (master switch, every job) end the wait and leave the save unread for the next ticks; the early returns clear the flag; a job left on keeps the wait.
- **Nothing writes the save while a game process exists, and the automatic pass waits for the game to close** (#419, priority p0; the part about edits made by hand was replaced by #449, see Changed): the editor's pass at launch wrote the save at the character-selection screen, shortly after the player left the world, while the game was still running. The write gate refused only during a single-player session read from the game's log (#65), and the log showed none at that moment, so the game being open "at the menu" let the write through; the game keeps its own copy of the rows in memory, and items that the write had changed could then not be moved. The gate now refuses whenever a Dune process exists, and when the process list cannot be read, whatever the log says (the log only chooses the sentence shown). Every write goes through one place (`installBlob`, reached from the automatic write and from restoring a backup, with the game check right before the rename), and a test lists every route to the save file so that a new one cannot skip the gate. The automatic pass at launch no longer prepares or writes anything while the game runs: it says "Automatic refill queued: the game is running; applied when it closes." and runs once, on the freshly read file, when the game has closed. Edits made by hand are queued in the same situation and written when the game closes. The banner no longer says that changes are written at once while the game is open. README ("How saving works") says the same. UAT: with the game open (at its menu, logged out to character selection, or in the world) open the editor with Automatic refill on: the page says the refill is queued, the file in `Saved/Cloud/PlayerClientStorage` keeps its modified time, and no new `-auto` backup appears; make an edit by hand and see it queued; close the game and see the refill and the queued edit written within a few seconds. Backups from before this change are untouched.
- **The character's pawn is the one the game links** (#33, register 2026-09-30): the editor found the pawn by guessing among the account's `PlayerCharacter` actors (the first one). It now follows `player_state.player_pawn_id`, the game's own link, and only a save without that link falls back to the account's lowest-id character. On every save checked the two are the same actor, so nothing changes there. Tests: `TestPlayerPawnFollowsTheGamesOwnLink`, `TestPlayerPawnFallsBackToTheAccountsCharacterWhenThereIsNoLink`.
- **Tabs and map layers say what is selected** (#180, register 2026-10-15 UX-08 and register 2026-09-30 F-21): the open tab and sub-tab carry `aria-current` and each map layer toggle carries `aria-pressed`, so a screen reader announces the state that was only a colour before. Test: `the selected tab and sub-tab say so to assistive technology, and a map layer toggle says whether it is on`.
- **A failed SQL statement is shown where its result goes** (#22, register 2026-10-07 UX-5): a refused or failing statement in the SQL console only raised an error message, and the result of the previous statement stayed on the page as if it answered the new one. The reason is now written in the result area as well, and the old result is gone. Test: `SQL console: a refused or failed statement shows its reason in the result area and clears the old result`.
- **The keyboard focus stays on the control you used** (#180, register 2026-10-15 UX-07): the page is drawn again after almost every action, which threw the focus to the top of the page, so a person using the keyboard lost their place after each tab, sub-tab or button. The editor now notes which control had the focus (by id, or by its data attributes and label) and puts the focus back on the same control in the new page, unless something else took it. Test: `the keyboard focus stays on the control that was used when the page is drawn again`.
- **The red Delete button is readable on the dark theme** (#180, register 2026-10-15 UX-09): its white label was 3.53:1 on the red fill; it now uses the same dark-on-light and light-on-dark label colour as the accent button (5.4:1 dark, 5.7:1 light). `TestFilledButtonLabelsReachFourPointFiveInBothThemes` checks the label of the accent and the red button against their fills in both themes.
- **Reload now no longer throws away a held queue** (#305, review finding A1): when the queued edits conflicted with the game's newer file and were held, "Reload now" read the file again, put nothing back, removed the journal and answered ok, so the held edits were lost without a word. It is refused now (code `queue_held`, "discard them first, then reload") and the queue and its journal stay as they were.
- **A refused export is shown, not saved as the table's file** (#22, register 2026-10-07 UX-6): the CSV and JSON buttons of Database > Tables saved whatever the server answered, so a refusal (a table that is not available, a bad name) was downloaded as `items.csv` holding `{"error": ...}`. The reason is now shown as an error message and nothing is downloaded. Test: `Database: an export the server refuses shows its error and downloads nothing`.
- **A virtual table named like one of SQLite's own no longer hides from the schema check** (#4, register 2026-10-07 S2/S4): a hand-made save holding a virtual table called `sqlite_...` was skipped by name and the save stayed writable. The check now skips nothing by name (SQLite's real internal tables have a root page and were never reported anyway); such a save opens read-only with the table named. Test: `TestFingerprintCatchesAVirtualTableNamedLikeAnInternalOne`.
- **Queued changes found at start-up are put back whole or held** (#305, review finding A5): the changes an earlier run left in the journal were put back one by one; the ones that applied were written within seconds, the ones that did not were only a notice that was gone when the editor closed, and when none applied the journal was deleted. They now go back the way the automatic write puts them back: all of them, or none. If one does not apply the whole queue is held, its journal is kept, nothing is written, and the page says which change conflicts and why, until the queue is discarded.
- The provenance note of the embedded game-table files names the game build they were read from (Steam build 20502675, last updated 2025-10-23, before both files were added) (#260).
- **Opening a save no longer deletes another editor's temp file** (#257, audit findings ARCH-5, DBA-12): the temp files a crashed run leaves next to the save were removed when the save was opened, before the editor took the one-editor-per-save lock, so a second editor started on the same file could delete the temp file of the first while it was writing. They are now removed after the lock is held, and a refused second editor removes nothing. The direction of the "fresh" flag of the game check (ARCH-4, fixed in #242: writes ask for the process list again, only the status poll may use the three-second answer) is pinned by a test.
- CI calls the organisation's shared security workflow by commit instead of `@main` (#178, CLD-6); `windows` and `ui` are required checks of `main`.
- **A change that depends on one that could not be put back is not put back either** (#251, audit findings DBA-1, ARCH-3, DBA-11): when Reload now or a restart puts the queued changes back one by one and one of them does not apply, a later change that works on a table it worked on with a value in common with it (the id of the row it was to add) is no longer run: it would have changed the game's own row with that id. It is listed as not put back, naming the change it depends on. The whole put-back now holds the editor's lock from the first change to the last, so nothing is made, discarded or read in between, and Reload now keeps the journal file until the changes that went back have replaced it. The start-up put-back waits for an automatic write like the other paths. The read checks of #272 are pinned with the editor's own Solari change (100 + 250 over the game's 5,100 is held, not written as 350).
- **The automatic refresh no longer races an edit** (#305, review finding A2): the page's refresh asked "is anything queued?" and then asked to read the file again, as two steps; an edit that landed in between was thrown away and the request answered ok. The check and the read now happen together under the save's locks (`DiscardIfClean`), and an edit that arrived keeps its place in the queue.
- **Solari follow the stack size, and a removal takes what it reports** (#258, audit findings DBA-6, QA-12, QA-11, DBA-8): adding Solari fills the backpack stacks up to the 50,000 a slot holds and makes more, as Give already did (900,100 in one stack is no longer written); removing takes from the last stack back and deletes a stack it empties, and removing more than the stacks hold is refused with what the backpack holds (it used to delete the first stack and report success). A new item takes its stats from the oldest item of its template in the save (the lowest id), so the same give writes the same stats every time. Tests added for the 500/501 boundary of a stack edit, a stack of another grade not being topped up, and the "not enough room" message.
- **The first backup of a session is kept** (#253, audit findings SEC-1, GRC-4, DBA-7, DBA-9, QA-3, SEC-2, ARCH-7): the pruning of the automatic backups never removes the oldest one, the first one this session took (the file as it was before the editor wrote anything) or the one just taken, counts and removes only files named the way the editor names them (and for this save), and orders them by the time in the name, not the file time. A burst's backup is reused only while it still holds the bytes it was taken with, and a backup taken of a file the game had just saved is refused instead of being reported as the restore point. A future-dated or foreign file in the folder does not stop a backup. The test of the retention can now fail when the newest or the first backup is deleted.
- **A change a reload dropped is reported until you have seen it** (#252, audit findings ARCH-1, DBA-4, ARCH-9, UX-30, UX-31, GRC-15): when Reload now or a restart cannot put a queued change back onto the game's newer file, the notice that lists it no longer disappears when the others are written (nor when the page reads the game's next save by itself); it stays until you press OK. A queue that was not kept (the journal is off) and cannot be put back says that it cannot be written and offers Discard queue, instead of promising to write it later. Messages the page builds by hand say "queued" only when the edit is queued, and "Teleport" no longer says it was queued when it was written. The queue is listed in plain words in the one banner (since #247); tests pin that.
- **A write that does not read back as written no longer overwrites the game's newer file** (#305, review finding A3): when the file after the rename was not byte for byte the edited save (for instance because the game saved right after it), the editor put the copy taken before the edits over it without looking and said "the previous file was restored" even when that had failed. It now looks at the file again, writes nothing, and says what it found: the replacement did not take effect (the loaded file is still there), or the file is neither the edited save nor the loaded one and was left as it is, with the copy taken before the write in the backups folder. The edits stay queued.
- A queued edit is no longer put back over what the game changed meanwhile (#251, audit finding E1): the journal keeps what each edit read (a hash of the rows every query returned) and asks the same queries of the newer file first. An edit worked out from rows the game has since changed (Solari plus 250, the next free slot, the next item id) is reported as a conflict, and the automatic write holds the queue instead of writing a stale total over the game's. Journals written before this have no reads and are put back as before.
- **Warning banners have their colour back** (#243): the round-2 skin used `--warn` and never defined it, so every banner (queued changes, cannot write, read-only, lost contact) and warning pill was drawn as plain text; both themes define it now, and a test fails if any `var(--x)` is undefined. The SQL write card, the item delete question and the config replace question no longer talk about a Save step that was removed; a test fails if they do again.
- **The write queue is safer** (#241): the automatic backup pruning removes only the backups an automatic write took (it deleted every older one, including the pre-editor copies, once there were 60); a backup is reused within a burst of edits only while the file is still the one the last write left (a file the game saved meanwhile gets its own); a journal that outlived its write is not replayed a second time (the edits were put back twice); when the game saved changes that conflict with the queued edits the whole queue is held and nothing is written (before, the edits after a conflicting one could act on the game's own rows), and the page says so with the conflicts and a Discard queue button; Discard, Reload now and Restore wait for an automatic write in progress; the single-player check read a three-second-old process answer at the write and spawned tasklist on every status poll (the flag was the wrong way round).
- **A failed read no longer leaves half a queue** (#305, review finding A4): when queued edits were put back onto the game's newer file and one of them conflicted, the file is read again to undo the ones that had applied; the put-back wrote the journal with only the applied edits first, so if that second read failed the queue was left as that part (in memory and on disk) and the next write would have put it into the game's file. The journal now keeps the whole queue until the outcome is known, and a failed read leaves the whole queue held (nothing is written while it is held); a failed first read no longer drops a held queue either.
- A number the editor cannot store (NaN, infinity, a fraction where a whole number is meant, a value above 2^53) is refused instead of being saved or wrapped to a different value; a specialization row is held to the XP and level a track can have; a view no longer comes back empty because one cell holds infinity (#168).
- **Text contrast is checked in both themes on every surface** (#306, review finding B2): the muted text on a warning or info banner was 3.84 to 4.18:1 in the dark theme and the light accent colour on the beige tiles 4.37:1. Banners now use a slightly lighter (dark theme) or darker (light theme) muted colour for their `.mut` text, and the light accent is a little darker (`#a44f07`; the labels on it are better still, 5.7:1). The style-sheet test now reads the colours from the CSS, in both themes, for the page, the panels and every tinted banner, instead of only the light theme's plain surfaces.
- Refills, Repair Gear, Add Specialization XP and Grant customizations plan and write in one step, so a double-click can no longer over-fill or add twice (#166). Repair Gear changes only the durability values it repairs and leaves the rest of an item's data exactly as the game wrote it (#167). Refill and the Power tab agree on which inventory holds a power device's fuel (#165); the scope of the one-click operations and the start-up refill is written down in `docs/design/2026-10-15-auto-refill-scope.md`.
- **Two quick clicks no longer run a bulk action** (#306, review finding B1): the buttons that act on every base ask first by turning into "Yes, ..." and run on the second press, but two presses a few milliseconds apart (a double click) counted as the answer. A second press less than 400 ms after the first, and the second half of a double click, are ignored and the button keeps asking; the question now lasts about 15 seconds instead of 8.
- Repair all to max takes a fraction of a second on very large bases instead of over a minute (#170). Vehicle repair never lowers a module's recorded maximum, and every vehicle durability rule is covered by a test (#171).
- Shrinking a land claim is refused if it would leave a cell cut off from the totem; Expand and Apply share one write path, and a new cell must connect back to the totem (#172).
- Database > Tables and the cell editor work on all 94 tables (#173). The 61 tables the game stores without a rowid failed with "no such column: rowid"; their rows are now addressed by primary key. The foreign-key gate also tells their orphans apart by primary key, so an edit that repairs one orphan and creates another is refused.
- The leftover routes for single building pieces and recovered vehicles are gone, and editing, deleting or giving an item only reaches your own inventories and your base's containers. The SQL write console locks the same tables as the Tables view (#169). Land-claim writes still accept any totem id (#194).
- **What a queued edit read is hashed with whole blobs** (#305, review finding A6): the hash of the rows an edit looked at covered only the first 24 bytes of a blob (the length of the text shown for it), so the game changing a later byte went unseen and the edit was put back over it. Blobs longer than 24 bytes are now hashed whole; shorter ones hash as before. A journal kept by an earlier version whose edit read a longer blob finds a different hash and is reported as not put back (the safe side) instead of being applied.
- A crafted save can no longer blank a view with a NULL building type or leak formulas through column names in a CSV export, a file over 256 MB is refused before it is read, error messages no longer carry folder names, and the account, Funcom and FLS ids on the Player Summary stay hidden until you press Show (#174).
- When the default port is busy the editor says so; two editors can no longer open the same save; the session cookie is per port and the editor answers only for its own address (#175).
- A save on a network share, a removable drive or outside the game's own folder needs a confirmation before it can be written (#176).
- **Stale text corrected** (#306, review finding B4): the box shown after an unexpected error said nothing was written "unless you pressed Save" (there is no Save button; changes are written as they are made, after a backup) and now says that; README calls the map tab Saved map and says the page guard is a session cookie, not a token on every call; the feature inventory says its list of tabr-tau routes is historical (it still names the removed review and commit routes); and the changelog no longer lists the accessibility entry twice or runs two entries together on one line.
- **Three edits read their data inside the edit now** (#305, review finding A7): Bring a vehicle (the player's position), Set the Landsraad term (the term id) and Refill water containers (the list of containers) read what they work from before the edit started, so a queued copy of the edit replayed those values as fixed numbers over whatever the game had saved since. They read through the edit's own query now, which is kept with it: put back onto a newer file where the player moved, a new term began or a container was added, the edit is held and reported instead of being written over it.
- Completing a Journey node whose children have rows but which has no row of its own now completes the node too, and children are matched exactly instead of by LIKE. Changing the automatic-refill switch and assigning a specialization track at the same moment no longer loses one of the changes; a damaged `settings.json` is reported on the refill card and left alone. The Live Map no longer adds a window mouseup listener every time it is drawn (#182).
- Clearer messages (#179): Admin repair starts at 100 and says why when it repairs nothing, the review pane names items and shows before-and-after values, config writes no longer say "not saved yet", a signed-out page and a failed view say what to do, and a refused edit in a box goes back to its old value. The refill buttons say they act on every base, and the start-up refill result stays on screen for 15 seconds (#165). (The review pane named here was removed in #238.)
- **A browser that blocks site storage no longer stops the page** (#306, review finding B5): the page read the last open tab from the browser's local storage when it loaded and wrote it when a tab was clicked, without guarding either; a browser or window that blocks storage (it throws on the first touch) left the page blank. Both go through a guarded reader and writer now, and the page starts on the Player tab when storage is unavailable.
- Layer 3 review of the audit fixes (#206): Shrink claim reads, checks and deletes in one step; the damaged-settings message names the file, not its folder; the single-editor lock retries briefly before reporting another editor; error clean-up no longer strips the separators of a root path; Database > Tables finds WITHOUT ROWID rows by their typed key; confirming a save location is accepted only while the guard is asking for it.
- **Keyboard and screen reader fixes, round 3** (#306, review items of group C): in a question box the scrollable list of changes and the SQL statement can be reached by Tab (so they can be scrolled with the keyboard), have a name, and are part of the keyboard trap; and the remove link of a player tag is named "Remove tag ...".
- **Faults answer 500 with a code, and a refused write is a 409** (#306, review items of group C): a write that failed on the disk side (the file could not be replaced, the new file could not be written, the written file did not read back as written, the backup did not verify) or that failed its own checks (integrity check, round trip) answered 400 with no code, as if the request were wrong. They answer 500 with `write_failed` or `integrity_failed`; the "game may be running" and "save is read-only" refusals answer 409 (they were 400) and keep their codes. The words of the messages are unchanged, and the page keys on the codes, not on the status.
- **A kept journal that cannot be used is reported, not ignored** (#305, review finding A8): the journal of queued changes is named from the lower-cased path of the save but was matched on the exact path, so on Windows a save opened under a different spelling of its path (case) had its kept changes ignored without a word; a journal that could not be read, or was written by a different version, was skipped the same way. The path is compared ignoring case on Windows, and any journal that is skipped is moved aside (`pending-....json.skipped`, not deleted, since the next change would write over it) and shown as a notice: the queued changes kept by an earlier run could not be put back, and why.
- The editor says at once when the game has saved since it opened (#160). The banner used to say "You can keep editing" during a single-player session, and the first sign that the game had rewritten game.db was a refused Save that dropped every edit. The status poll now reports a changed file (a size and time check, then a hash, so it stays cheap) and the banner offers Reload now; the single-player banner says to close the game or go to its menu before editing.
- Free text in an item box is no longer sent as an item id (#161). Typing "Spice" (not an item; the item is Spice Melange) put an item no game has in the backpack. The Give box, the give queue and the base container box now take only a catalog name, a catalog id or an id already in the save, and the server refuses anything else with a sentence that says what to do.
- **Config file edits are serialised, use a temp file of their own and keep their backups to 60** (#306, review items of group C): an edit of a game `.ini` read the file, changed a line and wrote it back with no lock, so two edits at once could drop one of them, and it wrote through one fixed temp name (`Game.ini.tmp`), which two edits at once shared, and which would have overwritten any other file of that name. Edits now take a lock, write through a temp file named for them, and two backups in the same millisecond no longer share a name. The backups folder no longer grows without limit: the newest 60 backups of each file are kept, and the oldest ever taken (the file as it was before the first edit); a file whose name starts the same is not counted.
- Repair Gear makes an item as good as new (#153). An item that was repaired at a workbench records a decayed maximum below its full one; the repair set its durability to 100 and left the worn-down maximum. It now sets full durability (the item's maximum when it records one, else 100) and restores that decayed maximum too, and only ever raises an item.
- Repair Vehicles no longer skips crafted modules, and makes a module as good as new (#151). A module that was never repaired at a workbench has no recorded maximum in the save, so the repair said "no recorded maximum" and did nothing for a crafted Sandbike. It now sets every module to its template's full durability, and a recorded maximum that repairs at a workbench wore down is restored to it as well. The full durability depends only on the vehicle family and the kind of part (the same at every tier, Mk1 to Mk6, and for the unique variants): an engine, PSU, wing or tread, storage, boost, launcher or scanner is 1000 on a Sandbike or Treadwheel, 1750 on a Buggy or Light Ornithopter, 2000 on an Assault Ornithopter, 2500 on a Sandcrawler and 3000 on a Transport Ornithopter; a chassis lasts twice that and a hull (cabin, cockpit, tail) one and a half times. The Dune Awakening database's item pages give these, and every saved module of those templates reached exactly those values. Scrap-tier (_0) modules and PolarCap generators are still left alone when they record no maximum, and the message now names those templates instead of only counting them.
- CI now also runs `go vet` and `go test` on a Windows runner (#85); before, Windows-only code was only cross-compiled, which let a Windows-only test failure (#82) through.
- **Four small page fixes** (#306, review items of group C): a cell of the Database view that was NULL goes back to NULL (not an empty text) when it is cleared; the sentence about putting an edited value back gives the whole old value up to 200 characters instead of the first 37 (a value too long to show says so); the status poll is not sent again while the last one is still waiting for an answer; and the address of an exported table is no longer discarded in the same instant the download is started.
- A build made on Windows no longer opens every real save read-only (#104). The editor compares the game's triggers and views with the built-in schema to refuse saves with hidden logic; the built-in schema file is CRLF on a Windows checkout while the game's own saves use LF, so the same trigger looked "modified" and all edits were refused ("This save is read-only. modified trigger actor_fgl_entities_cleanup_orphaned_entities"). Line endings are now ignored in the comparison and the schema file is pinned to LF. Linux and release builds were not affected.
- **An editor that holds no lock removes nothing** (#305, review finding A10): when the lock file next to the save could not be made for a reason other than another editor holding it, the editor went on without a lock but still removed the temp files next to the save and swept the decoded copies of earlier sessions, which another editor's write or session could be using. The sweeps now run only when the lock is really held; starting without one is unchanged.
- The Vehicles tab lists only your own vehicles (#92). A vehicle's owner is the owner-rank row of its permission record for your character (`actors.owner_account_id` is empty for every vehicle in real saves); the many grade-0 vehicles the game places in the world have no such record and are no longer listed, repaired by **Repair all vehicles** or movable with **Bring to me**. The tab says how many world vehicles are hidden. Recovered and backup vehicles are shown for your character only. A vehicle with no owner record counts as a world vehicle.
- Bases > Storage lists only the storage containers that belong to your base (#75, #74). It used to list every inventory of every non-player actor (most of them not storage): generator and turbine fuel slots, refineries and fabricators, vehicles, CHOAM terminals and the world's loot containers. Now a container is listed when it is a storage building type (Storage Container, Medium Storage Container, Chest, Small Storage Container) whose owner entity is your base's totem, as the console does. It shows your own name for the container when you set one, its type, and items out of its slot capacity, instead of the sum of stack sizes that read oddly ("Total units"). Refineries and fabricators are no longer in this list.
- Saving is blocked only while a single-player session is active, not whenever the game is open (#65). The game process stays up in multiplayer and at the menu, and only a single-player session holds the save. The editor reads the game's log (`Saved/Logs/DuneSandbox.log`): a local `...Survival_1?listen` map starts a session; a `?closed` or remote `ip:port` browse, `RequestExit` or `Log file closed` ends it; the game's final write lands about 3 s later, so Save also waits 15 s. Checked against real logs: every start pairs with an end. Failing closed is unchanged: if the game runs and the log cannot be read, Save is blocked and the banner says why. Config edits still refuse while any Dune process runs, because the game rewrites its configs on exit.
- **Backups are ordered by when they were taken** (#305, review finding A11): the automatic backups were named with the computer's local time and the pruning removed the ones whose names read oldest, so after a daylight-saving change or a new time zone (the local time runs backwards) the newest backups could be the ones removed. Backup names now carry the time in UTC (`game-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSSZ-NNNNNNNNN-auto.db`) with the nanoseconds padded, a backup is always later than every one already in the folder even if the clock was set back, and the pruning and the list of backups order by the instant in the name. Names made by earlier versions are still recognised.
- The running-game check matched one guessed process name that was never verified, so on a real machine it could say "not running" while the game was open and Save then failed late with "changed on disk" (#52). It now matches any process whose name starts with `Dune`, and the banner and refusals name what was found. If the check itself cannot run, Save is blocked and the banner says why, instead of assuming the game is closed. Verified on a real machine: the game runs as `DuneSandbox.exe`, `DuneSandbox_BE.exe` and `DuneSandbox-Win64-Shipping.exe`; the launcher (`GT.Launcher`) does not match, and leaving single-player does not end the game process.
- `tools/send-snapshot.ps1` parse error (`"$dir:"` read as a drive-qualified variable).
- **One slow moment no longer costs the whole journal** (#305, review finding A9): writing the journal of queued edits had no second attempt; if a virus scanner or indexer held the file for a moment the write failed, the journal that was already kept was deleted and journaling was turned off for the session. The write is now tried up to four times, and when it still cannot replace the old journal the old one stays on disk (it holds the edits up to the last write that worked) while the page says the later edits are not kept.
- Two comments that named another project are reworded (a CSS comment and the #236 entry of this changelog); nothing in the repository refers to anything outside it.
- **A save that could not be read back is no longer written twice** (#358, review R6 finding M1): after the file was replaced the editor read it back twice with no pause, and if a scanner or indexer still held the new file it answered "the edits stay queued" although the file already had them; the next automatic write then put the queue onto it again (a give of 500 became 1,000). The read-back is now tried up to 5 times over about 2 seconds; if it still cannot be read the answer says the save was written and could not be verified, the hash of what was written is kept (in the journal too), and the next attempt, a reload or a restart first checks whether the file is that one: if it is, those edits are done with and only edits made after the write are written. A test holds the new file exclusively and counts the rows.
- **A journal that could not be kept up to date is reported at the next start, not replayed** (#358, review R6 finding M2): when the journal could not be written (4 attempts) the old one stayed on disk, and a restart put it back as if it were the whole queue ("Applied 5" while edit 6 was lost); with the journal off the marker of the file being written was not kept either, so a stop between the replacement and the removal of the journal put the old edits onto a file that had them. The journal is now marked incomplete in a file next to it (`pending.json.incomplete`, which carries the marker of the write); a restart does not put it back, sets it aside as `pending.json.skipped`, and lists the edits it held and says that later ones may be missing. A journal that is set aside is no longer overwritten after 98 earlier ones (L7).
- **A give returns the error of a read that failed** (#39, register 2026-10-07 ARCH-6, D8 and D9): the used-slot list and the stats source of a give were read with their errors dropped, so a failed read looked like "no slot is used"; they return the error now. `SECURITY.md` says what stays by design: no free-space check ahead of a write (a write that cannot be made fails before the file is replaced and changes nothing) and a window of milliseconds between the last check and the rename (a game save inside it is caught by the read-back). The register counts of 2026-10-07 are now 56 fixed, 12 obsolete, 9 accepted risk and 7 open.
- **"Could not tell whether the game is running" no longer blocks writes by chance, and the check starts no program** (#366, #359): the editor found out whether the game was running by starting `tasklist.exe` about every ten seconds; when that start failed now and then ("exit status 1") the editor refused every write and showed a banner that **Write now** could not clear (it answered "Nothing was waiting."). The check now reads the system's process list in memory (the Windows process snapshot, same `dune*` name rule, case-insensitive), so no program is started, no console can flash and there is no exit status to fail. A check that fails is tried again three times, a quarter of a second apart, before it blocks anything; the status poll uses the answer of the last 15 seconds (it used 3) and keeps showing the last good answer for up to 30 seconds if one check fails, so one failure does not flip the banner; a failed answer is reused for only 2 seconds, so the banner goes away at the next poll once the check works, without a restart. A check that gates a write is still never answered from the cache and still fails closed (the edit waits) when it cannot be made after the retries, now with the reason. The banner for it has **Check again** next to **Write now**; both run the check at once and say the result ("the game is running: edits will queue", "the game is not running: edits are written at once", or "the check failed again: ...") instead of "Nothing was waiting." when nothing is queued. The check is the new route `POST /api/game/check`.
- **Clear this container confirms what really goes** (#358, review R6 finding L1): the number the person confirmed was the top-level rows only, the items inside a bag went without being counted, and a container that changed to other items of the same number was cleared all the same. Before the question the page now asks the editor (`GET /api/inventories/clear-preview?inventory_id=N&include_currency=true|false`) what the clear would remove right now: the items, the items inside bags (any depth), the currency stacks kept, and a token (a hash of the container, the currency flag and every id of both lists). The question names the items inside bags and the number to type is the total; the request carries `count`, `nested` and `ids_hash`, and the clear refuses with 409 `conflict`, changing nothing, when the count, the nested count or the set of ids is not the one confirmed (`ids_hash` and `nested` are required, 422 `bad_argument` otherwise). The answer of a clear has `nested`.
- **Reset level and XP no longer leaves fewer total skill points than are spent** (#358, review R6 finding L2): the points the levels gave are taken back from the unspent points only (no more than are unspent), and the total goes down by the same number, so total minus unspent, the points the skills hold, is unchanged. A character with every point spent keeps them all. The question says so, the answer has `pointsTaken` and `pointsKept`, and the toast says how many points could not be taken back because they are spent in skills; reset the skills first, with the box ticked, to take back all of them.
